CHAPTER I

On Tríśrā's Austerlities

རྟོགས ཆུན:

सूत सूत महाभाग मिहं ते वचानामुना ।

न तृपता: सभे वयं पीला ह्रीपालन कृतं शुभम् ॥ १ ॥

The Rsis (of the Naimisa forest) addressed Sūta (fondly): O highly Fortunate One! Your nectar-like words are very sweet. We are not satiated with what you have described to us as the auspicious saying of Dvaipāyana Vyāsa.

पुनस्त्वां ग्रुधिष्ठाक्ष: कवां पौराणिकांी शुभाम् ।

वैदेश कथितां रचां प्रसिद्धां पपपातिणीम् ॥ 2 ॥

O Sūta! We desire to ask you again to narrate to us the auspicious saying of this Purāna, beautiful, famous, and sin-destroying and authorised by the holy Vedas.

द्वगासुर इति ख्यातो वीर्यांस्त्वयुतासकः ।

स कर्थ नित्रतः संख्ये वासवेन महात्मना ॥ 3 ॥

Viśvakarmā had a son, named Vītrāśara, who was very well known, and very powerful. How was it that he had been slain by the high-souled Indra? Viśvakarmā was a powerful Brāhmin and belonged to the gods' party; his son was stronger.

त्वहा वै सुर्यक्षीयस्त्युजो बलवतः ।

शक्रेन प्रातिज: कस्मादुर्घडधोपनिर्भाजतः ॥ 4 ॥

How was it that he had been killed by Indra! The Devas are born of the Sativa qualities; men are born from the Räjasic qualities; and all the birds, etc., are born of the Tāmasic qualities.

देवा: सत्त्वगुणोत्थमा मानुपा राजसा: स्मृता: ।

तिर्वेछस्तामसा: प्रोक्ता: पुराणाममवादिषिथ: ॥ ५ ॥

वितोपो3त्र महान्यमाति नूनं शतमघेन ह ।

छलेने बलवान्यूत्र: श्रकेन ग्विनिपातितः ॥ 6 ॥

This is the opinion of the Pandits, versed in the Purāṇas and Āgamas. But in this act of slaying Vītrāsura, a great contradiction arises; for the powerful Vītra was killed merely under a pretext by Indra, the performer of the hundred sacrifices, and endowed with Satva qualities.

विष्यु: प्रेरित्यित तत्र स तु सचल्यर: परः ।

प्रविष्ट: पविमध्ये स छन्दान भपात्यभू: ॥ ७ ॥

साँदि विधाय स होयं मन्त्रितोजरी महावलः ।

हरिथ्यां सत्यमुतुर्ज उतकेनेन ग्रातितः ॥ ८ ॥

And Indra was prompted to do so by Viṣṇu, the head of those who possess Satīva qualities; while Viṣṇu himself entered in disguise into the thunderbolt so that he could kill Vṛtra. The powerful Vṛtra entered into a treaty and kept himself peaceful when Indra and Viṣṇu violated truth and treacherously killed him by Jalaphena (the watery foams).

कृतमिच्छेण हरिणा किमोत्स्तृत साहस्म् ।

महान्तोर्षि च मोहेन बँकिताः पापबुद्धः ॥ ९ ॥

O Sūta! The great wonder is this: That Indra and Viṣṇu turned out so bold as to forsake the truth. This, then, is (therefore very clear that the

high souled persons become deluded and act sinfully.

अन्यायवर्तीनोउत्थर्ष भवन्ति सुरास्तमाः ।

सदाचारेण युक्ति देया: शिष्टाच्यमानता: ॥ 10 ॥

एवं विशिष्टधर्मेण शिष्टलं कीईरां पुनः ।

The Heads of the Devas act very wrongly; they are reckoned as polite simply because they observe the mere outward forms of good conduct as approved by the Śāstras. How can the mere observance is outward forms constitute politeness?

हत्वा वृयं तु विश्वस्तं श्रकेना छचना पुनः ॥ १ १ ॥

प्राणं पापफलं नो वा ब्रह्मतासमुद्वय् ।

किं च त्वया पुग प्रोक्ते चुवासुप्यध: कृतः ॥ १ २ ॥

श्रीद्वया श्री तत्त्वाथि चितं मोहयतीह नः ।

Had Indra, who killed in disguise Vṛtra relying on his words, to suffer any punishment for the sin that he incurred in killing a Brāhmaṇa? It was told by you before that Vṛtra had been slain by the Devi Bhagavati; but the general belief is that Indra killed him. Our minds are puzzled on this point. (So clear our doubts on this point.)

सूत उवाच

श्रणन्तु मुनयो वृर्तं चूत्रासुर्याध्रवम् ॥ 13 ॥

यथेनेण च सम्पानं दुःखं हृत्यासमुद्वम् ।

Sūta said: O Munis! Hear the incident of the killing of Vṛtrāsura and the punishment that Indra had to suffer due to his sin of Brahmahatya (killing a Brāhmin.)

एवमेव पुरा पृष्टो व्यासः सत्यवतीस्तुः ॥ १४ ॥

पारतीक्षितेन राज्ञपि स व्यदाह् च तद्बुद्वे ।

This question was asked by the King Parikshit and replied by Vyāsa, the son of Satyavatī. I will tell you what Vyāsa had told before.

जनभेजय उवाच

कर्ते चूरासुरः पूर्व हृते मघवाता मुने ॥ 15 ॥

सहायं विद्युभासादा छवना सात्चिकेन ह ।

कंव च देया निहतो दैत्योउसी केन हेतुना ॥ 16 ॥

कवेमोकवधो द्वाभ्यां कृतः स्यामुपिनुर्गाव ।

Janamejaya asked: "O Best of Munis! How was

it that in former days Indra, endowed with the Sattva qualities, killed Virṭrasura, with the aid of Viṣṇu? And how and why was it that he was killed again by the Goddess Bhagavati? O Lord of Munis! How could one body be killed by the two; our curiosity has been excited to hear the truth,

तदेतच्छोतुमिच्छामि परं कौतृत्ति हि मे ॥ 17 ॥

महतां चरितं भृणवक्को विरज्येत मानवः ।

कथयामादोषचं त्वं वृत्रासुर्वधाशितम् ॥ 18 ॥

What man is there that does not like to hear any more of the glorious deeds of the high-souled persons! Kindly narrate to us the slaying of Vṛtra by the Devi Bhagavatī.

व्यास उवाच

धन्योप्रसि राजसत्व बुद्धिदीयुंगी

जाता पुराणश्रवणोस्तिसादारा।

पीतावप्रभुतं देवेवास्त्रुं सर्वेशा

पाने विद्याण: प्रभवन्ति वै पुनः ॥ 19 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O King! You are blessed, since your taste to hear the events of Purāna has grown so much; the Devas even get their thirst for drinking nectar; but when quenched, they do not like to drink any more.

दिने दिने ते उधिकाभक्तिभाव:

कथासु राजनमूनीयकांति: ।।

श्रोता यदैकप्रवण: श्यानीत

वक्ता तदा प्रतिमाना ब्रवीति ॥ 20 ॥

O King! Your name and fame are widely spread. Your Bhakti (devotion) to the Purānas is growing more and more daily. A speaker gets very much delighted when his audience hears him with undivided attention.

युद्धे पुरा वासवद्व्रयोर्ध्रेदे प्रसिद्धे च तथा पुराणे ।

दुखं सुरेन्द्रता तथैव लब्धं हृता सिंगुं ल्यामुपायमेवे ॥ 21 ॥

O Lord of the earth! The fight between Vītra and Vāsava that occurred in days of yore is famous in the passages of the Vedas and the Purānas; as well as the suffering that Indra had to encounter as his punishment when he had killed the innocent son of Viśvakarmā.

Book VI Chapter I

चित्र किमत्र नृपते हरिवजर्भूट्वां

चछ्छ्वाता विनिर्तस्विशिरोउव चुनः ।

मायावलेन मुनवोर्धि विमोहिताते

चकूश्र तिंच्यमिन्शं किल पापभीता: ॥ 22 ॥

O King! The Munis, who fear sin very much, commit yet blameable act under Māyā; then what wonder is there that Viṣṇu, and Indra would kill Triśirā and Vṛtra merely under a plea.

विष्यु: सदैव कपटेन जायान

दैत्यानस्तातमूर्तिरपि मोहमवाय कामम् ।

कोन्योरित तां भगवर्ती मनसाउपि जेतुं

शक्कः समस्तजनमोहकर्ती भगवानीम् ॥ 23 ॥

When Viṣṇu, the incarnate of Sattva qualities, gets deluded by Māyā and kills deceitfully the Daityas always, then how can you expect any other man to conquer even mentally the Mahā Māyā Bhavānī, Who deludes all the beings!

मत्थादिवीयिषु सहस्वयुगेषु सदा:

साक्षान्वल्पि यथा विनीयोजितोउ ।

नारायणो नरसखो भगवाननंतः कार्य

कोति विहिताविहितं कदाचित् ॥ 24 ॥

O King! It is undet the compulsion of this Māyā that the Bhagavān, the Infinite, the friend of Nara, Nārāyaṇa, takes incarnations in thousands and thousands of Yugas in this Saṃsāra as Fish, etc., and does deeds sometimes lawful and sometimes unlawful.

देई धर्न गृदामिदं स्वजना मदीयं

पुत्रा: कलत्रमिति मोहमुपेय स्वर्वः ।

पुर्णयं कोत्यथ च पापचयं क्रौत्ति

मायागृपीरित्वलैर्विकेक्लीकृता यत् 125 ॥

The Devas and men, being confounded by his Māyā, become upset and disordered and say “that this body, wealth, house, sons, wife and relatives are all mine” and being thus deluded sometimes do virtuous and sometimes sinful deeds.

न जातु मोहं शपितुं नरः क्षमः

क्रश्रुद्विद्धपून परावराधित् ।

विमोहितल्लिचित्भेवेर मूलतो

वर्गीकृतात्या जगातीले भृगम् ॥ 26 ॥

O King! There is not even one, on the surface of this earth, though he may be well versed in finding out cause and effect, the knowledge of the high and low, that can be free from this Great Delusion; he is from the very beginning tied up by the three Guñas of this Māyā and thus remains under Her control.

अथ तौ मायया विद्यावासो मोहिती भुगम् ।

जठनुगुर्भचाना वूत्रं स्वार्थसाधनतत्यति ॥ 27 ॥

This explains that Viṣṇu and Indra both were deluded by Māyā and engaged in fulfilling their own selfish ends. They killed Vṛtārsāra under a pretext.

तदई संग्रवक्षया वृत्तानमवनीपते ।

कारणं पूर्वैतस्य चूत्रसावयोर्पिधः ॥ 28 ॥

त्वषा प्रजापतिहार्तीद्विशेषो महातपाः ।

देवानां कार्यकर्ता च निपुणो ब्राह्मणप्रियः ॥ 29 ॥

O King! Hear! I am now describing to you the cause of enmity between Indra and Vṛtra. Viśvakarma, the Prajāpati, was the great architect of the Gods, he was skilled, he was superior amongst the gods, a great ascetic and endeared by the Brāhmins.

स पुरं वे हैं विज्ञारसमि-ट्रेशेपातिकलामुक्त ।

विश्वस्तोवि विद्यालां तान्मा रूपेण मोहनम् ॥ 30 ॥

विधि: स वदनै: श्रेष्ठ्यंरोत मनोहैः ।

विभर्भिश्रानि कार्याणि मुखैः समकोनमुपिनः ॥ 31 ॥

वेदान्केन सेधोक्षितु सुरा वैकेन सोप्रवित् ।

तृतीयेन दिणुः सर्वां युगपच्छ निरीक्षते ॥ 32 ॥

He had enmity with Indra; and out of this enmity he created a son, very beautiful and named him Triśirasa Visvārūpa. That son had three faces very beautiful and lovely. Visvārūpa performed three different functions with his three different faces; with one, he used to study the Vedas, with the second he used to drink nectar (wine), and with the third he used to see simultaneously all the directions.

त्रिशिरा भोगमूलुच्य तपश्ठेक सुद्धकम् ।

तपस्वी स मुदुद्वितो धार्यमेव समाश्रितः ॥ ३३ ॥

Śrimaddevaibhāgavatam

Triśirā renounced the pleasures of the world and began to practise a hard tapasyā; he became a great ascetic, gentle, restrained in his passions and entirely devoted to his religion.

पश्रानिर्नाधनं काले पादपात्र निवेशनम् ।

जलमध्ये निवासं च इेमन्ते शिशिरे तथा ॥ 34 ॥

He practised Pañcāgni Sādhana in the summer season, tying his feet upwards on the branch of a tree with his head downwards; he remained in dew in the cold season, and, under water in the winter season.

निराहरो जितालामाउसी तत्कसर्वपरिष्रहः ।

तपृधारा मेघावी दुकर्न मन्दबुद्धिधि: ॥ 35 ॥

Thus he abstained from food and conquered his self and, forsaking all the worldly connections, practised a very hard tapasyā; very difficult, indeed, for those who are of dull intellects.

तं च दृष्ट्वा तपस्तं त्रैवदाम शवीधिति: ।

विवाद्यमानात्र शक्रोयं मायमधृति ॥ 36 ॥

दृष्ट्वा तस्य तपो वीर्यं सत्यं चाभिततेजसः ।

चिन्ता च महती प्राप हार्षिणं पाकाशासन: ॥ 37 ॥

विद्यांयान्तिक्षिरा मायमं शातिययति ।

नोपेध्यः सर्वेधा शत्रुवैर्यमानवले गुधैः ॥ 38 ॥

Indra became very sad and dispirited to see him practise such a Tapasyā and thought of the means so that he might not acquire his Indraship. The Pakaśāsaṇa Indra remained always very anxious to see the energetic penance practised by that ascetic of unbounded glory and his steady attachment towards it.

तस्मायुपायः कतव्यस्तपोनायाय साम्यन्तम् ।

कामस्तु तपतां श्रुः कामात्रश्यति वै तपः ॥ 39 ॥

He thought thus: “This Trīśārā is becoming stronger day by day with his penace, so he will kill me. The wise never look an enemy with indifference whose strength daily becomes greater and greater.”

तवैवाहा प्रकर्तेयं भोगास्तको भवेहाय ।

इति संचित्व मनसा बुद्धमान्वलमदैः ॥ ४० ॥

It is now my urgent duty to invent means on how to baffle his Tapasyā and he at last settled

that lust is the great enemy of asceticism; the practice of devout austerities is destroyed completely by lust; so I must try this very day that some how the Muni becomes attached to worldly lusts and enjoyments.

आज्ञायलोउपसरस्लाव्युप्रज्ञतोभने ।

उर्वेर्गी पेनकां रभां घृतावी च तिलोत्तमाण ॥ 41 ॥

The intelligent Indra, thinking thus, called the Apsarās Urvaśī, Menakā, Rambhā, Ghṛtāci, and Tilottamā and others proud of their beauties so that they might seduce Triśirā, the son of Viśvakarmā.

समाहुधावीच्छक्रतातादा रूपभार्थितः ।

प्रियं कुर्वयं मे सर्वा: कार्येडा समुपस्थिते ॥ 42 ॥

“O Apsarās! I have now got a very grave task to fulfil; all of you help me in this respect. A great enemy of mine, difficult to conquer is practising penance with his self-controlled.

यतो मेघा महाकछुस्तपत्थित दुर्जेः ।

कार्य कुक्त नष्ट्धप्रे पतोभयत माधिरम् ॥ 43 ॥

Start at once and with your dress suited to various amorous gestures and try hard to seduce him. Be all well with you; seduce him and remove the fever of my heart.

श्रृगार्वेर्वेर्विविद्योर्गैर्वैद्समुद्वेः ।

प्रतोभयत भई वः शमवधं ज्वरं मम ॥ 44 ॥

O Apsarās! What more shall I say, I am restless since I have heard of his strength in performing such hard austerities.

असत्योड़ महापभातस्य जाला तपोकलम् ।

बलवानासनं मेउदा प्रतीर्थ्यावितर्धितः ॥ 45 ॥

O Weak Ones! That powerful ascetic may acquire my place and thus dispossess me; this fear has possessed me.

भयं मे समुपायातं ब्रिणं नाशयतत्वालतः ।

उपकुर्बन्तु सहिताः कार्येउघ समुपस्थिते ॥ 46 ॥

Therefore destroy my fear as quickly as possible. This is the task now given to you; get united and do this good to me."

तच्छुत्वा वचनं नार्यं कच्छूत प्रणाताः पुनः ।

मा घयं कुक्र देवेश यतिव्यामः प्रतोधेने ॥ 47 ॥

Book VI Chapter I

The Apsarās, hearing him, bowed down and said: "O Lord of the Devas! Do not be afraid! We will try our best to seduce him.

यथा न स्वाद्धयं तस्मातथा कार्यं महातुँते ।

नृसर्गतीतिद्वारैंडु मुनेतस्व प्रतोभने ॥ 48 ॥

O highly Lustrous One! For the enticing away of the Muni, we will do all the things, dancing, music, and other amorous gestures and practices, that will discard your fear.

कटाईसूढ़भेदैंड मोहर्यिता मुनि विभो ।

तोतुएं वरामसाकं करिष्यामो नियन्तितम् ॥ ४ १ ॥

O King of the Gods! We will unsettle the mind of the Muni by our side glances and passionate gestures and postures, delude and tie him and then bring him under our control.

व्यास उवाच

इत्याभाव्य हरिं नायों ययुङ्धिरासोंउतिकम् ।

कुर्ट-न्यो विविधाभ्यावा-कामगाखोचितानपि ॥ ५० ॥

Vyāsa said: “O King! Thus saying, the Apsarās went to Tríśirā and began to exhibit various amorous gestures and postures as stated in the Kāma Śāstra.

गायन्यस्तालभेदैस्ता नृत्यन्त्य: पुरतो मुनेः ।

तं प्रलोभायतुं घकुन्निभाबान् वर्गाना ॥ ५१ ॥

They began to sing sometimes, sometimes to dance in tune with musical measures before the Muni. In short, they practised various amorous gestures to entice him away.

नारायस्य तपोराशिर्यानां विद्धवन् ।

इन्द्रियाणि वरेगे कृता मुकान्यविधि: स्थितः ॥ 52 ॥

But that asecting, blazing with the fire of Tapas, did not notice even the Asparās' various attempts; rather he kept all his senses under control and remained like a deaf, dumb, and blind man.

दिनानि कातिचितस्यार्थविस्त्यामो चे ।

कृत्वीय गाननृत्यादि प्रपड़ानतिमोहादून । ५३ ॥

In that lovely hermitage of the Muni, the Apsarās sang and danced ravishingly and remained a few day there.

न चचाल यदा कामं ध्यानाच्च त्रिसारा मुनि: ।

परावृष्य तदा देव्यः पुनः शकमुपस्थिततः ॥ ५४ ॥

कृताझलिपुटा: सर्वा देवराजमधावुब्न् ।

श्रान्त दीन भघवस्ता त्विबर्णद्वना भृणम् ॥ ५५ ॥

But when they saw that the Muni Trísirā did not swerve a bit from his meditative posture they returned tired, distressed to Indra and all, very fearful, began to address Indra with folded hands: "O King! We tried our best and we could not in any way make the Muni unsteady, very hard to surmount."

देवदेव महागर जयल्छ प्रमः कृतः ।

न स शक्यो दुर्गाधर्षे धियांच्वालियुं विभो ॥ ५६ ॥

उयायोऊनः प्रकर्तेल्यः सर्वेथा पाकशासन ।

नास्याके बल्मेतांस्मितापसे विजितेनिद्रये ॥ ५७ ॥

O Pākaśāsana! Please invent other means; we could not make the self-controlled Muni move away an inch from his position; it is our good luck that that high-souled Muni, an incarnate of blazing fire has not cursed us!"

दिष्ठा वयं न शपता: स्म यदनेन महात्मना ।

भुनीना वहितृल्येन तपसा डोतितेन हि ॥ ५८ ॥

Then dismissing the Apsarās, the evil-minded and dull Indra began to devise means, though totally unlawful on how to kill that good Muni.

विसृज्यावास: शक्रश्रितन्यास मन्दधी: ।

तस्वीव च कवीपायं पापवद्धिरसाध्यमन्ति ॥ 59 ॥

विसृज्य लोकतलज्ञां से तथा पापचयं भूगाम् ।

चकार पापबुद्धि तु तद्धाय महीतये ॥ 60 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वैधीपागतते महापुराणे षटचक्ये

प्रभातोर्षया: 11 7 11

O King! That Indra abandoned all shame, and fear of sin and ultimately came to a highly blameable and sinful conclusion how to kill him.

Here ends the First Chapter of the Sixth Book on Trísirā's austerities in Śrimaddevivbhāgavatam the

Maḥa Purāṇam, of 18,000 verses by

Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER II

On the Birth of Vṛtrāsura

यमस उवाच

अथ स लोभमुपोत्थ सुराधिध:

सामधिग्य गजानसनंस्थितः।

क्रिशिरसं प्रति दृष्यमितस्तदा

मुनियपश्यदीयपराक्रम् ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: The extremely covetous Indra, then, mounted on his Airāvata elephant and determined to kill the Muni.

तमधिवीक्ष्य दृझासनसंस्थित

जितारीरं सुममाधिवर्गं गतम् ।

रविविभावसुसत्रिभभोजसा

सुर्पतिः परमापदमभ्यगत् ॥ 2 ॥

He went to him and saw him immersed in deep Samādhi, firmly seated in his posture and with his speech controlled. At that time, a halo of light emanated from his body and he looked like a second Sun and a blazing fire. Indra became very sad and dejected when he saw that.

कथमसौ विविनटनुमहो

मया मुनिरपायमति किल सम्मतः।

रिपुर्यं सुसमिद्धिनोपोकल:

कथपुषेय इहासनकामुकः॥ 3 ॥

Indra then thought within himself thus: "Oh! Can I slay this Muni, free from any vicious inclinations, and endowed with the power of Tapas, blazing like a fire! This is quite against the Dharma.

इति विविन्य पविं परमायुधं

प्रतिमुभोच मुनि नासि स्थितम् ।

श्रशिवाकास्त्रिभ्यमाणुगं

श्रिशरां सुरासंघपति: स्वयम् ॥ 4 ॥

But, Alas! He wants to usurp my position; how can I, then, neglected such an enemy? Thus cogitating, Indra hurled at the Muni his swift-going, infallible thunderbolt, the Muni remaining engaged in his penance and shining like the Sun and Moon.

तदधिच्यातहतः स ध्यातले किल

पयात ममार च तापसः ।

शिखारिणः शिखारं कुलिशादितं

निपिततं धृति चाडूतद्रौनम् ॥ 5 ॥

तं निहल्य मुदामप

सुरेश्युकुरुंस मुनयसु सर्यस्तः ।

हा हातेते भूगमातर्नित्यतः

किं कृतं शतमद्वतं पाधिता ॥ ६।।

विनापराधं तपसां निश्रहतः श्रवीपिति: पायमितैतृतात्मा ।

फलं कितायां तरसा कृतस्य प्रामोतु पाणे हननोढ़ल्य ॥ ७ ॥

The ascetic, struck thus, fell on the ground and died, like a mountain peak struck by thunder falling on the ground and presenting a wonderful sight. Indra became very glad when he killed the Muni; but the other Munis then cried aloud: "Oh! We are killed! Alas! What a crime has Indra committed today! Oh! The vicious Indra has killed today this jewel amongst the Munis without any offence! Let, then, this sinner reap the fruits of his sinful act without any delay."

तं निहन्य ततसा सुरारजो

निजांगाम निजमन्दिरमाणु ।

स हतोउप विराज महाना

जीवमान इव तेजतां निधि: ॥ 8 ॥

तं दृष्ट्या पातितं धूमौ जीवन्तमवि बुझरा ।

चिन्तामापातिखांगाः किं वा जीवेदयं पुनः ॥ 9 ॥

Indra, then, went back soon to his own abode; on the other hand, the high-souled Muni, though killed, looked as it were, living by the lustre of his own body. Indra, then, seeing him lying like a living man thought that the Muni might get alive and so became very sad.

विषयप्र यमसाउजीव तक्षाणं पुतः स्थितम् ।

मप्वा बीक्ष्य तं प्राह स्क्वास्यैसुर्णं चः ॥ १० ॥

तक्षिजिधि शिरास्यस्य कुरुब बचनं मम् ।

मा जीवतु महातेजा भाति जीविज्व सत्यम् ॥ ११ ॥

इत्याकपर्थे बवस्तस्य तक्षोवाच विगहाय् ।

While he was thus arguing in his mind, he saw before him a wood cutter named Takşa and began to speak to him for his own selfish ends thus “O

Book VI Chapter II

Artisan! Cut all the heads of this Muni and keep my word; this highly lustrous Muni is looking as if it were alive; therefore; if you sever his heads, he cannot be alive." Takṣa then cursed him and spoke thus.

तहीवाच

महास्कन्यो भृणां भालित परपूर्ण तपोक्षिति ॥ 12 ॥

ततो नाहं करीष्यामि कार्यमेताद्वारहितम् ।

त्वया वे निर्वितं कर्म कृती सद्विवर्गितम् ॥ 13 ॥

उहं विधेमि पापाद्रे पुनर्यैव च मारणे ।

भृतोर्धं सुनिसरस्येव शिरसः कृतनेन किम् ॥ 14 ॥

भयं किं तेत्र संजारं पाकशासन कंध्यताम् ।

"O King of the Devas! The neck of this Muni is very hard and therefore cannot be severed; my axe is not at all fit for this work. Specially I cannot do such a blameable act." You have done a very heinous crime, quite against the law of the good persons; I fear sin; I will not be able to cut the heads of a dead man. This Muni is lying dead; what use is there in severing his head again? O Pakaśāsana! The killer of the demon Pāka! Why do you fear in this?"

इन्र उवाच

सजीव इव देहोउम्याभाति विश्यादुक्ति: ॥ 15 ॥

तस्माधिपेमि मा जीवेमुनि: शत्रुयं मम।

Indra said: “O Artisan! This Muni is my dire enemy. Life seems to be still lingering in his body; his body is still lustrous, I fear if the Muni be alive again!”

तक्षोवाच

नात्र किं जपसे विद्धनूरेणानेन कर्मणा ॥ १६ ॥

ऋषिपुत्रिमं दृत्वा बढ़ादृत्याभयं न किम् ।

Taksā told: “Do you not feel shame in doing this heinous crime, when you know everything? Do you not fear God for the crime of killing a Brāhmin?”

इन्व उवाच

प्रायश्रितं कर्त्रियाम पशुलापक्षयाय वै ॥ १७ ॥

शुत्रु सर्वथा बध्यघृछनेतापि महामते ।

Indra said: “I will make Prāyaścitta (penance) afterwards for the washing away of my sins; but my duty at present is to kill my enemy. O Fortunate One! The wise men, clever in polity, say that enemies must be killed by any excuse whatsoever.”

तक्षेताव

त्वं लोभापितृतः पार्यं क्रतिभि मवयवितृह ।। १ १ ॥

तं चिनाउंह कर्थ पायं करोति चद् त विधो ।

Taksa then replied: "O Maghavan! You are doing this sinful deed out of your avarice; but, O Lord! I have no cause whatsoever; how then without any cause, can I engage myself in such a vicious act?"

मखेषु खतु भागं ते कारिथामि सदैव हि ॥ 19 ॥

शिर: पशोस्तु ते भागं चेउ दास्यति मानवाः ।

शृल्केनानेन तिद्धि त्वं शिरायस्य कृक प्रियम् ॥ 20 ॥

Indra said: “O Takṣan! I will allot a share to you wherever there will be a sacrifice. The human beings will invariably offer to you the head of the animal killed at any sacrifice. Now cut his head according to this rule.

व्यास उवाच

एतच्छुत्वा महेन्द्रस्य वचनस्थां पुद्यान्वितः ।

कुठारेण शिरास्यस्य चकर्तं सुचुकेन हि ॥ 21 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O King! That Takṣa became very glad when he heard thus from Indra and struck off the heads of the Muni with his very strong axe.

छित्रानि जीण श्रिंधिणि पिततानि यदा भृति ।

तेभ्यस्तु पक्षिणः क्षिशुं विधिनेषुः सहसृशः ॥ 22 ॥

O powerful King! When the three heads, thus severed, fell to the ground, thousands and thousands of birds came out of those heads in quick succession.

कलर्विकासिततिरयस्तबैव च कर्मिंजला: ।

पृथक्युष्विनिषेन्तुमुंखतस्तसा तदा ॥ 23 ॥

The three groups of birds Kalavinikas, Tittiris and Kapiñjalas come out very rapidly from the three heads in due succession.

येन वेदात्यापीते स्म योमं च पिवतो तथा ।

तस्माद्वक्रतिकलोयेनुः सर्व एव कपिंजपताः ॥ 24 ॥

येन सर्वा दिशः कामं पिबपित्रव निरीक्षते ।

तस्मातु तितिरास्तव नि:सुतासितमपतेजसः ॥ 2 5 ॥

The Kapīnjala birds came out of that mouth that used to chant the Vedas and used to drink Soma; the Tittri birds came out of that mouth that used to see all the quarters as if it drank them; and the Kalavinika birds came out of that face that used to drink one.

यत्सुरारं तु तद्वक्तं तस्मातुं चटकाः किल।

विनिषेन्तृस्थिगिरस एवं ते विहाग नृप ॥ 26 ॥

Indra became very glad to see the birds thus coming out of his mouths and went back at once to his Heavens.

एवं विनिःसुतान्यूहा तेभ्यः शक्रसद्णडज्ञान् ।

मुमोद मसता राजझगाम विविदं पुनः ॥ 27 ॥

O King! No sooner Indra went back, than Taksa came back to his own house and felt himself very pleased to receive his share of sacrificial things.

गते शके तु तहापि स्यगृहे तस्या यये ।

यज्ञभां परं लब्धया मुदायम गमीते ॥ 28 ॥

On returning to his home, Indra thought that he had done his duty in slaying his powerful enemy.

इन्हो5थ स्वगृहं गत्या हत्वा शनुं महावलम् ।

मेने कृताधीयमानां ब्रहाहत्यामित्यन्ति ॥ 29 ॥

It did not pass in his mind that he had committed the Brahmatahyā sin (i. e., that he had killed a Brāhmin.

तं श्रुत्वा नितहां त्वहा पूरं परमधार्मिकम् ।

चुकोपातीव मनसा वचनं चेदमब्रबीत् ॥ 30 ॥

अनागसं मुनि वयमात्पूरं नितहावामः ।

तस्मातृत्वादियथामि तद्धार्थ सुतं पुनः ॥ 31 ॥

When Viśvakarmā heard that his virtuous son had been killed, he became very angry (in his mind) and said that as Indra had killed his qualified son engaged in asceticism without any offence, he would create another son to kill Indra.

सुगा: परभुंगु मे वीर्य तपसघ्र बलं तथा ।

जानातु सर्वे पाणात्या सकृतार्थ फलं मृत्ति ॥ 32 ॥

इलुक्न्वास्त्रिनं जुहावाय मनैराधवर्षणोदिति: ।

पुरम्योत्पादनाथीय त्वहा मोधेसमाकुक: ॥ 33 ॥

Let the Devas see his strength and power of

Tapasyā and let Indra, too, reap the far-reaching effects of his own Karma. Thus saying, Viśvakarmā, distressed with anger, offered oblations in the sacrificial Fire, reciting Mantram from the Atharvan Vedas, with the objects of producing a son.

कृते ह्रोमेउर्धात्रं तु सन्दीपाच्य विभावसोः ।

प्रादुर्बंभृव तसा पुरुष: पाचकोपम: ॥ 34 ॥

When Homa was per formed for eight nights consecutively, a man came out of that burning fire, as if he was the Incarnate of Fire itself.

तं दूरद्वाउं सुतं तद्धा तेजोबतस्पमवित्तम् ।

वेगात्यक्रतिटं वहैर्दीयमानमिवानलम् ॥ 35 ॥

उवाच वचनं तद्धा सुतं वीक्ष्य पुरः स्थितम् ।

इउत्रागो विवर्धेय प्रतापातपसे मा ॥ 36 ॥

Seeing the lustrous son before him, come out of the fire and endowed with power and energy, Viśvakarmā said “O Indra’s enemy! Grow by my power of asceticism.”

इत्युक्ति चवने त्वहा मोधप्रज्यलितेन च ।

सोउर्वर्तित दिंल सहज्वा वैश्वानरसमघुति: ॥ 37 ॥

When Viśvakarmā spoke these words, burning with anger, that brilliant fiery son began to grow, towering high above the Heavens.

जातः स पर्वताकारः कालमनुसम्मः स्वादः ।

किं कंरापीनि तं प्राह पितरं परमातुम् ॥ 38 ॥

कुक मे नामकं नाथ कार्य कथय सुठत ।

चितातुरोरिस कमानवं वृहि मे गोकाकारणम् ॥ 39 ॥

Within a moment that man looked a second God of Death and appeared like a mountain and shone like the God Himself. Then he spoke to his own father Vísvakarmā, who was very distressed “O Father! Put my name. Pray, what use can I be to you? Why do you look so aggrieved and anxious; please explain to me all the causes.

नाशयाम्यद्ध ते गोकामिति मे बतमाहितम् ।

तेने जातेन किं भूय: पिता भवति दुःवितः ॥ 4 0 ॥

I make a firm vow to-day that I will remove the cause of your sorrow. Father! Of what avail is that son to his father when he is not able to remove his sorrows!"

Book VI Chapter III

पिवामी सागरं सहाङ्गणीयामि धराधरान् ।

उर्धंतं वारायमध्य तर्पिं तिम्पतेजसम् ॥ 41 ॥

हन्मीन्द्र ससुरं सहो ययं वा देवलान्तम् ।

क्षिपामी सागरे सर्वान्समुवाट्य च मेदिनीम् ॥ 42 ॥

O Father! Shall I drink the ocean or crumble the mountain to dust or shall I obstruct the passage of the rising Sun or shall I kill Indra, Yama, or the other host of Devas or shall I root out the earth and throw it with all beings into the ocean?"

इत्याकर्णं वचस्तस्य त्वध पुरस्य पेणतम् ।

प्रत्युवाचातिमुदितस्तं सुतं पर्वतोपमम् ॥ 43 ॥

कृषिनात्यानुपानुया समाधठकौंस पुरक।

तस्माद्वुरं इति खलातं तत नाम भयिष्यति ॥ 44 ॥

O King! Hearing thus the sweet words of his son, Viśvakarmā gladly told his mountain like son "O my Son! You are now capable to save me from troubles (Vṛjna) hence you are named Vṛtra."

धाता तत महाभाग्र सिंगतरा नाम तापस: ।

त्रीण तस्य च शीर्षिणि ह्रामवन्दीर्विति च ।। ४५ ॥

वेदवेद्यान्त्वज्ञः सर्वविद्याविशारत: ।

संस्थिततपसि प्राविश्लोकीविमयप्रदे ॥ 46 ॥

गलेगु तृ हतः सोउढा वज्ञातेन सांगतम् ।

विनासप्रायं सहसा छिज्ञानि मस्तकानि च ॥ 47 ॥

O highly Fortunate One! Your brother, named Triśirā, was a great ascetic; his three faces were all very strong. He was thoroughly conversant with the Vedas and the Vedangas and well versed in all the other knowledges. He remained always engaged in practising asceticism, surprising to the three worlds. Indra killed my qualified son with his thunderbolt; that wicked soul severed the three heads without any offence.

तस्मात्वं पुरुषव्याप्र जहि शकं कृतासम् ।

ब्रह्मल्यापृतं पार्थ निश्चयं दुमाँति शकम् ॥ 48 ॥

Therefore, O Best of beings! Kill that vicious, shameless, doceful, wicked Indra, guilty of the sin Brahmatahyā.

इत्युक्चा च तदा त्वषा पुरशोकसमाकुलः ।

आयुधानि च दिव्यानि चकार विवधानि च ॥ 49 ॥

O King! Thus saying, Viśvakarmā very much confounded with the breavement of his son, created various divine weapons.

ददावमें सहसाक्षधाय प्रवलानि च ।

खद्गणुलमदाणिक्तोमरमुखानि वै ॥ 50 ॥

शगई धनुस्ताव बाण पत्रिं पडिणं तथा ।

चक्र दिव्यं सहसारं सुदर्गतरमग्रभम् ॥ 51 ॥

तृणीरी चाक्षयी दिव्यी कवचं चातिसुन्दम् ।

रधं मेघप्रतीकाशं चूदं भारसहं जवम् ॥ 52 ॥

He prepared weapons specially suited to kill Indra, the best axes, tridents, clubs, Saktis, Tomaras and bows made of horns and arrows, Parighas, Pattisās, divine discus like the Sudarśana Cakra, two divine inexhaustible arrow cases with arrows, nice Kavaca, very substantial air-like swift-going chariot looking like a cloud and capable to carry great loads; all these he created and gave over to his son.

युद्धोपकरणं सर्वे कृत्वा पुत्रय पार्थिव ।

दत्वासोड़ी प्रेरयामास त्यहा क्रोधसमित्वतः ॥ 53 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणी भच्छक्ये

दितीयोउधाय: 112 11

O King! Viśvakarma, the best of architects, excited by anger, made ready all the equipments necessary for war and gave them to his son Vītrāsura and sent him to kill Indra.

Here ends the Second Chapter of the Sixth Book on the birth of Vītrāsura in the Mahā Purānam

śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by

Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book VI Chapter III

CHAPTER III

On the Deva Defeat and on Vṛtrā's Tapasyā

ध्यास उवाच

कृतपरिस्त्ययनो चूजो ब्राह्मणैर्वेदारगरैः ।

निर्जगाम रथाठको हन्तु शर्मे महावल: ॥ १ । ॥

तदैव राक्षसाः कृगरा: पुरा देवपराधित्रिताः ।

समाजमूँश सेवार्थ वृत्रे ज्ञाला महावलम् ॥ 2 ॥

इन्द्रतास्तु तं दूर्व युद्धाय तु समागतम् ।

वेगादाग्य वृत्तान्ते शगशमुस्तस्य चौधितम् ॥ 3 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Having the Svastyayana

ceremony (a performance of rite to secure welfare or avert calamity) performed by the Brāhmaṇas versed in the Vedas, the powerful Vṛtra mounted on his chariot and started to kill Indra, the King of the Gods. The Dānavas that were previously defeated by the Devas now knowing Vṛtrāsura to be powerful, came up to him to serve his cause. The messengers of Indra, when they saw him ready for battle, hurriedly came to Indra and informed him all about his doings and other matters connected with it.

व्यास उवाच

स्वामिड्छीघमिड्याति चुत्रो नाम रिपुस्तव ।

बलावान्यंदने रूक्षत्स्वघु। चोल्यातिड़: किल ॥ 4 ॥

The messengers said: “O Lord! Vísvākarmā, having been very much grieved his son being slain, got very angry and by Abhicāra process (an incantation with a design to injure or magic spells or charms used for a malevolent purpose) has created a son in order to kill you.

अभिचारो नागार्थ तर क्रोधाचितेन वै ।

पुरवाताधितिजेत दुःसहो राक्षसैयुतेः ॥ 5 ॥

That indomitable Vṛtāsura is now your powerful enemy; mounting on his chariot he is coming here to fight with you, surrounded by other Asuras.

यत्नं कुम महाभाग शीघ्रमायाति साम्यतम् ।

मेकमन्दसङ्ग्राणी घोराष्ठप्रतिहाकण: ॥ 6 ॥

O highly Fortunate One! This enemy of yours is high as the mountain Meru; he is now coming hurriedly to you, making a terrible noise; guard yourself carefully."

तृतीयम्बन्तरे तत्र भीता देद्वाणा घृष्णम् ।

आगतावोषु: सुर्पतिे शुर्पवन्ते दूतभाषितम् ॥ 7 ॥

O King! While Indra was hearing the messengers, the Devas came there panic-stricken and terrified and said:

गणा कछुः

मथवन्द्रपितिमचाति भवन्ति श्रिद्धगालये ।

बहूनि भयशंसीनि पक्षिणां विस्तानि च ॥ 8 ॥

The Gāṇas said: O Lord of the Suras! Ominous signs are being seen in the houses of the Gods; the birds are making sounds, very inauspicious and foreboding a great calamity.

काकगूगास्तथा शेयनाः केकाढा दाफणा: खणा: ।

रूदन्ति विकृतः शब्दैरूकारैर्षवंनोरपि ॥ 9 ॥

Crows, vultures, herons, falcons, and other ugly inauspicious birds are crying and making hoarse sounds on the tops of houses.

चीचीकृधीति निर्वादातुळ्वित विहणा भुगम् ।

वाहानां च नेत्रेषो जलधारा: पतंत्यध: ॥ 10 ॥

Other birds are making incessantly harsh sounds like chichi kochy. The carriers of the several Devas are weeping and shedding tears always.

श्रूयतेजितमहाऊढ्वो कट्टितां निशगसु च ।

गक्षसीनां महाभाग भवनोपरि दाहणः ॥ 1 1 ॥

O highly Fortunate One! On the tops of houses are heard very loud and very dreadful sounds of the crying Rāksasīsī at dead of night.

प्रपतिति ध्वजास्तूर्ण विना वातेन मान्द ।

प्रभवतिन महोतात दिवि धूभ्यन्तरिक्षतः ॥ 1 2 ॥

O Giver of honour! The flags on the chariots are falling to the ground without any trace of wind. Thus ominous signs are being visible on earth and in the air.

कृष्णाम्बरधारा नार्यँ घमतिन च गृहे गृहे ।

यान्तु यान्तु गृहातपुणं कुर्सेचो विकृतानना: ॥ 13 ॥

O King of the Devas! The ugly faced women, wearing black clothes, are roaming from house to house and always repeating “Leave the house, and go away at once.”

राजी स्वत्पेषु कानानां सुतानां निजमन्दिरे ।

केशोल्छूनित राक्यसो भीवयन्यो भुणतुगा: ॥ 14 ॥

The Deva women while sleeping in their own temples are seeing in their dreams that terrible Rāksasīs, coming to them are cutting away their hair on their heads and are frightening them.

एर्वस्थितानि देवेशे भूक्रीणलाकायरस्तथा ।

गोमायवो रुदौतिम्न निशायां घबनांगणे ॥ १ ५ ॥

O Indra of the Devas! The inauspicious signs

Book VI Chapter III

like these and earthquakes and the falling of the meteors are taking place. The jackals come in the courtyard of houses at night and yell horrible heartrending sounds.

सराटानां च जालानि प्रभवन्ति गृहे गृहे ।

अज्ञप्रसूगरणादीनि दुर्विमितानि सर्वश: ॥ 16 ॥

Lizards are moving always in the rooms and the several limbs of our bodies are shaking and thus making very inauspicious signs.

व्यास उवाच

इति तेषां वचः शुत्वा चिन्ताभाग सुरोश्रः ।

बृहस्यति समाहुय पघछ च मनोगतम् ॥ 17 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing their words, Indra became very anxious and called Brūspathi, the Deva Guru, and asked him:

इन्न उवाच

ब्रह्मीकुमुत योगीण निमिन्तीन भवन्ति वे ।

वाताश्र दाराणा वाँन प्रपतैलुकता: खतः ॥ 18 ॥

Indra spoke: O Brāhmaṇa! Very inauspicious signs are being made visible; dreadful winds are blowing and stars are falling from the skies; what are all these?

सर्वेज्ञसि महाभाग समर्पे बिटनाशने ।

बुद्धिमाज्ञास्ततचज्ञो देवतानां गुरुस्तबा ॥ १९ ॥

O Intelligent One! You are very wise and versed in the Śāstras and the Guru of the Devas; you are omniscient and know very well how to remedy the evils.

कुरु शान्ति विधानज्ञ शत्रुळ्यविधधायिनीम् ।

यथा मे न भवेदुवुःखं तथा कार्यं विधीयतान् ॥ 20 ॥

Therefore perform the rites by which enemies can be killed; do such as our miseries be all averted.

बृह्म्यतिकावा

किं कारीम सहसाक्ष त्यवाउढ दुकृतं कृतम् ।

अनागसं मुनि हृता किं फलं समुपार्जित् ॥ 21 ॥

Bhaspati said: “O Thousand eyed! What shall I do? You have committed shortly a heinous crime; you killed that innocent Muni and so you have earned a very bad Kārmic effect.”

अत्युगं पुण्यपायानां फलं भवति सत्वम् ।

विद्यार्थ खतु कतल्वं कार्य तद्धृतिमिष्छत ॥ 22 ॥

Very violent sins and good deeds produce their effects very quickly. It is, therefore, highly incumbent on those that desire for their own welfare, to take up any work with great discretion.

परोतपातनं कर्म न कर्तव्यं कदाचन ।

न सुखं विदंते प्राणी परपीडापरायण: ॥ 23 ॥

It is never advisable to do any action that leads to the tormenting of others. Never do they find happiness who give pain to others.

मोहाल्लोभाद्बहावल्या कृता शक्र तव्याद्धपुना ।

तस्य पापस्य सहसा फलनेतनुपागतम् ॥ 24 ॥

O Indra! You have committed Brahmahatya, under the influence of greed and delusion; now suddenly has appeared the fruit of that act.

अवध्य: सर्वैद्यानां जातोउरी घुवसंज्ञकः ।

हंतुं त्वां स समायाति दानवैबहुभिवर्धतः ॥ 25 ॥

आयुधानि च सर्वाणि चजतुल्यानि सायल ।

त्वष्टा दत्तानि दिव्यानि गृहीता समुपचित्यतः ॥ 26 ॥

समागच्छति तुर्धौर्ष त्याहकः प्रतपावान् ।

देवेन्द्र प्रत्यं कुर्वेगाय मनुष्यंविष्यति ॥ 27 ॥

O King of the Suras! This Vītra Asura is born invulnerable to all the Devas. That powerful indomitable Asura chief is now coming, mounted on a chariot, to kill you, surrounded by the other Dānavas and taking with him the Viśvakarmāmade divine arms and weapons equal to thunderbolt. He is coming like a second Kāla, as it were to destroy the whole Universe. There is none in this Triloka, capable to kill him; and his death will not also take place.

कोलाहलसदा जातसदा बुवती वाक्यतो ।

गन्थवाँ: क्रिस्ता यक्षा मुनयक्षा तपोधानः ॥ 28 ॥

While Brhaşpati was thus speaking, a great tumultuous uproar rose at once. The Gandharvas, Kinnaras, Yakṣas, Munis and other Immortals began to fly away from their quarters.

सदानीय विद्यार्थवामाः सर्वे पलायिताः ।

तददूध्या महाद्यर्थ्यं शक्रश्रितापरायण: ॥ 29 ॥

Śrimadvevibhāgavatam

आज्ञाप्यमास तदा सेनेचोगाय संवेकान् ।

आनयक्षे वसुसुझनाशित्री च दिवाकरम् ॥ 30 ॥

पूर्णं च भर्ग वार्तु कुवेरं वरणं यमम् ।

विनानेषु समाकृष्ठ सायुभतः । 31 ॥

Indra seeing the Devas flying away became very anxious and gave orders at once that all subservient to him must be ready at once for battle; they must go and call the Vasus, the Rudras, the twin Aśvīns, the Ādītas, Pūsā, Bhaga, Vāyu, Kubera, Varūṇa, Yama and the other Devas to come there at once. The enemy is well nigh; so let all the Devas come on their Vimānas quickly there."

समागळन्तु तसा शुज्याती समाप्तम ।

इत्याज्य भुस्तितः समाक्रहा गजोतमम् ॥ 32 ॥

ब्रह्मस्तितं पुरीधाय निर्गतो निजमन्दितात् ।

तथैव विश्वश: सर्वे रखं रखं महामासिकता: ॥ 33 ॥

Thus ordering, Indra mounted on the Airāvata elephant and taking the Sura Guru in front started from his own temple. The other Devas mounted on their respective carriers and, firmly resolved to fight, started with all their arms and weapons.

युद्धाय कृतसंकल्पा निविद्युः शाखापाय: । यूत्रोध दानवैयुक्तः सम्प्राप्तो मानसोत्तम् ॥ 34 ॥

On the other hand, Vīrtāsura, surrounded by the Demons, came up to the beautiful mountain, adorned with trees, on the north side of the Mānasarovara Lake.

पर्वतं देहतावासं रयं पादपशोभित् ।

इन्द्रोपयाग्य संग्रामं चकार मानसोत्ते ॥ 35 ॥

Indra, too, came there with Brhaṣpati in front and attended by all the other Devas to that mountain, north of the Mānasa Lake and began to fight.

पर्वते देहतायुक्तो वाचस्मितपुर: सरः ।

तत्राभूद्राकर्ण युद्धे वृत्रवासवयोस्ता ॥ 36 ॥

A dreadful fight, then, ensued between Vṛtra and Indra with clubs, swords. Parighas, Pāśas, arrows, Šaktis, Parśus and other weapons.

गदासिपर्विः: पारुषैबिणीः शफित्मर्षधि: । मानुषेण प्रमाणेन संग्रामः शरादं शतम् ॥ 37 ॥

The terrible fight lasted for full one hundred human years, terrifying to the self-controlled Rsis and all the human beings.

बधुप दयदो नृगामुर्षीणं भावितात्मान् ।

वक्तानः प्रधारं भननस्ततो वायुगणः किल ॥ 38 ॥

Varuna first turned his back; then Vāyu, then Yama, the Sun and Moon and then Indra fled from the battlefield.

ततो विभावसुः शक्रः सर्वे ते निरांगा शणत् ।

पलायनपरान्दूर्वा देवानिंद्रयुगोगमान् ॥ 39 ॥

वृत्रोप्र चितरं प्रागादाशमस्यं मुदाद्वित्यम् ।

प्रणाय प्राष्त ल्यहारा चितृः कार्य मया कृतम् ॥ 40 ॥

देवा चिनिर्जिताः सर्वे संद्रा: समरसंश्विताः ।

विद्रुतासे गताः स्थानं यथा सिंहान्युग गताः ॥ 41 ॥

Seeing Indra and the other Devas flying away, Vṛtrāsura came to the hermitage and there bowed down to his father who looked very glad; and he said: "O Father! I have carried out your orders; Indra and all the other Devas are defeated in the battle; as elephants and deer fly away seeing a lion, so the Devas all fled away to their respective abodes.

इन्रः पदातिरामन्यायो3नीत गजोतमः ।

ऐरातकौउं भगवन्यूषण श्रिदोतमम् ॥ 42 ॥

I have taken the Airāvata, the best of elephants, away from Indra who fled away on foot. O Bhagavan! I have brought the elephant here. Kindly accept it.

न हताते मवा तसाद्युक्त भीतागरणम् ।

आज्ञापय पुनस्तत किं कंतीय तवैषितम् ॥ 43 ॥

O Father! It is not advisable to kill a man who is terrified, therefore I did not kill them. Now kindly order anything else that I may fulfil your desires.

निर्जर निर्भातः सर्वे भयभीताः क्षमातुाः ।

इंडोउधैरावतं लव्क्ता भयभीतः पलायितः ॥ 44 ॥

All the Devas fled away from the battle-field, very much tired and terrified; and what more to say than this that Indra, too, fled, leaving his elephant on the field."

Book VI Chapter III

व्यास उवाच

इति पुरवचः शुत्वा ल्यहा प्राह मुतावितः ।

पुरवानच्छ जातो5सि सफलं मम जीवितम् ॥ 45 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Visvākarmaī became very glad to hear his son's words and said: "To-day I can rightly say that I have got my son and that my life is successful."

त्वरात्रं पातितः पुर्र गतो मे मानसो जरः ।

निश्रलं से मनो जातं दूध्या दीर्थ तवाद्धुतम् ॥ 46 ॥

“O son! To-day you have sanctified me; my cares and worries are abated; my mind is also calm to see your wonderful prowess.

शृणु वक्ष्यामहं पुरा हिते तेउद निशामय ।

तपः कुरु महाभाग सावधानः स्थिरासनः ॥ 47 ॥

O Child! Now hear attentively what I say. O highly intelligent One! Now carefully sit in your steady posture (Sthirāsana) and practise Tapasyā.

विश्वासो वेर कलैकः कंभावित्याकासना: ।

शत्रुसे छलकर्ताउसिन नानाभेदिविशारादः ॥ 48 ॥

Never trust anybody; Indra is now your enemy, ever ready to find your faults and clever in sowing dissensions between you and your well-wishers.

तपसा प्रायते लक्ष्मीतपसा राज्यमुतमम् ।

तपसा बलवृष्टि: स्यासंग्रोम विजयसन्ता ॥ 49 ॥

O Son! Tapasyā is not an ordinary thing; Lakṣmī (prosperity) is obtained thereby; excellent kingdoms, increase of vigour, and victories in battles are obtained.

आराध्य दृष्टिणं देखे लब्ध्वा वरमनुतमम् ।

जहिं ग्रहं दुनारं ब्रहहत्यासमावृतम् ॥ 50 ॥

Therefore worship Hiranyagarbha and get excellent boons from him; then kill this vicious Indra, guilty of the sin Brahmahatyā.

साधान: स्थिती भूत्वा दात्तारं घत्र शंक्रम।

वात्रिजतं स वरं देद्यातस्तुण्ट्धतुरान्तः ॥ 51 ॥

Worship the auspicious Creator calmly and carefully. The four-faced Brahmā then will be pleased and grant you your desired boon.

तोषायिता विश्योर्नि ब्रह्णायामितीजासम् ।

अविनाशिल्यमासाठ जहि शकं कृतानगसम् ॥ 52 ॥

First please the Creator of indomitable prowess, from whose womb has sprung all this Universe, and get, then, immortality from Him. Then kill that guilty Indra, my enemy.

वैरं मनसि मे पुरा वर्तैत सुतधातजम् ।

न शान्तिमनुगच्छानि न स्वयासि सुखतेह ॥ 53 ॥

तापसो मे हत: पुजो निराग: पापन्य नय: ।

न विद्यामि सुखं घुर तं मामुद्धर दुःवितम् ॥ 54 ॥

O Son! My feeling of enmity due to the killing of my son reigns always in my mind; I cannot go to sleep peacefully nor do I get peace in any way. The vicious Indra killed my son; O Vṛtra! What more shall I say to you; I am merged in the ocean of sorrows; save me."

व्यास उवाच

तदाकर्णवं चितुर्वावस्यं वृत्रः क्रोयुत्नस्तदा ।

अज्ञामादाय च चितुर्जीगम तपसे मुदा ॥ 55 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus hearing his father's words, Vṛtṣāśura became inflamed with anger and, getting his permission, set out gladly to practise Tapasyā.

नस्यामदनमासाठ युण्यां देवधूनीं शुभाम् ।

सात्वा कुणासं कृता संसिंखतुं चित्यसानः ॥ 56 ॥

He then went to the Gandhamādāna mountain and performed his bath in the holy and auspicious river Mandākini, and, preparing a Sthirāsana, took his seat in the Kuśā grass, to practise the tapasyā.

त्वक्तात्रं वारिणां च योगाध्यापसपतयः ।

ध्यार्विश्वभुर्ज चित्र सेपोर्विष्टः स्थितयसे ॥ 57 ॥

He left off gradually taking his food, then subsisted on water only and remained engaged in Yoga; and, seated in Sthirāsana, meditated incessantly on Prajāpati, the Creator of this Universe.

मथवा तं तपस्वन्तं जात्या चितानुतेः छभुत् ।

गन्धर्वानीत्रयमास विदार्थं पाकगासनः ॥ 58 ॥

यक्षांश प्रवगान्सर्पणिक्रजरानमितीजसः ।

विद्याधरान्यसरो देवदूताननैकश:॥ 59 ॥

उपायासे: कृता: सम्यक्तपोद्धनाय माधिथि: ।

न चचाल तते ध्यानाच्छु: परमतापस: ॥ 60 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पश्छलक्ने तृतीयोअध्याय: ॥ 3 । ॥

Indra, on the other hand, knowing Vītrāsura engaged in tapasyā, became very anxious and sent to him Gandharvas, Yakṣas, Pannagas, Kinnaras, Vidyādharas, Apsarās and other Deva messengers, all of unbounded vigour to create obstacles in his austerities. These Gandharvas and other Devas Yonis, expert in exercising magical spells, tried

many ways and means and various gestures and postures to create disturbances in his penance; but that great ascetic Vītra, the son of Viśvakarmā, did not swerve a bit from his meditative state.

Here ends the Third Chapter of the Sixth Book on the defeat of the Deva army and on Vītra's tapasyā in the

Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣī Veda Vyāsa.

[Unreadable]

Šrimaddeviḥbhāgavatam

CHAPTER IV

On the Defeat of the Devas by Vrtra

व्यास उवाच

निर्नतासे परावृतास्त्रीविध्नकारः सुरा: ।

निराणा: कार्यसंसिद्धो तं दृष्ट्वा दृढ्चतेस्मा ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Suras that wanted to create hindrance in Vītra's tapasyā, seeing him firmly resolved, became disappointed in the fulfilment of their objects and returned to their own abodes."

जाते वर्षेगते पूर्णं बझा लोकपितामहः ।

तत्राजगाम तरसा हंसाकछ्धतुर्मुंखः ॥ 2 ॥

आगत्य तमुवाचैदं लक्षुः पुरु सुखी भव ।

स्वतन्त ध्यानं वं हूई द्वतायि तत वधीऊन्तम ॥ 3 ॥

Thus full one hundred years passed away. The four-faced Brahmā, the Gandsire of the Lokas, came there mounted on his carrier the Swan, and said: "O Vītra! Be happy; now quit your meditation and ask boon; I will grant you the boon that you choose.

तमसा तेऊढा तुओस्मि तां दूढ्वा चातिकर्हितम् ।

वंरं वरय घरं ते मनोशितर्षितं तव ॥ 4 ॥

O Child! Your body has become very lean and thin through your penance. I am now very pleased to see your this very hard tapasyā. Welfare be to you. Now ask the boon that you desire."

व्यास उवाच

चूरस्तदार्तिचित्रशर्ती पुरातो निशाय

वाचे सुधासमसरां जारंदेककर्तुः।

संत्यव्य योगकलनां सहसोदितिछत्

संज्ञातहर्षनयान्थुकलाकलाप: 11 5 || 1

पाटी प्रणाय शिरासा प्रणायाधिधातुः

बढ़ाजिल: पुत एव समाससाद।

प्रोबाच तं सुवरूत तस्पा प्रपर्थ

प्रेणासतिरान्द्वसिरा विवनेन नमः || 6 ||

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus the clearly distinct nectar like sweet words of the Creator Brahmā, Vṛtha shed tears of joy and suddenly stood up. And going to him, bowed down gladly before His feet, and, with folded hands, spoke to Him, Who is desirous to grant him boons, in a tremulous voice:

प्राप्त मया सकलदेवपर्द प्रभोडा

यहर्भैनं तव सुकुलैभगभू जातम् ।

वाज्छाजसि नाव मनसि प्रवणे दुरापा

तां प्रवबीयि कमलासन वैसि भावम् ॥ 7 ॥

“O Lord! To-day I have been fortunate to see Thee who art generally seen with great difficulty; and I have acquired thus the posts of all the Devas; O Lotus-seated One! I have got an insatiable desire burning within me. Thou art omniscient, Thou knowest everything; still I am speaking out my mind.

भृत्युंघ मा भवतु मे किल

लोहाकाशुणकार्डरिंशनिचर्यपरेशु शकैः ।

खूँद्र प्रयातु मम वीर्यमैताव

युढे यस्माद्वासी सवलैनरपैजेयः ॥ 8 ॥

O Lord! Grant that my death does not occur with iron, wood, dry or wet substances or with bamboos or any other weapons and let my strength and valour be increased very much in the battle;

Book VI Chapter IV

for, then, I will be unconquerable by all the Devas with all their armies.

व्यास उवाच

इन्यं सप्पार्थितो ब्रह्मा तमाह प्रहसप्रिव ।

ज्ञिष्ठ गच्छ भर्ते ते वाक्तितं सफलं सदा ॥ 9 ॥

न शुक्रेन पर नाईरँग न पायणेने दाहणा ।

भविष्यिति च ते मृत्युरिति सर्व ब्वीयमहूम् ॥ 10 ॥

ज्ञित दत्वा वरं बह्वा जगमा घुवनं परम् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus prayed for, Brahmā said to him smiling: "O Child! get up; I grant that your desired boon will always be fulfilled; now go to your own place Your death wo'nt occur with dry or wet substances or with stones or wood. I say this truly unto you." Thus granting the boon, Brahmā went to His Brahmaloka.

युसन्तु तं वरं लब्धया मुदितः स्वगृहं ययौ ॥ १ १ ॥

शगसं विगुरो तद्धवनं महापतिः ।

त्वषा तु मुदितः प्राप्त पुत्र प्राप्तवरं तदा ॥ १ २ ॥

स्वस्ति तेजसु महाभाग जहि शक्रं सिंगु मम् ।

हूला गच्छ विश्रिसो हन्तारं पापसंयुन्तम् ॥ १३ ॥

Vītra, too, became very glad on receiving his desired object, and returned to his own abode. The highly intelligent Vītra informed the father about the boon granted to him; Viśvakarmā become very glad to hear it. He said, “O highly fortunate One! Let all bliss and good fortune come unto you; kill Indra, my greatest enemy. Go and kill the murderer of my son Triśirā, the vicious Indra and return to me.

भव लं विद्वगाशीगः सम्याय विकयं रोगे ।

मगाधि छिपि विवृलं पुरनाशसमुद्ववम् ॥ 14 ॥

Be victorious in the battle and became the Lord of all the Devas and remove my mental agony due to the killing of my son.

जीवतो वाक्यकरणात्यशहे धृरिभोजनात् ।

गयायां चिपडदानाच्छ विधिभः पुत्रस्य पुत्रत ॥ 15 ॥

तस्मातुप्र मापात्यधे दुःखं नागितुमहित्र ।

त्रिश्रा मम चित्तानु नापसर्पति कर्हिचित् ॥ 16 ॥

A son becomes then really a son when he obeys the commands of his father and when he feeds

plentifully good many people on the Śrāddha day (after his father's death) and when he offers Pindā at Gayā. Therefore, O Son! Keep my words and try to remove my sorrows.

सुशीलः सत्यवाटी च तापयो वेद्यितमः ।

अपराधं विना तेन निरतः पापबुद्धिना ॥ 17 ॥

Know this as certain that Triśirā vanishes from my mind. Triśirā was very truthful, amiable and good-natured; he was an ascetic and foremost amongst the Vedic scholars. The wicked Indra killed my dear son without any offence."

व्यास उवाच

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा वृत्रः परमदुर्जयः ।

रथमाङ्ठा तस्ता निजर्जाम पितुर्णैतृत्ति ॥ १ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing the father's words, that extremely Vītrāsura mounted on his chariot and quickly got out of his father's house.

रणतदुन्धपिनिर्याँ सज्ञानं महाकलम् ।

कारयित्या प्रयाणं स चकार मदार्थिकं: ॥ १९ ॥

The proud Asura, then, marched to the battle, accompanied with his vast army, to the sounding of the conchsells and war drums.

निर्यंयी नवसंयुक्तः सेवकानिति संवद्न् ।

हत्वा शक्र प्रश्रीध्यामि सुरात्यमकंटकम् ॥ 20 ॥

Vītrā, versed in politics and morals, exhorted his soldiers before marching and said : “To-day we will kill Indra and possess the kingdom of the Immortals, freed of all enemies.”

इत्युक्त्वा निर्वगामायु स्वरी-ययरिवारितः ।

महता सैन्यान्देन भीषयत्रमवारतीम् ॥ 21 ॥

O King! Thus, accompanied by his soldiers, and raising a tremendous war-cry terrifying to the Devas, the Asura set out for battle.

तमागच्छनमात्राय तुराषाडपि सत्वः ।

सेनोष्ठांगे भयत्रस्तः कार्यवामास भारत ॥ 22 ॥

सर्वानाहुय ततसा लोकपालानिर्दभः ।

युद्धाद्धी प्रेयनस्वर्ण-व्योत्तत महावृत्ति: ॥ 23 ॥

O Bhārata! The King of the Devas, knowing that the Asura is quite at hand, became overwhelmed with terror and ordered at once the

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

soldiers to be ready for the battle and called quickly all the Lokapālas and sent them all for the battle.

पृध्यूई ततः कृत्वा संस्थितः पाकशासनः ।

तत्राजगाम वेगतु चूवः परबलादर्दिनः ॥ 24 ॥

देवदानवयोस्तावलभ्यामस्तमुतोःघवत् ।

वृत्रासवयो: संख्ये मनसा विजर्विषणोः ॥ 25 ॥

The highly lustrous Indra, the tormentor of the foes, arrayed his troops in order according to Grdhra Vyūha (the method in which the vultures arrange themselves while flying) and stayed there. On the other hand Vṛtra, the slayer of enemies, dashed unto that place with all swiftness.

A dreadful fight then ensued between the Devas and Dānavas; the two parties each, desirous to get victory over the other, fought awfully hard.

एवं परस्यं युद्धे सन्दीपे भयदे भृगम् ।

आकृतं देवता: प्रगुदैत्याशु परमां मुद्धू ॥ 26 ॥

तोभरैर्पिंद्यालेशं खद्धरैः परगुपिडुर्शैः ।

जन्यः परमयं देवदैवा: स्वस्ववरायुधैः ॥ 27 ॥

When the blaze of the battle fire rose to a very high pitch, the Devas dropped with sorrow while the Asuras became excited with joy. The Devas and Dānavas struck each other with Tomaras, Bhindipālas, axes, Parasūs, Patiṣas, and various other weapons.

एवं युद्धे वर्तमाने दारुको लोमहर्षेगे ।

गऊ क्रजाह सहसा वृढ़: क्रीभप्रमन्तिन: ॥ 28 ॥

अपावूल्य मुखे क्रिपला स्थितो चूवः शतकतुम् ।

मुक्तिडेपृभ्नहाराज पूर्ववैरमनुस्मर् ॥ 29 ॥

When the dreadful battle rose to a high pitch causing horripilation, Vītra became very angry and suddenly caught hold of Indra and denuding him of all clothes and armors swallowed him; he, then, remembering his former enmity, became very glad and stayed there.

गढ़े प्रतलेख वड्रेण सम्ब्धान्ता निर्जासताद ।

चुक्रुगु: परपातांतरे हा ग्रकौति मुदुघुँ; ॥ 30 ॥

अपावृते मुखे गढ़े म्रांक्र जाता सर्वे दिवैकास: ।

ब्रह्मास्ति प्रणायोयुदीन व्यक्षितचेतसः ॥ 31 ॥

किं कतल्वे द्विजश्रेष्ठ त्वमसाकं गुरः परः ।

ग्राको प्रस्ततु चुवेण रक्षिते देवतांतरिः ॥ 32 ॥

When Indra was thus devoured by Vītra, the Devas were overwhelmed with terror and cried out frequently, with great distress: "O Indra! O Indra! All the Devas became very dejected and grieved in their hearts to see Indra denuded of his armour and clothes in the belly of Vītra and bowed down to Brhaspati and said: "O Indra of the Brāhmaṇas! You are our best Guru; what are we to do now? Though the gods tried their best to save Indra, still Vītra has devoured him.

विना शक्रेन किं कुर्भ: सर्वे हीनपातकामः ।

अधिचारं कुक विषो सत्वः शक्रमुक्ते ॥ 33 ॥

We are all powerless, what can we do without Indra? O Lord! Perform quickly magic spells (Abhicāra process) which will lead to our Indra's liberation."

ब्रह्मस्तिवलाच

किं कर्तव्यं सुरा: शिक्षो मुखमयेप्रति वासवः ।

वृजीणतमादितो जीवप्रतिन कोढ़ीतर तेपोः ॥ 34 ॥

Brhaspati said: "O Suras! The king of the gods is swallowed by Vṛtra, he has been quite disabled; but Indra is living in his bowels; attempt therefore must be made that he comes out while living.

व्यास उवाच

देवाधिंतातुरा: सर्वे सुरासाई तथाकृतम् ।

दृष्टवा विमृष्य तसा चकुर्बनं विमुक्तचे ॥ 35 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O King! The Devas became very anxious to see Indra in that plight and took all the ways and means carefully how he might be freed.

असुर्जत महास्तचां तृपिमकां रिपुताशिनीम् ।

ततो विजूभ्यमाण: स व्यावृतस्यो बभूव ह ॥ 36 ॥

Then they created a state tending to cause yawning, very powerful and irresistible and calculated to destroy one's enemy.

विजृष्मयाणास्य ततो बृत्रस्यास्यदापतत् ।

स्वाज्ञान्यपि संबिध्य निष्कान्तो बलस्मुरुव: ॥ 37 ॥

Vīrtāsura then yawned and his mouth got widely opened and extended. In the meanwhile Indra, the destroyer of one's enemies strength, contracted all

Book VI Chapter IV

his limbs and came out of the expanded mouth of the Asura and fell down.

ततः प्रभृति लोकेषु ज्ञीपक्का प्राणिसंस्थता ।

जहुशुश सुरा: सर्वे शर्क इयूदा विनिर्मितम् ॥ 38 ॥

Since that time, this state of yawning has become prevalent amongst the beings. The Devas were all glad to see Indra thus come out.

ततः प्रवृत्ते युद्धे त्वोलोकभयप्रदम् ।

वर्षाणामयुतं यावहूफणं लोमहर्षणम् ॥ 39 ॥

When Indra thus got out, he fought again with Vītra for 10,000 years (Ayuta years). The fight was very dreadful, causing horripilation.

एकतश्ठा सुरा: सर्वे युद्धाय समुपस्थिता: ।

एकतो बतलांस्त्वादुः संग्रामे समवर्तेत ॥ 40 ॥

On one side all the Devas joined in the battle; on the other side, the pre-eminently powerful Vītra, the son of Viśvakarmañ fought.

यदा व्यक्तित रणे चुचो वरमदावृतः ।

पराजितस्तदा शक्रसंज्ञे सात्य चर्षितः ॥ 41 ॥

विवध्ये मघवा युद्धे ततः प्राय्य पराजयम् ।

विवादमगमन्टेवा दृष्टवा शक्रे पराजितम् ॥ 42 ॥

जगमूस्वक्तवा रांग सर्वे देवा इन्द्रपुरोभागः ।

गृहीतं देवसदनं चुवेणागत्य रंहसा ॥ 43 ॥

When Vītrāsura got more and more energy in the battle, Indra became gradually dwindled and was at last defeated. Indra became very much grieved when he found himself defeated; the Devas also were very dejected to see this. Indra and the other Devas quitted the battle-field and fled away.

देवोहानानि सविणि धुंकाउसी दानवो बलात् ।

ऐरावतोदपि दैत्येन युहितोसी गजोत्तमः ॥ 44 ॥

Vītrāsura too, quickly arrived and occupied the Heavens. Vītra began to enjoy by force the Heavenly gardens and took the Airāvatā elephant. विमानानि च सर्वाणि गृहीतानि विशांपते ।

उच्चे: श्वला ह्ववयो जाततस्तय वरे गते ॥ 45 ॥

कामयेनु: पारिजातो गणशासरां सतां ।

गृहीतं रत्नायां नुं तेन त्वसुतुने ह १। 46 ॥

O King! The Asura, the son of Tvaṣṭā, took away all Vimānas (the self-moving chariot of

gods), Uccaisārava, the best of horses, the heavenly cow, the giver of all desires, the Pārījāta tree, the Apsarās, and all other jewels of the Heavens.

स्थानधारा: सुरा: सर्वे निरिवुरुंगे संस्थिताः ।

दुःखमापु: परिध्धा चक्रभागासुरालयात् ॥ 47 ॥

चूत्र: सुरपूदं प्राय० बभूव मद्रार्वितः ।

त्वधातरीन सुखं प्राय० मुमोद सुतसंयुतः ॥ 48 ॥

The Devas, on the other hand, deprived of their shares in sacrifices and driven away from their Heavens, suffered very much. Vṛtāsura became puffed up with vanity, when he got possession of the Heavens. Viśvakarma, too, became very happy at that time and began to enjoy pleasures along with his son.

अमंत्रयिन्द्रतं देवा सुनिधि: सह भारत ।

कि कतव्यमिति प्रायो विवित्य भयमोहिति: ॥ ४ १ ॥

जमु: कैतासमवर्त सुरा: शक्रममवित्रतः ।

महावेदं प्रणाभ्योचु: प्रहा: प्रांजलयो भृगाम् ॥ 50 ॥

देवदेव महादेव कृपामित्यो महेश्व ।

सहासमान्यभधीतांतु वृज्योगतिपराजितान् ॥ 51 ॥

O Bharata! The Devas, then, united with the Munis and they began to consult about their own welfare. When the Devas took Indra with them and went to Mahā Deva in the Mount Kailāśa and bowed down to His feet very humbly and, with folded hands, spoke thus: "O Deva of the Devas! O Mahā Deva! Thou art the Mahesvara and the unbounded Ocean of Mercy! We are defeated by Vṛtrasura and we are very much terrified.

गृहीतं देवसदनं तेन देव कवीरमा ।

कि कतक्यमतः शम्भो बृहि सर्व शिवाय नः ॥ 52 ॥

Save us, O Śambhu! Thou dost good to all the beings; dost thou tell us, therefore, truly what are we to do now when that powerful Dānava has dispossessed us of our Heavens.

कि कुर्ज: कव च भच्छाम: स्थानप्रदा महेश्व ।

दुःखस्य नाएगवच्छामो विजानोषायामीक्षा ॥ 53 ॥

O Maheśa! Now dislodged, where are we to go? We are not finding any remedy by which our miseries can be destroyed.

साहायें कुक भूतेश व्यथिता: स्म कुपानिधे ।

यूत्र जहि मदोसित्कं वदानबलाहिपो ॥ 5४ ॥

O Bhūta Bhāvana! We are very much pained; helps us; O merciful One! That Vītrāsura has become intoxicated with vanity due to his being granted the boon. Therefore destroy him."

शिरा खचा

ब्रहाणं पुतः कृत्वा बयं सर्वे हरेः क्षयम् ।

गत्वा समेत्य तं विधुं चित्तयामो वधीडामम् ॥ 55 ॥

Śaṅkara said: “O Devas! We will keep Brahmā in the front and let all of us go to the residence of Hari and there consult with Him how to destroy this unruly Vṛtra.

स शफक्ष छलज्ञे बलवानुविद्मन्तः ।

शरणयछ दयाधिछ्धा वासुदेवो जनादनः ॥ ५६ ॥

The Janārdana Vāsudeva is fully capable to do all actions. He is powerful, knower of pretexts, highly intelligent, ocean of mercy, and fit to be asked by all for protection.

विना तं देवदेवेगं नार्थसिद्धमविष्यति ।

तस्मात्त च गतन्यं सर्वेकार्यार्थसिद्धदे १ 57 ॥

Without Him, the Deva of the Devas, no success is possible in any action. Therefore all of us ought to go there for the success in our undertaking.

व्यास उवाच

इति सखिल ते सर्वे बहा शकः सशंकतः ।

जभुपिंक्षिणः क्षयं देया: शरणपे भक्तवललम्। 5 8 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O King! Thus settling their plan of action, Indra and other Devas took Śankara and

Brahmā with them and went to the abode of Hari, who protects all and is gracious to His devotees.

नत्वा विद्युपदं देवास्तुषुवु: परमेश्वरम् ।

हरि पुरुषसूक्ति वेवोत्नन जगतुकम् ॥ 59 ॥

They, then, began to chant Puruṣasūkta hymns to Him and thus they praised the God Hari, the Guru of this Universe.

प्रत्यक्षोः भृज्ञगाथत्सेति स कमलापति: ।

समान्य च सुत्रान्वविन्युवाच उप:स्थितः ॥ 60 ॥

किमागता: स लोकेशा हरबहामन्विता ।

कारणं कथायधं च: सर्वेधां सुसत्तमाः ॥ 61 ॥

The Janārdana Hari, the Lord of Kamalā, then, appeared before them and, after showing his respect, addressed them thus: O Lord of the several Lokas! What have brought you all together with Brahmā and Śaṅkara hither? O best of Suras! Please tell me the reason of your coming here.

व्यास उवाच

इति शुत्वा हरेर्वाक्यं नोचुदैवा सभापितम् ।

चित्तविषहा: स्थिता: ग्रायः सर्वे प्राजलवस्तथा ॥ 62 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे पशवल्ये

चतुर्वाँस्थायः ॥ 4 ॥

Vyāsa said: "O King! Thus hearing Hari's words, the Devas could not reply anything; rather almost all of them remained with an anxious look with their hands folded, overwhelmed with cares. Here ends the Fourth Chapter of the Sixth Book on the efeat of the Devas by Vṛtra in the Mahā Purānam Śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Vyāsa said: “O King! Thus hearing Hari’s words, the Devas could not reply anything; rather almost all of them remained with an anxious look with their hands folded, overwhelmed with cares. Here ends the Fourth Chapter of the Sixth Book on the efetat of the Devas by Vītra in the Mahā Purānam Śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER V

On Praising the Devi

व्यास उवाच

तथा चिन्तातुर्गन् वीक्ष्य सर्वान्सर्वांतित्त्वित् ।

प्राह प्रेमभरोद्धान्तान्माधवो मेदिनीपते ॥ १ ॥

विष्णुर्वाच

क्रि मौन्माश्रिता युयं बुवंतु कारणं सुराः ।

सदस्व्राउपि तच्छुत्वा यतिच्ये तत्रिवारये ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Nārāyaṇa, the Lord of Lakṣmī, and the knower of the essences of all subjects, seeing the Devas extremely attached to him and anxious, spoke to them thus: O Suras! Why have you kept silent? Tell me why you have all come, let it be good or bad, tell me; I will try to remove your miseries.

Book VI Chapter V

देश ऊचुः

किम्भातं तव विषो स्थि लोकेषु वर्तते ।

सर्वे वेद भवान्कार्यं किं पृष्ठसि पुनः पुनः ॥ ३ ॥

त्वया पूर्वे बलिर्बंदः शगडो देवाधिपः कृतः ।

वामनं वपुताश्याय क्रान्तं तिभुचनं पदे ॥ ४ ॥

The Devas said: “O Lord! Is there any thing unknown to you in this Trilokī; You know everything; why then art Thou asking us again and again? In ancient times You in your Dwarf incarnation overspread the three worlds by Your three feet and thus bound the King Bali in his own premises and gave of sovereignty over the Devas to Indra.

अमृतं त्वाहुतं विजणो दैत्याश्र विविपतिततः ।

त्वं प्रपुः सर्वदैवानां सर्वापहित्विवारणो ॥ 5 ॥

O All Pervading One! It is You who deluded the Daityas and procured nectar for the Devas, and it is You who sent them to the house of Death. Therefore, O Lord! You are the one and only one that is capable in warding off all the evils that befall on the Devas.

विष्णुस्वाच

न भेतल्यं सुरावरा वेच्युपायं सुसंमतम् ।

तद्धाय प्रवक्ष्यामि येन सौख्यं भविष्यति ॥ 6 ॥

Thus hearing the Devas' words, Viṣṇu said: "O Suras! You need not fear; I know one remedy, approved by all, by which that Daityas might be killed so that you would be happy. I am now giving it out to you.

अवश्यं क्रपाँयि मे भवतां हितामात्मा ।

बुद्ध्वा बलेन चार्थेन येन केन छलेन वा ॥ 7 ॥

Your welfare, your benefit must be looked at by me whether by the exercise of my intelligence or by using my prowess, by wealth, by pretext or by any other means whatsoever.

उपाया: खलु चत्वार: कवितास्त्वर्शिभि: ।

सामावय: सुढ़लेवे दुर्दैसु विशेषतः ॥ 8 ॥

Four means, viz., conciliation, gifts, sowing discord, or punishment are mentioned by the wise

statesmen to be applied to friends and specially to the enemies.

बहाणास्थ वयो दत्सलपास़ारतीधेतन च ।

दुर्जल्यं च संप्रातं वरत्नप्रभावतः ॥ 9 ॥

Brahmā was worshipped by Vṛtra with severe austerities and He granted boons and it is due to the influence of that favour that this Asura has become so indomitable.

अजेवः सर्वैभृतानां त्वधुं समुपपादितः ।

ततो बलेन वृद्धिं स प्रातः परपुरंबतः ॥ 10 ॥

दु:साध्योजसी सुरा: शकुर्विना सामप्रातरणम् ।

प्रलोभ्य वर्जगामयेयं हन्तव्यस्तु ततः परम् ॥ 11 ॥

The more so that Viśvakarmā created him from the sacrificial fire; it is through all these causes that the Demon Vṛtṣārusa, the conqueror of the enemies' stronghold, has grown up so very powerful that he can hardly be conquered by any being.

गच्छध्यं सर्वान्यर्वा यत्रासित बलवन्तः ।

साम तस्य प्रपुंजभयं तत एनं विजेषध्य ॥ 12 ॥

O Suras! First peace must be negotiated with him; then deceive him, otherwise the enemy will be very difficult to conquer. First entice him and bring him under control; then kill him.

संगम यणवान्कूला विश्वास्य समयेन हि ।

मित्रत्वं च समाधाय हेतल्यः प्रबलो रिपुः ॥ 13 ॥

Now take the Rsis and Gandharvas with you and go where the powerful enemy Vītrāsura is residing and make a treaty with him; thus he will be conquered.

अनुव्यः संप्रदेक्ष्यायि वज्रमय्य वरायुधम् ।

साहाय्यं च कर्तियासि शक्रस्पहां सुगतमाः ॥ 14 ॥

Swear on oath and accede to the terms he proposes and thus create faith in him; then cultivate friendship with him; lastly, when time will come, kill that powerful enemy.

समयं च प्रतीक्ष्यं सर्वैवायुप: क्षये ।

मरणं विबुधास्तस्य नान्यथा सम्पत्थिति ॥ 15 ॥

O Suras! I will also enter, unseen by anybody, into the excellent weapon of Indra, his thunderbolt

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

and will help him in due time. Wait till the period of his longevity expires; otherwise his death will never take place.

गच्छधर्मविधि: सार्थ नाथवां: कपटावुता: 1

इंज्ञण सह मित्रत्वं कुर्वर्ध वाक्यवादानतः ॥ १ ६ ॥

Now go to that Asura, with Gandharvas and Rsis and cunningly cultivate friendship between him and Indra, by conciliatory words; when he begins thus to put his confidence, then deceive him. I will enter hidden into the strong well-covered thunderbolt.

वथा स याति विश्रासं तथा कार्य प्रतारणम् ।

गुप्तोई संपवेक्ष्यामि परिवं संघादितं इदम् ॥ १ ७ ॥

When Indra will come to know that the Demon has put complete faith in him, he will hurl his thunderbolt against him and thus the enemy will be killed and not otherwise.

विश्रभं मधवा श्रगु हनिष्वित न चान्यथा ।

विश्रास्य कृते पार्थ पंकृता शक्रस्तु पुरुषः ॥ १ ८ ॥

O Lord of the Devas! Do not consider for the present the act of treachery that you will commit: take my help and kill that wicked Demon with thunderbolt.

मस्तहायोख वज्जेण शान्तीव्यति पापिनम् ।

न तोषो3त्र उले शगी जाठ्यमेव प्रकुर्जत: ॥ १९ ॥

To practise hypocrisy with an hypocrite is not considered a sin; specially no powerful enemy can be killed only by the well known rules applicable to warriors, without any deceit.

नान्यथा बलवान् वध्यः शूरधर्मेण जायते ।

वामनं रूपमाधाय यमात्यं बक्षितो बक्षितः ॥ 20 ॥

I also deceived, before, Bali, with my dwarf body and again I deceived all the Demons by showing myself as a beautiful woman; therefore to practise deceit with a strong deceitful enemy is never considered a sin.

कृत्वा च भोहिणीवे दैव्या: सर्वेपि वक्षिताः।

पबंतः सहिताः सर्वे देवीं भगवर्ती शिक्षाम् ॥ 2 1 ॥

Know this. O Devas! Now you all conjointly worship the Devi Bhagavatī with Mantras and

prayers and take Her shelter; the Yoga Māyā, then, will help you.

गच्छधं शरणं भावै: स्तोजमनैः सुरोतमाः ।

साहायं सा योगमाया भवतां संशियास्यति ॥ 22 ॥

वन्दामहे सदा देवीं सात्चिकों प्रकृति पराग ।

सिद्धिदां कामवां कामां दुरापामकृतात्मधिः ॥ 23 ॥

We too, worship that Devi, the Highest Prakrti, the Incarnate of pure Satva Guna, Who grants success, bestows us all our desires, Who is Herself the object of desires, and Who is never realised by any except by those Yogis, self-controlled pure men.

इंत्रोर्षि ता समाराध्य हनिष्यति सिंगुं गे।

मोहित्री सा महामाया मोहलिययात दानवम् ॥ 24 ॥

Indra, too, will certainly be able to kill his enemy in battle if he worships Her; for the Mahā Māyā, the Creatrix of Delusion, will, when worshipped, delude that Demon.

मोहितो मायाव युः सुखसाधो भविष्यति ।

प्रसवायां परांवायां सर्व साधयं भविष्यति ॥ 25 ॥

Thus deluded by Her Māyā, Vītrāsura will easily be killed by him; there is no doubt in this, what more do you want than this that everything will be successfully accomplished when the Devi Ambikā is propitiated and gets well pleased.

नोचेमनोराथावादिनं कंस्याय विधायिति ।

अन्त्यामिवस्कपा सा सर्वकारणकारण ।। 26 ॥

She regulates the hearts of all and is the Cause of all causes. Without Her worship no one's desires can be expected to be accomplished.

तस्मातां विश्वजन्नीं प्रकृति परमातृतः ।

भज्ञं सात्विकेभर्ते: श्रनुजागय सतमाः ॥ 27 ॥

Therefore, O Best of Suras! Worship the Universal Mother, the Prakrti Devi with greatest devotion and with greatest purity for the destruction of your enemy.

पुरा मवाउपि संगाम कृत्वा परम दारभणम् ।

पढ़वर्षसहस्राणि नितही मधुकैटफी ॥ 28 ॥

See! In days of yore, I fought for five thousand years, dreadfully with the two Demons Madhu and Kaíṭabha and then killed them.

Book VI Chapter V

स्तुता मया तदात्यर्थ प्रससा प्रकृति: परा ।

मोहिती ती तदा देवी छलेन च मया हतो ॥ 29 ॥

विप्रलक्षी महाबाहू दानवो मदार्थिति ।

तथा कुलघं प्रकृतेनैर्जनं भावस्यभुतः ॥ 30 ॥

I worshipped, then, the Mahā Māyā, the Highest Prakrti; She was thus pleased and deluded the two Asuras; thus the two powerful Asuras puffed up with vanity were deluded and thus I could kill those terrible Daityas under a pretext.

सर्वेधा कार्यसिद्धि सा कार्यसिंत सुरोतमाः ।

एवं ते दत्तमतयो विद्याना प्रभविष्यानुना ॥ 31 ॥

Therefore, O Suras! You, too, worship that Highest Prakṛti with the greatest devotion; She will then surely fulfill your desires."

जम्पुते मेसीखारं मन्त्राद्रामपिडतम् ।

एकाने संश्चिता देवाः कृत्वा ध्यानं जयं तपः ॥ 32 ॥

गुह्युर्जंगता धार्जी सृष्टिसंहाराकिरणिभू ।

भक्काममुद्रामंबां संसाराक्लेशनाशिनीम् ॥ 33 ॥

O King! When the intelligent Visnu enlightened thus the Devas, they went to the top of the Mount Sumeru, adorned with the Mandāra trees, remaining at a secluded place, recited slowly Her Mantrams and thus engaged in asceticism and meditation, began to chant hymns and praise that Universal Mother, the Holder of the world, the Remover of all worldly ailings and the Creatrix, Preservrix and Destructrix of the world and the Bestover of all desires to Her devotees.

देश ऊचुः

देव प्रसिद परिपाहि सुगन्मतान्

चूगसूगे रामरे परिपीक्षितांश ।

दीनानिताशनपरे परमार्थतचे

प्राप्तिस्वर्दिकमलं गरणं सदैव ॥ 34 ॥

The Devas said: "O Devi! Be graciously pleased unto us! O Thou, the Destructrix of the afflictions of the distressed! We have taken refuge unto Thy lotus-feet. We have been defeated by Vṛtrāsura in the battle, we are very much oppressed and afflicted.

त्वं सर्वविश्वजननी परियलायमान्

पूत्रानिवासितपितातिपुसंकेतप्रम्न् ।

मातनं तेउस्वविदितं भुवनयेप्रप

कस्मादुपेक्षासि सुरानसुपत्रतान् ॥ 35 ॥

O Thou, the Highest Reality! O Thou, the Mother of the whole Universe! Protect us as a Mother protects her child; we are fallen into this difficulty arising from our enemies.

त्रैलोक्यमेतदिज्वलि विहितं त्वयेव

ब्रहा हरि: पशुपतित्वल वासत्नोल्या: ।

कुर्वन्ति कार्यमिहलं स्वलग्न न ते ते

धूभंगचालनवशगतिहूरतिं कामम् ॥ 36 ॥

O Mother! Nothing is hidden from Thy knowledge in the three worlds. Why art Thou taking no notice of us, that are being tormented by the Asuras! O Mother! Thou createst, preservest, and destroyest the three worlds; Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Maheśa are created by Thy mere will and are doing all Thy works.

माता सुताम्यरिघावत्यियाति दीनान्

गीतिस्ववैय रचिता प्रकटापशयान् ।

कस्मात्र पालयसि देखे विनापराधानस्मार-

स्वदर्शिशरणाकरणारासाढे ॥ 37 ॥

O Mother! They are not independent; by the contraction of Thy eye-brows, they are directed and enjoy all the pleasures. The Mother protects her sons afflicted with various difficulties and dangers, even when they are found guilty of various offences. It is Thou that hast made this rule; then why, O Merciful! Art Thou not protecting us who are quite innocent and whom Thou dost know as having taken refuge unto Thy lotus-feet.

नूनं मन्दधिबजानपदाय: किलेले

थक्ति विहाय विषये सुखभोगलुखाः ।

नेमे कटाक्षविषया इति चेच चैया सिंतः

सुते जनकर्तौरं चापि दृष्टा ॥ 38 ॥

O Devi! If Thou thinkest that we forget Thee, being too much attracted by the enjoyments that Thou hast been pleased to confer unto us and therefore we are proper not to be looked upon with

Śrimaddevibhāgavatum

Thy merciful eyes, we would say that this is quite true; but, O Mother! Nowhere is seen such a feeling of a Mother to Her child; we are no doubt, objects of Thy mercy and favour always.

दोषो न नोउज जनानि प्रतिभागिति चित्ते

यते विहाय भजनं विषवे निममना: ।

मोहल्यवा विरचितः प्रभवल्यसी

नततमात्मल्याबककणे दयसे कर्थ न । ३ ९ ॥

Besides there is no fault of us in this matter, O Mother! that we do not worship Thee and become immersed in sensual enjoyments; for Thy creation, the Moha (delusion) is very powerful and deludes us. O Mother! Thou art naturally Merciful! Knowing these, why art Thou not showing mercy unto us.

पूर्वं त्वया जननि दैत्यपतिवर्षितिष्ठो

व्यापादितो महिष्करणधरः किताबजी ।

अस्मकृते सकललोकभयवाहोउसौ

ब्रुं कवं न भवदं विधुप्तोषि मातः ॥ 4 0 ॥

O Devi! Thou hadst killed before in battle, for our sake, the powerful Daitya Chief Mahiśāsura, very terrible to all the beings. Then why art not Thou, O Mother! killing this dreadful Vṛtrāsura?

गुभमलाउसतिवलावानुजो निरुभमस्ती

भातौ तदनुगा निहता हतौ च ।

दृत्र तथा जहि खलं प्रबलं द्याएँदे मन्ते

विमोह्य तथा न भवेढ्याएँसी ॥ 4 1 ॥

O Mother! Thou hadst killed the two brother Daityas, Śumbha and Niśumbha, extraordinarily powerful, and the other Daityas that followed them; O Thou, the embodiment of mercy! Similarly destroy now this deceitful strong Vṛtrāśura. O Mother! Delude this proud Asura so that he could not manifest, in the least, his power.

त्वं पालयाद्ध विबुधानसुरेण मातः

सन्तापिनानिततां भयविक्छलांश ।

नान्यो5ति को3पि भुवनेषु सुर्गात्रिंहता यः

क्लेशजालयिखलं निवदैत्स्वजास्कता ॥ 4 2 ॥

We are very much troubled by the Asuras and overwhelmed with terror from them; Thou savest

us; for there is no other in the three worlds that can by his own force remove the sorrows and sufferings of the Devas.

यूत्र देदा तव यदि प्रथिता तथापि

जहोनामृषु जननुःखकरं खरं च ।

पापात्समुद्धर भवानि शरैः पुनाना

नोचेत्यायतीत तमो ननू दृष्टवृद्धि: ॥ 43 ॥

O Mother! Though Thou hast shown favour towards Vṛtra, now dost kill him soon, whose nature is cruel and tormenting to others. O Bhavānī! Better dost Thou save him from sin by Thy holy arrows. Otherwise that vicious. Asura will surely enter into the hideous Hell.

ते प्रापिता: सुरवनं विद्धुपारयो ये हत्या

राणेपि विशिष्टः किल पाचितास्ते ।

त्राता न किं निरसयातभवाह्याद्रों

यच्छत्रवोप्र न कि किं विहित्रंसि शूजम् ॥ 44 ॥

It is for his welfare that Thou oughtest to kill him. Those that had been before enemies of the Gods, Thou didst purify them by weapons in the battle-field and hadst sent them to the Nandana Garden in the Heavens. O Thou, the Mercy personified! Was it not that Thou didst not save them from hell? Then why art not Thou killing this Vītrāsura!

जानीमहै सिपुरसी तब सेवको न

प्रायेण पीडवति नः किल पाभवूद्धि: ।

यस्तावकसिर्वह भवेद्यमरानसी

किन्चल्यादंकजरात्वनु पीडवेझा ॥ 45 ॥

We know this for certain that the Asura is Thy enemy, not Thy servant; for that mischievous soul is giving us trouble. O Mother! How can he be Thy servant and devotee who torments the Devas that are always engaged in worshipping Thy lotus feet. O Mother! How can we perform Thy worship?

कुर्म: कवं जननि पुजनमह ते उम्ब

पुष्पादिके तव विनिर्मितेय वयमात् ।

मन्त्रा वयं च सकलं यगर्जित्रकर्णं

तस्माद्वानि चरणे प्रणता: स्म नूनम् ॥ 46 ॥

Book VI Chapter V

The flowers and other acticles used in worship all are created by Thee; especially we and the Mantras, in fact, everything is the manifestation of Thy power. Therefore, O Bhavānī! We worship Thee by laying ourselves prostrate on Thy feet. Best Thou pleased.

वन्यास्त एव मनुजा हि भवतीत भक्या

पार्वेब्जं तव भवाधिव्यतेषु पोतम् ।

वं योगिनोर्षि यनसा सततं स्मरिते

मोक्षार्थिनो विनतारणविकारमोहाः ॥ 47 ॥

Those men are blessed that worship with devotion Thy lotus feet for crossing this ocean of world. O Devi! Those Yogins that want final liberation and forsake therefore all attachments, vikāras and delusions, even they attain success then only when they meditate Thy lotus feet.

वे यात्रिकाः सकलवेदिविदोंरि नूनं

त्वां संस्मरिति सत्ततं किल हीमकाले ।

स्वाहां तु तृतीजननीनमुरुंधाणां भृध:

स्वधां पितुनाणस्य च तृप्तिकेतुम् ॥ 48 ॥

Those that are great Sacrifices and know best the essence of the Vedas, even they when they offer oblations to the sacrifice, utter “Svāhā” that is cheering to the Devas and “Svadhā” very consoling to the Pitts; thus they always think of Thee (for “Svāhā and Svadhā” are Thy names only).

मेधाउस कान्तिस प्राणत्नस प्रसिद्धा

ब्रैद्धस्वमेव शिरादायंकी नराणाम ।

सर्वे त्यमेव विषधं वघवन्येउस्मिन्

कृत्वा द्वारासे भक्तां कृपया सदैव ॥ 49 ॥

O Mother! Thou art the retentive power and memory. Thou art the beauty, Thou art the peace, Thou art the Buddhist (intellect) well know to clarify men's minds; and Thou art the prosperity and wealth of all these three worlds. O Devi! Those that worship Thee, Thou givest them, out of mercy, those wealth in some way or other."

व्यास उवाच

एवं स्तुता सुरैद्वी प्रव्यक्षा सारभवत्ता ।

चारूकपधारा तन्वी सर्वभिरणभूषिता ॥ 5 50 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O King! Thou worshipped by the Devās, the Devi Bhagavatī appeared before them in a very beautiful form, thin, adorned with all ornaments.

पाणाङ्गशराभीतिलसद्वाहुचतुध्या ।

शालिकीपाकाजालरशनावब्द्रसल्किदः ॥ 51 ॥

Her two hands holding a noose, and goad, and the other two hands making signs to discard all fear and ready to grant boons; Her loins very beautiful, girdled with a gold band with small bells pending and making sweet tinkling sounds; Her feet with anklets (ornaments) making sweet sonorous sounds with tiny tinkling bells.

कलकधरीया कान्ता ब्वणलकर्णपुत्रु ।

चन्द्रखण्डसमाबद्धरलमौलिवराजिता ॥ 52 ॥

मन्दिमास्तारविन्दाया नेत्रबरविभूषिता ।

पारजिताप्रसूनान्छनीलवभसमप्रभा ॥ 53 ॥

Her voice was exceedingly sweet and lovely, Her forehead was adorned with the crescent of the Moon and on Her head was glittering a diadem of jewels, Her lotus-face adorned with sweet soft smiles and with Her three beauteous lotus-eyes looking like Indibaras Her body was of a red colour like the Pārijāta flowers and Her limbs were marked with red sandal-paste.

रकाध्वयपीधाना रकचन्दनचर्धित ।

प्रसादमुपुर्धी देवी कर्णगारससागरा ॥ 54 ॥

सर्वेयुक्त्रावेषाढ्या सर्वद्वितारिध; पत्र ।

सर्वांजा सर्वकर्जी च सर्वाधिष्ठान्कारिणी ॥ 55 ॥

सर्वेवदान्तसिंस्दा सच्चिदान्दिक्षणी ।

प्रणीपुस्ता समालोक्य सुरा देवी पुरःस्थितान् ॥ 56 ॥

तानाहु प्रणतान्दा किं कः कार्य ब्रवनु भाम ।

She was dressed in a red attire. The Devi looked well pleased, like an ocean of infinite mercy, wearing complete dress suited to happy interviews, the Creatrix of all this Cosmos, the Highest, the Knower of all, the Directrix of all, and the Great Upholder of all. She looked like an embodiment of the Truth of all Vedāntas and the Incarnate of

Śrimaddevibhagavatam

ever Existence, Intelligence, Bliss, the Mahā Devi Bhagavati Bhuvaneśvari. The Devas all bowed down before Her standing in front of them. The Mother then spoke: “What business have you got here? Speak to Me.”

देवा ऊनुः

मोहैर्यैं नि सुं चुरं देवानामितिदुःखदूम ॥ 57 ॥

यथा विश्वसते देवांस्तथा कुरु विमोहितम् ।

आयुधे च बलं देहि हतः स्याधेन वा सिुः ॥ 58 ॥

The Devas said: “O Bhagavatī! Vṛtrāsura is tormenting much the Devas; Bewitch him. O Devi! Do such as he can trust the Devas; and impart then strength on our weapons such as he can be killed.”

ध्यास उवाच:

तथेयुक्त्वा भगवती तत्रैवान्तथीयत ।

स्वानि स्वानि विकेतानि जभुदैवा मुदाविता: ॥ ५९ ॥

इति श्रीमदैबीभागवते महापुराणो पशुरकम्बे देवीमाहात्मये

पत्रभोउवाय: 11 5 11

Vyāsa said: “O King! That will be done”, Saying thus, the Devi departed then and there. The Devas became very glad and returned respectively to their abodes.

Here ends the Fifth Chapter of the Sixth Book on the praising of the Devi by the Devas in Śrimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhaigavatam

CHAPTER VI

On Vrtrasura Slain

व्यास उवाच

पूर्व प्राप्तवरा देवा ऋष्यवघु तपोधना: ।

जम्पु: सर्वे च संमेऋ चूत्रस्याममुत्तम् ।

ददुशूलत तं वृयं ज्वलनामि तेजसा ॥ १ ॥

धक्ष्यन्तीमव लोकास्ती-ग्रास्त्तमिव चामरान् ।

ऋष्योय दय ततोघ्येल चूजभूचुः। प्रियं वचः ॥ २ ॥

देवकायाँधीयद्व्यर्थे सायमकृतं रसात्मकम् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus getting the boons from the Devi, the Devas and the Rsis blazing with their asceticism, all united and consulted with each other; then they went to the excellent Āśrama of Vṛtra. There they saw Vṛtra in a sitting posture and with his own Tejas (fiery spirit in him) as if ready to burn the three worlds and to devour all the Devas. The Rsis, then, spoke to Vṛtra the sweet words full of sentiments for the serving of the Devas' ends, according to the principle of conciliation.

རྟོགས་ཆུང་
