वंशस्य ऋषणाच्छ मुक्तः ॥ 53 ॥

Getting thus the child equivalent in form and qualities to Hari, the powerful Indra-like king became happy and freed from his debt due to his family line, became very cheerful and glad.

इति सकलसुराणामीशेणार्थितं

तं सकलनुणगणाकां पुत्रमासाठ राजा ।

विविधभ्युखविनोदैर्भियांश सेव्यमाने

व्यहरत निजगेहे शकतृत्यप्रतायः ॥ 54 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते माहपुराणे

षष्टकन्ये विशोरायाः ॥ 20 ॥

O King! The king, powerful like his enemies, began to enjoy in his own palace with his all-qualified child, that was given to him by Nārāyaṇa, the Lord of the Devas. He was always served by his dear wife and all sorts of pleasure and he felt himself enjoying as a King would do.

Here ends, the Twentieth Chapter in the Sixth Book on the son born of mare by Hari, in the Mahāpuranam in Śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XXI

On the Installation of Ekavira

भ्यास उवाच

जातकर्मादि संक्कारांक्रहार नृपतिस्तव ।

दिने दिने जगमाग्यु वृद्धिं बालः सुलातिलतः ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! In the meanwhile the King Turvasu performed the Jātakarma (a religious ceremony performed at the bātrakha (a child) and other ceremonies of the child. The boy was nurtured duly and began to grow older day to day.

यूर: सेसारंज प्राय सुरुखूद्ववम् ।

इष्णयविधोक्षं च मेने तेन महात्मना ॥ २ ॥

The King began to enjoy his worldly life on getting this son and thought within himself that

the boy had freed him from the three debts due to the Fathers, the Rşis and the Devas.

घड़ेअप्राणतं तस्य कृत्वा मासि यथाविधि ।

तृतीयेघ तथा वर्षे चुडाकरणमुतमम् ॥ ३ ॥

Next, in the sixth month, the King performed the Annaprāsana ceremony (putting the boiled rice in the mouth of the child) and in the third year performed regularly his Cūdākarana (the ceremony of the first tonsure) ceremony.

चकार ब्राह्माणज्वे: मनुष्य विविधर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्धर्न

गोर्धिश्व विविधेद्रीनैर्यचकानितततायि १ ४ १ ॥

He distributed on those occasions various

Book VI Chapter XXI

articles, wealth and cows to the Brāhmaṇas and other articles to various other mendicants and made them glad.

वर्षे चैकादृषे तस्य मौजीबन्धनकार्मे वै ।

कार्यिता धनुर्वैद्याध्ययत पार्थि १ ५ १

In the eleventh year, he performed the boy's holy thread (Upanayana) ceremony and tying the girdle made of a triple string of Muñja grass and put the boy to learn archery.

अधीनवेदे पुरं तं राजधर्मविशारादम् ।

दूद्वा तस्याधिकेवाय मिति चढ़े जनाधियः ॥ 6 ॥

Next when the boy passed off proficiently in the study of the Vedas and in learning the kingly duties, the King desired to instal him on the throne.

पुधाकंयोगसंयुक्ते दिवसे नृपसत्तमः ।

कारयामास संभारनाश्चिकार्यादगत् ॥ 7 ॥

The King Turvasu then collected with great care all the necessary articles for installation in an auspicious day, the combination of Pusyā asterism and Arka Yoga.

द्वितानाद्य वैद्वान्यर्वैशास्त्रविद्धश्रणत् ।

अभियेषेक चकाराते विधियन्त्स्वतानजस्य ह ॥ 8 ॥

He called then the Brāhmins, well versed in the Vedas and in the Śāstras, and became ready, in accordance with due rites, to perform the installation ceremony of the prince.

जलमानीय तीर्थेभ्यः सागरेष्यघु पार्थिवः ।

स्वयं चकार विधिवर्भेबिके रुष्पे दिन ॥ 9 ॥

Waters were brought from various sacred places of pilgrimage and from the several oceans and on an auspicious day the King performed himself the installation of his son.

धर्म दचावा विशेष्यो राज्ये पुत्रे निवेश्य स ।

जगाम वन्मेवाशु स्वर्गकाम: स भूपति: ॥ 10 ॥

When the ceremony was over the King gave away hoardes of wealth to the Brāhmins and giving the charge of his kingdom to his son, he went to the forest with a desire to ascend to the Heavens.

एकौरीं नृपं कृत्ता सम्पान्य सचिवानय ।

भार्येया सह भूपालः प्रयिवेश जयं शी ॥ ११ ॥

Thus placing Ekavīra on the throne, the King Turvasu shewed respects to his ministers, and, controlling his senses went to the forest accompanied by his wife.

मैनाकशिखरे राजा कृत्वा तर्तीयमाघमम् ।

निर्वं यत्रकलहाराश्रिन्यायास पार्वतीम् ॥ १ २ ॥

On the top of the Maināka mountain he took up the vow of Vānaprastha and sustaining his life on leaves and fruits began to meditate Pārvatī.

एवं स नुपतिः कृत्वा दिष्ठोत सह भार्याय ।

मृतोउसी बासलं लोकं गतः पुष्पेक कर्मण ॥ १ ३ ॥

इत्रलोकं पिता प्राप्त इति शुलासंह हैहयः ।

चकार वेदनिर्दिकं कर्म वैशोर्ध्वेदितिबम् ॥ १ ४ ॥

. Thus when his Prārabdha Karma ended, he left his mortal coil with his wife and went by virtue of his good deeds to the Indraloka. Hearing that the King had ascended to Heavens, his son Ekavīra Haihaya performed his funeral ceremonies according to the rules laid down by the Vedas.

कृतीतता: क्रिया: सर्वा: पितु: पार्थिवन्दनः ।

राज्य चकार मेघायी पिंगा दर्त सुसमान् ॥ 15 ॥

The King's son, the intelligent Haihaya, performed, one after another, all the ceremonies due and began to govern the kingdom which was free enemies.

एकवीरोअ धर्मैः प्रायः राज्यमन्तमम् ।

बुधुजे विधित्याभोगसचिवेशु सुमानितः ॥ 16 ॥

The virtuous King Ekavīra remained duly obedient to his ministers after he got possession of his kingdom and began to enjoy all the best things.

एकसिंन्दिवसं राजा मनियुर्भेः समन्वितः ।

जगाम जाइवीतीरे ह्यास्ड़ः प्रतायवान् ॥ 17 ॥

The powerful Kings one day went on horseback to the banks of the Ganges with the minister's son.

संपश्यन्यादपात्याफीकिलालापसंयुतान् ।

पुष्यितान्तसंयुक्तान्यदुपार्शिविराजितान् ॥ 18 ॥

Roaming about, he found there the boughs of trees had assumed a very graceful appearance, with loads of fruits, echoed with the sweet voice of the cuckoos and with the humming of the bees.

भुनीनामाश्रामन्स्थ्यानेद्यविनिरनादितान् ।

होभधूमातृताकाशाम्भागावसमातृत् ।। १९ ॥

Close by were the hermitages of the Munis, where the bucks were skipping about and at other places the Vedas were being chanted. The smoke was seen rising from the altars, where oblations were being offered and appeared to form like a black canopy in the Heavens.

केदाराखिलसमपबवानोषिकाभि: सुर्धितान् ।

प्रकुलपढ़ जारामार्कुकुर्जांश मनोरमान् । 20 ॥

Full ripe grains were enhancing the beauty of the fields and the cowherdesses were merrily watching the fields. Places of recreations adorned with full blown lotuses and beautiful groves were attracting the attention of the victiors.

प्रश्माणा: प्रियालांस्तु घमफलान्यनसूमान् ।

बकुलासिलकाधीयान्दारांश प्रधुलितान् ॥ 21 ॥

The various trees, Priyāla, Campaka, Panasa, Bakula, Tilaka, Kadamba and Mandāra, and others were adorned with fruits, stealing away the minds of the people.

शालांस्तालमालांश जम्बुचृतकद्यबकान ।

सूब्ध्छज्ञाहकीतोये प्रकुललं शतपत्रकम् ॥ 22 ॥

At other places, other trees Sāla, Tamāla, Jack, Mango, Kali Kadamba, etc., stood gracefully. Next when the King went to the Ganges water, he saw the gay beautiful full blown lotuses were spreading their fragrant scents all around.

पढ़ुं जं चातिन्याठ्यामपश्यद्वनीपति: ।

द्रिंक्षेण जलजस्याय पार्शुं कमललोचनाम् ॥ २३ ॥

कनकाथां सुकर्शीं च कम्बुयीवां कुशोदरीम् ।

विवोहीं सुन्दरी किंचित्समुखान्युपयोर्यम् ॥ 24 ॥

सुसारां चातसर्वांशीमयपयल्कन्यकां नृपः ।

रुदती तां सखीं नयत्वा विहूलां दुःखपीक्षिताम् ॥ 25 ॥

साशुनंत्री कन्दमानां विजने कुररीमिव ।

संर्वेश्व सरा पच्छ्छ कन्यकां शोकाकारणम् ॥ 26 ॥

सुर्से बृहि काउसि तं कस्य पुत्री शुभानने ।

नार्यादी वेदकन्यात्र कर्थे तिविंषि सुन्दारि ॥ 27 ॥

On the right side of these lotuses, he saw a lotus-eyed girl. She was shining like the gold, her

beautiful hairs were long and curling; her throat was like a Kambu, belly thin, lips like the Bimba fruits, several other limbs well built and graceful, breasts riene a little, nose beautiful and all her body was exquisitely lovely; that lady just blooming into youth was suffering bereavements from her comrades and was very distressed and seemed bewildered. She was crying like an ewe in a dense lonely forest. Seeing her, the King asked her what was the reason of her sorrows? "O Cuckoo-voiced One! You are as yet a girl; who has left you alone in this state?"

कथेक्रौकिनी वाले त्यत्ता केन पिक्सवरे ।

पतितेच वय गता: कान्ते पिता वा बृहि समाप्ति ॥ 28 ॥

किं ते दुःखमारतषु कथयाठा मपातिंके ।

कोरीमि दुःखनागं ते सर्वदीय कृणेशेटि ॥ 29 ॥

O Sweet One! Tell me where is your husband now or where is your father? O One looking askance! What is your trouble; explain it to me. C thin-bellied One! I will, no doubt, remove all your sorrows and troubles.

न राज्ये मम तर्विकृ पीडां कोउपि कोरल्यम् ।

न धर्षे चौराजं कानेत न राक्षसधयं तथा ॥ ३० ॥

मायि शासति पभाणते नोर्याता दाक्णा भुवि ।

भयं न व्याप्रसिंहेच्छो न भयं कंसचिछद्वेत ॥ ३ १ ॥

O fair-bellied One! I will, no doubt, remove all your sorrows and troubles. O fair-limbed One! In my dominion nobody ever gives trouble to any other body. O lovely One! There is no fear in my kingdom from thieves or Rāksasa; or any fear from any serious dangerous calamities on this earth, fear from lions, tigers or any other dangers while my sway is predominant.

वद्य वामोक कर्मात्चं वित्यपं जाइक्रवीते ।

कोषीय जगहीनात्रन कि के दुःखं वद्यव मे ॥ 32 ॥

O One of beautiful thighs! Why are you crying on this lonely bank of the Ganges? Tell me what is your pain?

हन्यहं दुख्यत्युं प्राणिनां पृथिवीतले ।

दैंदं च मानुषं कानते ज्ञातेतनमाज्ञुतम् ॥ 33 ॥

Book VI Chapter XXI

O Pure One! I can remove the pains and miseries, even of a serious nature, of men, whether they come from the Deva or human sources; and this is my principal vow.

विशाललोचने वृहि क्रोगेम तव चिन्तितम् ।

इत्युक्ते वचने राजा श्रुत्योवाच मुखुस्वना ॥ 34 ॥

शृणु राजेन्द्र बक्षयामि मम शोकस्य कारणम् ।

विवितिरात्रितः प्राणी कर्थं र्वतीत भूपते ॥ 35 ॥

O Large-eyed One! Speak what is your inmost desire; I will carry it out instantaneously." When the king thus spoke, that beautiful woman spoke in gentle words: "O King! Heart the cause of my sorrows.

प्रवंधीय महावाहो यदर्थ र्वती त्वाह्म ।

तर राजादान्येगे शगाजा परभधार्मिकः ॥ ३६ ॥

O King! Why will the people cry, to no purpose, unless calamities come before them? O Mighty-armed One! I now tell you why I am weeping.

भ्यो नाम महाराज: संतानरहितो भृभम् ।

तस्य भयंां सुविख्याता रुक्मरेडोत नामतः ॥ 37 ॥

O King! There was a very religious King named Rabhya in another province that is not yours. At first he had no issue. He had a very beautiful wife named Rukmarekhā.

सुरूपा चतुरा साध्वी सर्वलक्षणसंयुता ।

अपुत्रा दु:वित्ता कान्तमिव्युवारा वुन: पुन: ॥ 38 ॥

कें जीवितेन मे नाय धिन्युवा जीविते मय ।

वंध्याया: सुख्तीनाया हुप्रयुवा धरातले ॥ 39 ॥

She was clever, chaste and endowed with all auspicious qualities. But isuseless as she was, she remained very sorry and, in a remorseful tone, she spoke to her husband Raibhya: "O Lord! I am barren; I have no sons; I am therefore a very unhappy creature. My life is in vain; what use is there in my living?

इत्येवं भार्याय भूप प्रेरितो मख्युतमम् ।

चक्का ब्राह्मणांस्त्रानारुव विधिवतदा ॥ 40 ॥

When the queen thus spoke very distressedly, the king called the Brāhmaṇas, versed in the Vedas, and began to perform an excellent sacrificial ceremony, in due accordance with the Vedic rules.

With a desire to get a son, he made many presents in profuse quantities.

पुरकामो धर्म भूरि ददावध यथोदितम् ।

हूयमाने धृतेत्यर्थे पावकादितिसुपभत् ॥ 4 1 ॥

When copious quantities of ghee were offered as oblations, there arose, from the fire, a girl beautiful in all respects and endowed with all auspicious signs.

अविर्बंभूव चार्वीनी कन्यका शुभलक्षणा ।

विवोषीं सुदतीं सुधूः पूर्णचन्द्रिनिभानना ॥ 4 2 ॥

कनकाभा सुकेशाना तस्याणिता मुदुः ।

सुरकानयना तत्वी रक्तयादाला भुगम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

Her teeth were very nice, eyebrows very lovely, face enchanting like a Full Moon, the lustre of the body lovely and of a golden colour; her hairs were fine and curling, her lips like the Bimba flowers; her hands and face were of a red colour; her eyes were red like lotus and her limbs were soft and gentle.

हुतापानातस्पूद्धता होजा सा स्वीकृता ततः ।

होता प्रोवार याजनं गृहीत्या ता सुभ्यमान् ॥ 44 ॥

राज-न्यूजीं गृहाणेमां सर्वतक्षणास्युतान् ।

एकावलीव सभ्यन्तां हृदयानाद्युताशन्त् ॥ 45 ॥

When the girl arose from the fire, the priest (Hotā) took that lean and thin lady of a nice waist by her arms and presented her to the King and said: "O King! Accept this daughter, endowed with all auspicious signs.

नामनोकबाली लोक खलाता पूत्री भविष्यति ।

सुवित्तो भव भृपाल पुत्रा पुत्रसमानया ॥ 46 ॥

सन्तोयं कुर राजेन्द्र दत्ता देवेन विद्याना ।

होतुवांक्यं पुर शुक्षा युद्धता तो कन्यकों रुभणम् ॥ 47 ॥

When Homa was being performed, the daughter came up like the garland Ekāvalī; therefore this girl became famous in this world by the name Ekāvalī. O Ruler of the earth! Take this girl, resembling a son and be happy. O King! Visnu, the Deva of the Devas, has given you this Jewel, this daughter; so be contented."

जग्राह भयमीती होरा दुत्ता सुर्मंभतम् ।

गृहीरः नृपतिस्तां तु ददी पल्यै वरानानम् ॥ 48 ॥

अभाव्य रुक्मेरख्ये गृहाग सुभगे सुर्गतम् ।

सा तां कमलपत्राङ्गी प्राय कन्यां मनोरामम् ॥ 49 ॥

Hearing thus the words of the priest, the King saw this good looking girl and with gladdened heart took the beautiful daughter from his hands. Thus with that lovely daughter he went to his wife Rukmarekhā and said: "O Beautiful One! Take this daughter." The queen Rukmarekhā felt the pleasures of having a son when she got in her arms lotus eyed beautiful daughter.

जहर्षं पृदिता राजी पुरं प्राय्य यथा सुखम् ।

चकात मूब्तं कर्मे जातकर्मिटिकं शुभम् ॥ 50 ॥

The King next performed the natal and other ceremonies of the daughter and did all other acts as if she had been a son to him duly in accordance with the rules.

पुरजन्मस्मुर्नं यत्नसर्वे विधित्वन्तः ।

समाय च मखं राजा द्विजेष्यो दक्षिणा शुभाम् ॥ ५ १ ॥

The King performed his own sacrificial ceremonies and gave away lots of Dakṣinās to the Brāhmins and dismissed them and became very glad.

दत्वा विसृज्य विघे-त्रानूदं प्राप महीपति: ।

द्विते दिते सितापार्यो पुरुबढ्छ्वा घृष्टां बभौ ॥ 52 ॥

That beautiful girl was nursed and cared after like a son and she grew older day by day. The Queen Rukmarekha was very gladdened to get her. मुदं च परमां प्राप नृपभार्य सुतान्विता ।

उसवस्तिद्रेत तस्य प्रवृतः सुतन्यमनः ॥ 53 ॥

On that very day the birth day festival was performed as on the occasion of the birth of a son. And that daughter grew older, very affectionate and dear to all.

युनी पुत्रसमास्त्यर्वे बभूव बल्लभा किल ।

रातो मनिस्तुत चाहं सुखुदे मन्यधाकृते ॥ 54 ॥

O Lovely one! You are a king and intelligent too; I will describe to you all the details; Hear. I am the daughter of the minister to that King.

यगोतवी च मे नाम स्यानं वय आवयोः ।

वयय्याउं कृता राजा मीडिनाय तया सह ॥ 55 ॥

स्वत सहचरी जाता प्रेयुक्ति द्विताग्रियम् ।

My name is Yásovati. That daughter and I look alike and of the same age. Therefore the king has

made me her comrade. I spend my time day and night always with her as her constant dear companion.

एकावली गंधर्विति यत्र पचानि पश्यति ॥ 56 ॥

तत्र सा साते बाला नान्यस सुखमापुवात् ।

सुद्रें जाड्वीतीरे भवन्ति कमलान्यपि ॥ 57 ॥

स्मभाण तत्र याता मत्स्मिता सखीयुत ।

Ekāvalī likes very much to remain and sport wherever she finds sweet scented lotuses; at other places she does not find happiness. At the distant banks of the Ganges many lotuses grow; therefore Ekāvali goes there with great pleasure to that place with me and her other fellow mates.

मया निविदितं राज्यजी ते कमलाकान् ॥ 58 ॥

प्रेक्षमाणाउतितूरे सा प्रयाति निर्जिने बने ।

निषेधितास दयाजसी गृहे कृत्वा जलागयान् ॥ 59 ॥

One day I told the King that Ekāvali used to go daily to a distant solitary forest to see the lotus-lake. Then the King addressed her not to go and he got a lake built within the compounds of his palace and planted many lotus seeds therein.

कमातन् वापयित्वाश्व पुधितान्ध्रमरातुतान्।

तथापि निर्वीये बाला कमलास्तचतेना ॥ 60 ॥

Gradually the lotuses began to blossom and the bees came there to drink honey. Still she used to go outside in search of lotuses. Then the King sent armed guards to accompany her.

तदा राजा स्थापना: प्रेरिता: शरक्षापाय: ।

एवं स्थापता तन्बी मत्सेता सखीयुता ।

मौड़ाथँ जाइतीनेते विन्यमाठित याति च ॥ 61 ॥

इति श्रीद्वेभावते महापुराणे कथलकम्ये

एकविंशोराधयः ॥ 21 ॥ 11

Thus that thin-bodied daughter of the King used to go daily to the banks of the Ganges for play, guarded by armed soldiers, accompanied by myself and other companions. Again when the sporting was over, she used to return to the palace.

Here ends the Twenty-first Chapter on the Sixth Book on the installation of Ekavīra and the birth of Ekāvali in the Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevībhāgavatam by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book VI Chapter XXII

CHAPTER XXII

On Stealing away Ekāvali

यगोकल्याच

प्रातरूथाय नत्वंची चलिता च सखीयुता ।

चामरैंकीयमाना सा रक्षिता बहुरक्षिभि: ॥ 1 ॥

Yasovati spoke: “O King! One day Ekāvali got up early in the morning and went to the banks of the Ganges, accompanied by her companions; they began to fan her with a chowrie.

सायुघ्रोतिसन्द्रैः सहिता वरविणी ।

क्रीडार्थयन्र राजेन्द्र संग्रता नतिनीं शुभाम् ॥ 2 ॥

The armed guards accompanied her. Slowly she went where there were the lotuses in order to sport with them.

अहमध्यना सार्यें गंगातीरे समागता ।

अस्पोरिभः स्रोता च कमतेः क्रीकामाना ॥ 3 ॥

I, too, went with her playing with the lotuses to the banks of the Ganges and both of us began to play with lotuses with the Apsarās.

एकावली तथा चाहें जाते क्रीकापर यदा ।

सहसैय तद्धउद्यातो दानवो बलसयुंतः ॥ 4 ॥

कालकेनृतीति छात्रो राक्षसैर्वहुभियुंत: ।

परिचासिन्याचावपाणातोमरयाणिधा: ॥ 5 ॥

When both of us were deeply engaged in the play, then one powerful Dānava, named Kālāketu, came up there suddenly with many Rāksasas armed with parighas, swords, clubs, bows, arrows and tomaras and many other weapons.

इद्य चैकावली तेन रूपयीवनशणितिने ।

द्वितीया कामपत्नीव क्रीऊमाना सुपंकजैः ॥ 6 ॥

Ekāvali was playing with the best lotuses when Kalaketu saw her in that state, blooming with beauty and youth as if like Rati, the Goddess of Love.

मयोकैकावली राजकॉर्ज दैत्व: सम्पाततः ।

गछचोः रक्षपालानां मध्ये पढ़जलोचने ॥ ७ ॥

O King! I then spoke to Ekāvali: "Look! Who is this Daitya that has come here unexpectedly; O Lotus-eyed One! Let us go into the central part of our armed guards.

विमुर्षवें सडी चाऊँ त्वरवेग नते भयात् ।

मध्ये वे सैनिकानां तु सायुधानां गुणतयज ॥ ८ ॥

कालकेतुतु तां द्ववा गोहिनीं मदन्तुतः ।

गदां गुर्गी गृहीना तु धायमानः समगतः ॥ ९ ॥

रक्षकानूतः कृत्वा जग्राहुंबलोचनम् ।

त्रसां वेयुवंसुक्तां कैद्यानां कृणोदरीम् ॥ 10 ॥

O King! My companion and myself consulting thus, went out of fear immediately into the centre of the armed guards. Kālaketu was seized with the arrows of Cupid, and no sooner he looked at that beautiful young lady than he, with a very big club in his hand, hurriedly came to us, drove away the guards, and took away my lotus eyed companion, of thin waist. Then the young lady, helpless began to tremble and cried aloud.

लकेनो मां गृहाणैते मा चोक्तोर्धि दानवः ।

न मां जग्राह कामार्तैरां गृहीत्वा विनि:सुतः ॥ 11 ॥

Seeing this, I spoke to the Dānava: Leave her and take me. The passionate Dānava did not take me but he went away, taking my companion.

तिउ तितिउे घामोने रक्षकासां महाबलम् ।

प्रतिथध्य तु संग्रामं चकुर्गविस्यकाकरम् ॥ 12 ॥

The guards exclaimed: “Wait, wait; do’t fly away with the girl; we are giving you a good lesson.” Thus saying, they made the powerful Dānavas stop and both the parties engaged in a very terrible conflict, astounding to all.

त्ययापि राक्षसा: कृतः सर्वतः राक्षयापायः ।

युयुधू रक्षैकः सार्थं स्वामिकार्यं कृतोदायः ॥ 13 ॥

The followers of the Dānavas, more cruel and all fully armed began at once to fight with great enthusiasm for their Master's cause.

संग्रामस्तु तदा जातः कारणकेतोस्ताय गे।

निह्त्य रक्षकानसर्वानृहीत्वेनां महावलः ॥ 14 ॥ ॥

Kälaketu himself began to fight afterwards terribly and killed the guards. He, then, with his followers, carried away my companion towards his own city.

युक्तो राक्षससैयेन निर्जनाय पुरं प्रति ।

वीध्य तां कर्तीं बालां गृहीतां दानवेत नु ॥ 15 ॥

I, too, followed my companion, when I saw her thus carried away by the Dānava and crying out of fear.

पृछतोई गत तत यत्र नीता सखी माम ।

विकोर्गिती यथा सा मां पश्येतिि घतदानुगा ॥ 16 ॥

I also walked crying aloud by those tracks as would enable my Sakhi to see me. She, too, seeing me, became somewhat consoled.

साथी मागलां तीक्ष्य विक्रीलस्याध्यन्ता ।

गताउई तत्सयीपे तु तामाभाष्य पुन: पुनः ॥ 17 ॥

सा मां प्रायातितुःखात्तं सत्मयवेदसामुकूला ।

कप्टे गृहीत्वा मां भूप करोद भृपुगःश्रिता ॥ 18 ॥

Crying out repeatedly I approached her. She was already very distressed and when she saw me, she clasped me closely around my neck, perspiring and stunned and, becoming more distressed, cried aloud.

स मामाह कालकेतुः प्रीतिपूर्वमिदं वचः ।

समाधासय भीर्तं वं सहीं चझललोचन्त्य। ॥ १९ ॥

Kālaketu then shewed his liking for me and told that my quick-eyed companion was very afraid and that I might comfort her.

प्राप्तं ममाह नगरं देर्वलोकसमं प्रिये ।

दासोस्मि तब तथा हि कस्माकत्टसि कातग ।। २० ।।

कवयैनों सखी तेउघ सत्यथा भव सुलोचने ।

He told me thus: “O Dear! My city is like the Deva’s abodes; you vill soon be able to go there. From today I become your slave, bound by love. Do not cry thus distressedly; be comforted.” In these words he told me to comfort my dear companion.

इत्युक्क्वा मां सखी पार्थे सामायेप्य यथोत्तमे ॥ 21 ॥

जगाम तरसा दुह: पुरे स्वस्य मनोहरे ।

सै-येन महता युक्तः प्रफुल्वद्वनोबुकः ॥ 22 ॥

Thus speaking, that villain made both of us mount on the beautiful chariot and making us sit by his sides went gladly and quickly to his own beautiful palaces, followed by his army.

एकावली तथा मां च संस्थाध्य धवले गुहे ।

राक्षसानुहरक्षार्थी कल्यामास कोदिगः ॥ 23 ॥

That Demon placed both of us in a beautiful house white washed and mirror-like and kept hundreds and thousands of Rāksasas to watch and protect us.

दितीये दिवसे सेठ्य मनुपदाच रहो नृप ।

प्रबोधय सखी वालां गोचेंती विहातुगन् ॥ 24 ॥

On the second day he called me in private: “Your companion is very much distressed on the bereavement from her father and mother and is lamenting; make her understand and console her.” पत्नी मे भव सुशोणि सुखं भूंख्य वथेयितम् ।

राज्यं तद्वीर्यं चन्द्राये सेवकोंड़ं सदा तव ॥ 25 ॥

राज्यं तद्वीर्यं चन्द्राये सेवकोंड़ं सदा तव ॥ 25 ॥

He told me to speak the following words to my companion: "O One of beautiful hips! Be my wife and enjoy as you like. O One with a face beautiful like the Moon! This kingdom is yours; ever I am your obedient slave."

पुनरुक्ते मया वाय्वं श्रुवा तद्धारितं खतम् ।

नाहं क्षतप्रद्धि वक्तु त्वमेना कथय प्रभो ॥ 26 ॥

Hearing his unbearable harsh words I said: "O Lord! I will not be able to speak her these words, disagreeable to her. You better speak this yourself."

इतुक्ते चचने दुष्टो मदतक्षतामतमतः ।

उवाच विनयादोंत सर्वी क्षामोदरिं पित्रायू ॥ 27 ॥

क्रूगोदरी त्वया मन्यो निश्रिनोति तमोभारि ।

तेन मे दूरवं कानते हतं ते वर्गतां गतम् ॥ 28 ॥

तेनाहं तव दासोउदा कृतोउस्मीति विनिश्रयः ।

भज मां कामबाणेन पीषितं विवर्गं भृगम् ॥ 29 ॥

When I spoke thus, that wicked Dānava struck by the arrows of Cupid began to speak gently to my dear companion of thin belly, thus: "O Dear One! To day you have successfully cast on me the Vaśikarana Mantra (one of the Tāntrik processes by which a lover is made to come under subjection); O Beloved! Therefore it is that my heart is stolen and so much brought under your subjection; this has converted me into a veritable slave of yours; then know this as certain that I am your slave; O

Book VI Chapter XXII

Sweet One! I am very much troubled by the Cupid's arrows and I am semi-unconscious; therefore O Lean-bellied One! Worship me.

यौवनं याति रंभोच चछ्जलं दुर्णपं तथा ।

सफलं कुरु कल्याणि पर्निं मां परिस्पष्य च। ३० ॥

O One of beautiful thighs! This youth is a very rare and transient thing; O Auspicious One! Now embrace me as your husband and make your youth a veritable success."

एकावल्युवाच

पित्राड़ं कल्पिता पूर्व दातुं गरासुताय वै ।

हैहयस्टु महाभागः स मया मनसा सुत: ॥ 31 ॥

Ekāvali said: “O Fortunate One! My father wanted to hand me over to a prince named Haihaya; I also mentally adopted him as my husband.

क्यमन्यं भवे कान्तं लव्क्वा धर्मे सनातनम् ।

कन्यामर्ग विहाराव बेलिस शास्त्रविन्दिशुम् ॥ 32 ॥

You are certainly aware of the Śāstras; how can I now abandon the eternal religion and the virtue of a woman and take up another husband.

यस्मै द्वापिता कामं कन्या ते पतिमानुयात् ।

परन्ताना सदा कन्या न स्वतन्त्र कदाचन ॥ 33 ॥

The girl must accept him to whom the father betrothes. The girl is under every circumstances dependant.

इत्युक्तोप्रि तथा पापी विराराम न मोहितः ।

न मुमोच विशालाईं मां च पार्थित्यांत तथा ॥ 34 ॥

Never do they get any independence." Though Ekāvalī said thus, the vicious Daityas struck by Cupid's arrows, did not desist and did not leave me and that larged-eyed companion.

पातालविदः तस्य पुरं परमसूक्टे ।

तशसे रक्षितं दुर्गं मफित्तं परिषदायून् ॥ 35 ॥

His city lies in Pātāla and is a very dangerous place; always it is guarded by Rāksasas and surrounded by a moat; inside is built a beautiful and strong fort.

तत्र तिहिति दुःखातां सखी मे प्राणवल्लभा ।

तेनाहं विरोहाना रारतीमि सुखूःत्रिका ।। ३६ ॥।

Now my dear companion, the queen of my heart,

is staying there with a grievous heart and I am here wandering hither and thither very much troubled on account of her bereavements."

एकरीर तवाच

कर्थ ल्यम संप्राणा पुरातस्य तुरामनः ।

विस्ययो मे महान्न तत्वं बृहि वरानने ॥ 37 ॥

Ekavīra said: “O Beautiful-faced One! How have you been able to get away from the city of that wicked demon and how have you been able to come here? I am perfectly at my wit’s end.

त्वया च कथितं वाक्यं सदित्यं भाति भाविने ।

हैहयाथें कलित्या सा प्रियेति मन साम्यान् ॥ 38 ॥

Say quickly all these. O Proud One! I doubt your words; the father of your dear companion resolved to give his daughter to Haihaya in marriage; now I am that Haihaya.

हैहयो नाम राजार्जं नान्योर्जित पृथिवीपितः ।

मद्यं कथिता सा किं सखी तव सुलोचना ॥ 39 ॥

I am the King by that name, on this earth; there is no other King by the name of Haihaya. Is it that your dear companion is meant for me?

एतन्ये संगयं सुचु छेतुभाईस भाधिनि ।

अहं तानानिध्यामि तं हत्या राक्षसाधमम् ॥ 40 ॥

O Bhāminī (passionate woman)! Remove my doubts; I will kill that villain Rāksasa and bring just now your dear companion; there is no doubt in this.

स्वयं दर्शय मे नस्य यदि ज्ञानासि सुझते ।

राङे निवेदितं किं वा तत्विचे चातितुःखिता ॥ 4 1 ॥

O One of good vows! Shew me that place, if it be known to you. Has any body informed her father that She is suffering from so many troubles?

यस्यैष बल्लभ पुत्री न किं ज्ञानिते तां हेत्म।

नोष्टाम: किं कृतसेनैत ततो मोर्चहतेलें ॥ 42 ॥

Has her father come to know that her daughter has been stolen and carried away? And has he made my effort to rescue her from the hands of that villain Rāksasa?

वन्दीकृता सुर्गा ज्ञाला कयं तिहित सुखितः ।

असमर्थां नृप: किं वा कारणं बृहि सत्वम् ॥ 43 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

Is it that the King is calm and quiet, knowing that his daughter has been kept in prison? Or is it that he is unable to free her from bondage? Say quickly all these things before me.

स्वया प्रेपहतं चेते गुणानुक्नवा हामानुपान् ।

सख्या: पडूकपगक्षिक कृत: कामवशो भूषण् ॥ 44 ॥

O Lotus-eyed One! You have captivated my mind by describing the extraordinary qualities of your dear companion and have made me passionate too.

कटा पश्यामि तां कान्तां मोचवित्वाजित्सुक्रटत् ।

इति मे इदयं चाढ़ करोत्पतिमनोरध्य् ॥ 45 ॥

Alas! When will it be that I will free my beautiful beloved from the greatest perilous situation and shall see her face and her eyes, beaming with joy.

ब्रह्मे न पमानोपायं पुरे तस्मातितुर्णे ।

कर्थं त्वमाता तस्मात्स्कृटादर तद्धर ॥ 46 ॥

O Sweet speaking One! Say, by what means I can go to that impassable city. How have you been able to come from there?"

यशोत्पल्यबाच

बालभावान्यमा मज्नो भगवत्वा विशां पते ।

प्राणोस्तित ब्राह्मणातिस्स्द्यासकीज्यान्यपूर्विके ॥ ४७ ॥

Yasovatī said: “O King! In my early age I got the Mantram of the Devi Bhagavatī with its seed Mantram (mystic syllable involving in it the power connoted by the Devi) and how to meditate it.

तत्वास्थितया रात्रनयाय चित्ते विचारितम् ।

आराध्यायी सत्तं चपिडकां चचडविक्रमम् ॥ 48 ॥

While I was in the Dānava's place I thought out that juncture I would worship the powerful Caṇdikā who gives instantaneously one's own desires.

सा देही सेविता कामं बन्यमोक्षं कार्तियत ।

भक्तानुकाम्पी नार्फ्ति: समथाँ सर्वसाधने ॥ 49 ॥

If I worship that Śakti, That fructifies all desires, That is all mercy to Her Bhaktas, certainly She will free my dear companion from this her bondage.

या विश्व सुचने शक्या पालत्यवैल सा पुनः ।

कल्याने संहृत्येव सिनाकारा निराश्रया ॥ 5० ॥

Though She is really without form, yet She, without anybody's help, by Her own force, She is creating, preserving, and at the end of the Kalpa, destroying this Universe.

इति सझित्य मनसा देखी विश्रेष्ठशी शिवाय।।

ध्याता रक्ताध्वरों सौम्यां सुरुकन्यन्तां हुदि ॥ 51 ॥

संस्मृत्य मनसा रूपे मन्त्रजाययात्राभवम् ।

उपासिता मया देही मासमेकं स्माधित्य ॥ 52 ॥

Oh! She is very wonderful indeed! Thus thinking I began to meditate that auspicious red-robed and red-eyed Devi, the Lady of the Universe, and recollected mentally Her form and repeated silently Her Bijā Mantram.

स्वनेप मम समायात भक्तिभावेन तेणिता ।

भाग्रहभृता याचा किं सुताउसीति चिण्डका ॥ 53 ॥

उत्तिष याहि तरसा गज्ञातिरं मनोहाम् ।

आगमिष्यति ततासी हैहयो नृपयुगवः ॥ 54 ॥

When I meditated thus for merely one month. Candikā Devi became, through my devotion, manifest to me in my dreams and began to speak in sweet nectar-like words: "You are now asleep; go quickly to the beautiful banks of the Ganges.

एकबीरी महाबाहु: सर्वेशुविद्यदिनः ।

दत्तावेशेण मन्यनो महाविद्धाभियः परः ॥ 55 ॥

दन्तोदसैसोदी सततं मापुणसोडेनिषिततः ।

मध्यासक्तमितिंनंव मप पूजापरायण: ॥ 56 ॥

The enemy destroyer, the powerful Ekavira, the greatest of all the kings, will come there. Dattātreya, the Great Lord of the Munis, has given him my Mantra named Mahāvidyā; the King also constantly worships me devotedly with that.

माथेव सर्वभूतेषु ध्यायज्ञते च मत्यतः ।

स ते दुःखविनारां वै करीस्थिति महापतिः ॥ 57 ॥

मासुतो विहरंतत तव जाता भविष्यते ।

हत्वा तं ग्रासं घोरो भोविष्यति मानिनिथि ॥ 58 ॥

His mind is constantly attached to Me and he constantly worships Me. What more to say then this fact that the king, extremely to Me, meditates on Me as the internal controller of all beings. That intelligent son of Lakṣmī will come for sport to

Book VI Chapter XXIII

the banks of the Ganges and will remove all your sorrows.

एकावलमेकवरी: सर्वशाखविशारदः ।

पशुतार्वे पति: कार्यस्विया राजसुतः शुभः ॥ 59 ॥

इत्युक्चलांतदीये देवी प्रबुद्धार्ज तदेव हिं ।

क्रायितं स्वपन्तुत्तनं देल्याशूनार्यन्त तथा ॥ 60 ॥

That king Ekavira, versed in all the Sāstras will kill the Rāksasas in a dreadful battle and will rescue Ekāvali. So now you pay heed to my word. Lastly, She told me that her companion should marry that beautiful King, endowed with all auspicious qualifications." Thus saying, She disappeared and I instantly woke up.

प्रसत्रदान जाता श्रुत्वा सा कमलेक्षण।

विशेषेण च सन्तुहा मामुवाच शुचिस्ता ॥ 6 1 ॥

Then I informed my lotus-eyed dear Sakhi all the details of my dream as well my worshipping the Devi; hearing this, her lotus-face beamed with joy and gladness.

गच्छ तन त्यारयुना कुरु कार्य मम प्रिये ।

सत्यावल्या भगवती साउंवों मोक्षे विधायस्ति ॥ 62 ॥

That sweet-smiling Ekāvalī very gladly told me "O dear Companion! Go at once for our success."

That truth-speaking Bhagavatī Ambikā Devi will release us from our bondage.

इत्पाज्ञात तथा चाहे सख्या वे प्रेमयुक्त्या ।

मत्वोसरणं घुक्ते तस्मात्स्थानताद नृप ॥ 63 ॥

O King! When my dear companion ordered me thus, I thought it proper, as also dictated to me in my dream, to go out and soon I did it.

चालिताड़ः ततः शीर्घ माहतेवीप्रसादतः ।

भाग्रानं शीर्घगतिमघा प्राप्ता नृपात्मज ॥ 64 ॥

O King! Due to the grace of the Great Devi, I came to know the way and I also got the quick motion.

इत्येलक्रवितं सर्वे कारणं मम दुःखजम् ।

कसलं कस्य सुतश्रौति वद् वीर यद्य तथा ॥ 65 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पशत्कम्बे

श्राविशोश्याः ॥ 22 ॥

Thus I have described to you the cause of my sorrow. O Hero! Who are you, whose son are you? Speak truly to me.

Here ends the Twenty-second Chapter in the Sixth Book on the narration to Haihaya the stealing away of Ekavali in Srimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharsī Veda Vyāsa.

Book VI Chapter XXIII

CHAPTER XXIII

On the War Between Haihaya and Kālaketu

व्यास उवाच

तस्यास्तु वचनं श्रुत्वा रमापुत्र: प्रतपावान् ।

प्रकुल्ववदानीभोजरतामुावा विश्यामपे ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! That powerful son of Lakṣmī, Haihaya, became very glad to hear these words of Yāsovati and said:

राजोवाच

रामोह सर्वस्या पृष्टो वृत्तान्तो विशदाब्धर: ।

हेहयोर्दं चैकबौरिनामा सिन्धुसुतानुतः ॥ 2 ॥

“O One of beautiful thighs! Hear in reply to your query: I am Haihaya, the son of Laksmī, and I am known in this world by the name of Ekavīra.

मनो मे यच्च्या नूनं परतनंर कृताङ्कृते ।

किं कंरोमि क्व गच्छामि विरेशुणातिपीक्षितः ॥ 3 ॥

प्रथमं रूपमार्थातं सर्वैलोकातिरां त्वया ।

तेन मे विहृतं जातं कामबाणाहतं मनः ॥ 4 ॥

ततस्तस्या गुणा: प्रोक्तास्तैतु चित्तं हुतं पुनः ।

यत्व्योक्ते पुत्वाक्वयं तेन मे विम्वमोटभवत् ॥ 5 ॥

Now you have made my mind dependent. What am I to do now? where to go? Thus distressed with bereavement from your dear companion, my mind is struck with Cupid's arrows and is confounded with her extraordinary beauty that you just now described. Next your described her qualifications and my mind is ravished.

एकावल्या वचः प्रोक्ते दानवाठो मया वृतः ।

हैह्यसतं चिना नान्यं वृष्णोर्मीति विनिश्रयः ॥ 6 ॥

तेन वाक्येन तत्वांशि भूत्यो3हमधुना कृतः ।

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

Again when you described before me what she uttered in the presence of the Rākṣasa, I am struck with great wonder. Your dear companion Ekāvali said before the vicious Dānava Kālaketu “I have already selected the King Haihaya. I will not select any other than him, this is my firm resolve.” These words have converted me into her slave.

त्यया तस्याः चुकेशान्ते बृह्ि विक करवाणि याम ॥ ७ ॥

स्थानं तस्य न जानामि राक्षसस्य दुरात्मनः ।

O sweet-haired One! Say now that service can I do to you both? I am not acquainted with that wicked demon's palace; never I went to his city. गतिमों नास्ति गमने पुरे ताकिम्युलोचने ॥ ८ ॥

वद मां तव शिलागलिश तत्र प्रापयितुं क्षमा ।

O Fair-eyed One! Say how I can go there; for you are the only one that can lead me there. Therefore take me quickly to that place where your beautiful dear companion is staying."

प्राययाणु सखी ते सा यत्र तिहिति सुन्दरी ॥ ९ ॥

हत्वा तं राक्षसं कूरं मोचविष्यामी संग्रतम् ।

विश्वशो गोकसन्तंला रावसुघौत तव प्रियम् ॥ १० ॥

Your dear companion, the daughter of the King is very much afflicted with sorrow; soon I free her, by destroying that cruel Rāksasa. There is no doubt in this. O Auspicious One! I will rescue your dear companion and bring her to the city of yours and hand her over to her father.

विषुक्तुःखां कृत्वास्तु प्रापियथामि ते पुम् ।

पिंचे चास्या: प्रदास्यामि कन्याभेकावतीमहम् ॥ १ १ ॥

Then that King, the enemy destoryer, will perform the marriage ceremony of his daughter. I think this is the desire of your heart.

पश्रादिवार्हे कतिजसी राजा पुत्रमा: परन्तः ।

एवं ते मनसः कामो मम चापि प्रियवेदे ॥ १२ ॥

O Sweet-speaking One! Know that that is also my desire. O Beautiful One! Now that desire will be fulfilled by your efforts.

भविष्यति स सम्पूर्णे: साधनेन तवायुना ।

उपशयु गुरुं तस्य पश्य मे तंखे पशक्रमम् ॥ १३ ॥

Show me quickly that place and see my prowess.

O One with a face beautiful like the Moon! It seems that you will be able to do my work.

यथा हनिम दुराचारं परदायराहाकम् ।

तथा कुरु प्रिंवं कर्तु शक्तिसि वरवर्षिनि ॥ 14 ।

मार्ग दर्शय तस्याद पुरस्य दुरांमस्य च ।

Soon do such as I can kill that wicked demon, who steals others wives. Now show me the way to the impassable city of that Rākṣasa.

व्यास उवाच

तत्रिश्णा प्रियं वावयं पुदिता च यशोक्ती ॥ १ ५ ॥

तमुबाव चमापुत्रं गमनोपायमात्रत ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing the sweet words of the prince, Yásovati became very glad and gently began to speak out how he could go to the demon's city.

मद्रं गृहाण राजेन्द्र भगवतासु सिद्धिदम् ॥ 16 ॥

द्रास्थियाति सत्याप दुर्गे गछसपतिलनम् ।

“O King! Take the success-giving Mantra of Bhagavatī and I would then be able to show you today the city guarded by Rāksasas.

सज्जो भव महाभाग गमानय यमा सह ॥ 17 ॥

सैन्येन महता युक्तस्तय वृद्धे भविष्यति ।

कालकेनुर्महावीरो ग्रासैर्वतिभिश्रुतः ॥ 18 ॥

O King! Better arrange to take your vast army with you; for you will have to fight no sooner you go there. Kālaketu is personally a great warrior surrounded by Rākṣasas of great power and strength.

तस्मामन्तं गृहीत्वा तं बज तत्र मया सह ।

दूर्णशिवायामे ते माणे पुरस्यायव दुरातन्तः ॥ १९ ॥

Therefore be initiated in the Mantram of Śrī Bhagavatī and accompany me. So you will surely be successful. I will show you the way to the city of that Demon.

हत्वा तं पापकर्माणी मोचयागु च मे सखीम् ।

श्रुत्वा तद्वचनं वीरो मन्त्रप्रग्राह सत्यतः ॥ 20 ॥

द्नात्रयेहद्वयोगग्राप्ताज्ञानिवसाराख्यभात् ।

योगेश्वीमहाम्बनं जित्योकीतिलकाधियम् ॥ 21 ॥

तेन सर्वन्ता जाता सर्वान्तक्षारिता तथा ।

तया सह जगामायु पुरं तस्य सुदुर्मनम् ॥ 22 ॥

Book VI Chapter XXIII

Slay that vicious and vilest of the Rākṣasas and rescue my dear companion." Hearing thus, Haihaya was duly initiated into the great Mantram of Yogēsvari, named Trilokitilaka Mantra (Hrim Gauri Rudradayite Yogēsvari Hum Phat Svāhā is the Yogēsvari Mantra), by Maharṣi Dattātreya, accidentally come there (as if ordained by Fate), the chief of Jñāmins (the Gnostics), that is conducive to the welfare of the beings. Thus by the influence of the Mantram the King got the power of knowing all things and going everywhere with unobstructed speed.

रक्षितं राक्षसौधौर: पातालमिव पत्रगैः ।

यगोवत्वा च सैयेन मत्ता संसुत्तो युप: ॥ 23 ॥

Then the King Haihaya quickly went with Yaśvatī to the impassable city of the Rākṣasas, accompanied by a vast army.

तमायांतं समालोक्य दृतास्तव भयातुतः ।

क्रोगान्तोभिययु: पाशुं कालकेतोतस्त्रसिन ॥ 24 ॥

The city was surrounded by snakes and guarded by the terrible Raksasas like the city of Pātāla. The messengers of the Rāksasa, seeing the King coming, were struck with terror and crying aloud quickly went to Kālāketu.

तमुचु: सहसा गत्वा राक्षसं काममोहितम् ।

एकावलीसीपर्यक्ष कुर्बर्ते विव्यान्द्रह्न् ॥ 25 ॥

Kālāketu, struck with Cupid's arrows, was sitting beside Ekāvalī and was speaking many modest words when the messenger went there suddenly and said:

दूता क्युः

राजन् योगेवर्ती नारी कामिन्या: सड्घारिणी ।

आयाति सह सैन्येन राजपुत्रेण संयुता ॥ 26 ॥

“O King! The attendant of this lady Yasovatī is coming here with a prince and an army.

जयन्तो वा महाराज कार्तिकेयोदयवा नु किम् ।

आगच्छति बलोन्मन्तो वाहिणीसहितः किल ॥ 27 ॥

O King! We cannot tell exactly whether the prince is the son of Indra, named Jayanta or Kārikeya. After all, puffed up with the strength of his army, he is coming here.

संयतो भव राजेन्द्र संग्राम: समुपस्थितः ।

देवपुत्रेय युध्यस्य त्वज वा कमलेक्षणाम् ॥ 28 ॥

O King! The battle is imminent; now make your arrangements fully and carefully; fight with the son of a Deva or abandon this lotus-eyed Lady.

इतो दूरैं5ति सैन्यं तद्वीजनययाततः ।

सज्जो वब महीपाल दुर्बुधि घोषायगु चै ॥ 29 ॥

O King! At a distance of three Yojanas from this place, he is staying with his army. Now equip yourself and quickly declare the war by blowing the war trumps etc.

व्यास उवाच

तेषां तद्वचं भूत्वा राक्षसः क्रोभूम्त्रितुतः ।

राक्षसान्प्रेयामास सत्यान्त्यबलान्वडून् ।। ३० ॥

गच्छधं राक्षसा: सर्वे सम्युखा: गरक्षाणयः ।

तानाज्य कालकेतु: पूघछ प्रणायास्ति: ॥ ३। ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing the messenger's words, Kālāketu, the King of the Demons, became overwhelmed with anger and at once sent many powerful Rāksasas, holding all sorts of weapons and spoke out to them: "O Rāksasas! With weapons in your hands, go before them quickly."

एकावर्ती समीपत्थां विश्वगं भुगुत्:वितान् ।

कोउभयागित तत्वद्रिक्त पिता ते वापर: पुवान् ॥ 32 ॥

Ordering them thus, Kālāketu asked in sweet words Ekāvali who was in front and very distressed. O Thin-bellied One! Who is coming here? Is he your father or any other man coming with his army to release you. Speak this to me truly.

त्वदर्शे सैन्यसंयुक्तो बृहि सर्व कुशोवित् ।

पिता ते यदि सम्प्रायो नेतुं त्वां विरहातुः ॥ 33 ॥

जाला ते पित्तं सत्यबसंग्रामं न करोयहम् ।

आनस्तिया गृहे पुर्जा रनैवर्केक्षेईये: शुधे: ॥ 34 ॥

If your father comes here to take you back, being very much distressed with your bereavement, I will never fight with him, if I come to know this truly; rather I will bring him to my house and worship him with the excellent horses, gems and jewels and clothing. Really I will show my full hospitality duly to him when he comes here.

Śrīmaddevībhāgavatam

क्रौति तस्य चातिथ्यं गृहे प्राप्तस्य सर्वेथा ।

अन्येक्षोदि सम्पान्तलं हन्नि निशितः शरेः ॥ 35 ॥

And if any other person comes, then I will take his life by the sharpened arrows; there is no doubt in this. Know this as certain whoever comes here for your rescue is brought by the hand of Death to me.

आनीतः किकल काने परागया महात्मना ।

तस्माद्व विज्ञालाङ्कि कोउम्यागती मंदधीः ॥ ३६ ॥

अज़ाला मां दुराधर्ष काललर्भ महावल्न ।

Therefore, O Large-eyed One! Say who is this fool that is coming, not knowing me as the powerful and unconquerable Kāla (Death).”

एकावल्युवाच

न जानेड़ं महाभाग कोउयमायाति सत्वतः ॥ 37 ॥

न मेजिल विदितः कोर्पि सित्यात्यावत बच्चे ।

नयं पिता मे न प्राता कोउपयोगेस महावत: ॥ 38 ॥

किम्बधिमह घ्यातात नाहं वेद विनिश्रवम् ।

Ekāvali said: “O Highly Fortunate One! I do not know who is this coming to this side with a violent speed. O King! How can I know that when I am in this state of confinement in your house. This man is not my father nor my brother. Some other powerful man is coming here. I do not know exactly what for he is coming.

दैत्य उवाच

एव वदन्यमी दूता वयस्या ते यशोक्ति ॥ 39 ॥

समानीय च तं वीरामानतेित कृतोदाया ।

वव गता सा सखी कानते विद्यथा कार्यविशेषि ॥ 40 ॥

नान्यः कोउपि ममातिरीयं मे प्रतिवलो भवेत् ।

The Demon said: "My messengers say that your comrade Yaśovati has take with her that warrior and is coming to this side with great energy. Where has your clever companion gone now? O Lotus-eyed! There is no enemy in the three worlds strong enough to fight against me."

व्यास उवाच

तृलमिश्रजंते दृतास्तान्ये वै समानातः ॥ 4 1 ॥

ते हीबुल्साति शीता: कालकेतुं गृहे स्थितम् ।

किं सत्योर्जेसि महात्राज समीपे सैन्यमागतम् ॥ 4 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Just then other messengers hurriedly came there terrified and spoke to Kālāketu who had been staying in the house, thus: "O King! The army has come quite close to the city and how are you staying in the house, calm and quiet?

निरीच्छ नगरातूर्ण सैयेन महता वृतः ।

इति तेषां वचः श्रुत्वा कालकेतुर्महावलः ॥ ४३ ॥

Better march out of the city with your vast army as early as possible." The powerful Kālaketu, then, hearing their words, mounted on the chariot and quickly went out of his city.

यथामहु त्वत्ते निर्वीयं स्वपुराद्धि: ।

एकवीरोप्रि सहसा ह्यामकड़ः प्रतापवान् ॥ 44 ॥

The King Haihaya, on the other hand, suffering from the bereavements of his dear lady, suddenly came there mounted on horseback.

आगतस्तत कामी-या विशेषो समाकुलः ।

युढ़ं तवोर्भूतत्र चूत्रवासवयोरि ॥ 45 ॥

The terrible fight ensued then and there between the two and each one struck the other with sharpened weapons and the quarters all around blazed with their glitterings and clashings.

गस्तार्वेबंधा मुक्तैरादीपतिद्वानतम् ।

वर्तीमाने तदा युद्धे कातताणां भयावहे ॥ ४ ४ ॥

When the terrible fight was going on, Haihaya, the son of Lakṣmī, struck Kālaketu, the King of the Daityas with a very powerful club (Gadā).

गदादा ताउवामास दैर्वं सिन्धुसुतासुतः ।

स गतासुः पतातेल्यां वज्ञात हजावलः ॥ 47 ॥

Thus struck by the Gadā, the Lord of the Daityas fellon the ground like a mountain, struck by lightning, and died.

पतातिवासा ततः सर्वे गछसा भयपीडिताः ।

यशोवर्ती ततो गत्वा वेगादेकावर्ती ततं ॥ 48 ॥

उवाच मभुतं वाणी विश्वितां पुदिता भुगम् ।

All the Rāksasas fled away on all sides, struck with terror. Yásovati went then very hurriedly with a gladdened heart to Ekāvali and began to speak to her in terms of surprise and in sweet words:

एष्ठाति नृपपुत्रेण दानवोरभौ निपातितः ॥ 49 ॥

एकबीर्ण धीरेण युद्धं कृत्वा सुदारुभणम् ।

Book VI Chapter XXIII

O Dear! O Dear! Come, Come; the great warrior, the prince Ekavīra has killed the Lord of the Daityas in a dreadful battle. That King in now waiting, tired in the midst of his soldiers.

स्कन्यावोरप्रयौरा रावा निश्तवधा क्षमातृः ॥ ५० ॥

दार्णं कांक्षागाले क्षुतकपगुषतव ।

पश्य तं कुटिलापाक्रि मनोभयवसம் नृमम् ॥ ५१ ॥

He has already heard from me about your beauty and qualities; and now he is expecting to see you. O One Looking askance! Now satisfy your eyes and mind by seeing that King who is like the Cupid.

कथिता तं वयं मपूर्व तस्याएे जाइक्रवीते ।

पूर्णीगुराः संज्ञानतेनासी विश्रातुाः ॥ ५२ ॥

यांक्षित तां चारुकर्ण उद्धू नृपतिनन्दनः ।

When I described to him before on the banks of the Ganges your beauty and qualifications, he got enamoured of you and now he is suffering from bereavement and wants to see you.

सा तस्य वचनं शुचा गमनाय मनो दरे ॥ ५३ ॥

तज्जमाता भृणं भीत्या कोभागं प्रादया तथा ।

Thus, hearing, Ekāvali determined to go to him and as she yet unmarried, she became abashed and afraid.

कां तत्थ मुचंघे ऋष्ये कुमारी ह्वारगा भृगणे ॥ 54 ॥

स मां गृहुति कामार्ते इंति चितातुकला सती ।

She thought how could she see the prince as she was unmarried. It might be that be being passionate would catch her by her arms.

यगोवत्या युता तत्र नर्यान्तित्या ययों ॥ 55 ॥

कन्यावारोत्तिनालेना मलिनावस्थापिति ।

Thus, troubled with thought, that daughter of the King, with a sad look, and wearing poor clothes, Ekāvali went with Yaśovatī on a palanquin, carried on men's shoulders.

तामागतं विशालास्थीं इद्वा राजसुतेप्रबवीत् ॥ 56 ॥

रूर्नं देहि तन्विंग तृविंचे नवेचे मभ ।

Seeing that large-eyed daughter of the King coming there, the prince said: "O Beautiful One! My two eyes are very thirsty to see you. Satisfy my eyes and mind by shewing yourself to me.

कामातुरं च तं बीक्ष्य तां च लज्ञाभरावुतामू ॥ 57 ॥

नीतिजा शिष्यभाज्ञा तनुवाच यथोवती ।

राजपुर विपात्ययस्यावेयंतं दातुपिच्छति ॥ 58 ॥

एपारपि लद्धगा नूनं घविता संगमस्तव ।

Seeing the prince passionate and the King's daughter very much abashed, Yásovati, who knew the rules of modesty, thus spoke to the prince: "O Prince! The father of my dear companion expressed a desire to betroth her to your hands. She is also obedient to you. Therefore your meeting will certainly take place.

कालं प्रतीक्ष्य राजेन्र नयैनी चितृरतिनकम् ॥ 59 ॥

स विदाक्रियिकी कृत्वा दास्यतीति विदिक्षयः ।

O King! Wait; take her to her father; and he will perform duly the marriage ceremony and betroth her to your hands. Know this to be quite certain."

स तस्या वचनं तध्यं मत्वा सैन्यसमित्वतः ॥ 60 ॥

समेते कामिनीभ्यां तु ययौ तत्सितृताश्रमम् ।

The King took her words to be quite just and true and taking those two ladies went with his army to the house of the father of Ekāvalī.

राजपूर्वी तथायातां शुत्वा प्रेमसमित्वतः ॥ 61 ॥

प्रययौ सम्युवल्पूर्णं सचिवः परिवेशितः ।

Ekāvali's father became very glad and cheerful to learn that his daughter was coming and, accompanied by his ministers, went hurriedly to her. बहुभिविर्वसैनैंद्य तृत्री सा महिनान्वारा ॥ 62 ॥

यगोवत्या तु चुत्तान: कथितो विस्तारतुना: ।

After a long time the King saw his daughter in poor clothings and became highly pleased. Yašovatī then described in detail all what happened before the King.

एकवीरं मिलित्वादसौ रूहमानीय चादगत् ॥ 63 ॥

पुण्येऋढ़ कारयामास विवाहं विधिपूर्वकम् ।

The King then with his minister brought with great love, courtesy and gentleness Ekavira to his house and on all auspicious day performed the marriage ceremony of him with Ekāvalī, in accordance with due ceremonies and rites.

पारिषहू ततो दत्त्वा संपुज्य विधित्वतदा ॥ 64 ॥

पूर्वी विसर्जयामास यथोवत्या समवितात् ।

Then the King gave away many clothings, ornaments, jewels, and articles for fitting a house and many other things and worshipped duly and sent his daughter together with Yaśovatī away with the King Haihaya.

एवं विवाहे संवृते रमापुत्रो मुद्रावित्तः ॥ 65 ॥

पूर्ण प्राय० बद्धन्योगत्बुभुजे प्रियवा समम् ।

Thus the marriage ceremony was performed and the son of Laksmī gladly returned to his house and began to enjoy many pleasures with his wife. Then,

in course of time, in the womb of Ekāvali the King Haishaya got a son named Kṛtavírya.

बभूत तत्या: पुरस्तु कृतवीयांमिथ: कित।

तत्सुतः कार्तवीयांसृ वंगोउयं कथितो मया ॥ 66 ॥

इति श्रीमदैशीभागते महापुराणे पश्चकन्चे जयोविरोगे उध्याः पितः 123।

The son of this Kṛtavīrya is known as Kārtavīrya. O King! Thus I have narrated to you the origin of the Haihaya dynasty.

Here ends the Twenty-third Chapter in the Sixth Book on the battle of Haihaya and Kalaketu in the Mahā Purānam Śrimaddevibhāgavatam by Maharsi Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER XXIV

On Vikṣepa Śakti

राजोवाच

भार्वेरस्न्युखीभोजाध्छृते दिव्यक्रायरास्म ।

न तृत्यिमीयाच्छाम विश्वस्नु सुधया सम्म ॥ १ ॥

The King Janamejaya said: O Bhagavān! I am not satiated with the drink of the divine sweet nectar-like words coming out of your lotus mouth. विश्वसिमदायाखनं कथितं भवता मम् ।

हैहयानां समुपतिन्सितात्रम्यमप्रदा ॥ २ ॥

You have described to me in detail the wonderful and variegated story of the origin of the Haihaya dynasty; but, O Muni! There has arisen in my mind a curiosity to know something more on this subject.

परं कौतुहलं मेउत्र यदिषुणुः कमलापति: ।

देवदेवो जगाधावः सुहित्स्थ्यंतकाकाः ॥ ३ ॥

सोउपवर्षभावामपत्रो भगवाह्नरित्यन्तुः ।

परतनः कर्थ जातः स्वतनः पुरुषोतमः ॥ ४ ॥

See the Bhagavān Viṣṇu, the Lord of Lakṣmī, the Deva of the Devas, the Ruler of this whole Universe and the Cause of the Creation, Preservation and Destruction; yet that Best of Puruṣas Śrī Bhagavān had to assume a horse form. He is undecaying and independent, how then He came to be dependent?

एतन्ने संशयं बह्यउ्छेतुभहींस संग्रितम् ।

सर्वज्ञस्वं मुनिश्रेष्ठ बृहि वृत्तानमढुतम् ॥ 5 ॥

Clear this doubt of mine. O Best of Munis! You are omniscient; therefore satisfy my curiosity by describing this wonderful event.

व्यास उवाच

शृणु राजन्यवक्ष्यामि संदेश्यास्य निर्णयम् ।

तथा श्रुतं मया पूर्वं नारदामुनिसतमात् ॥ 6 ॥

बहाणो मानसः पुत्रो नारदो नाम तापसः ।

सर्वैत्रः सर्वेगः शान्तः सर्वैताकप्रियः क्रकिः ॥ 7 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hear what I heard of yore from Nārada how this doubt was removed. The mind born son of Brahmā, Maharṣi Nārada got powers to go everywhere by virtue of his Tapas, could know everything, was of a calm and quiet nature, dear to all and he was a poet.

स चैकदा मुनिश्रेष्ठी विचरन्युधिवीमिमम् ।

वाद्यन्महर्ती वीर्णां स्वरातनसम्यित्नम् ॥ 8 ॥

ब्रह्धधतरात्रीना सामानं घेदाननेकाः ।

गायनायश्रममुत संग्रातोउध मामाश्रमम् ॥ 9 ॥

On one occasion he went out on tour round the world, playing with his lute in time with Svara and Tāna. One day he came to my Āśrama, singing many things concerning Brhat Rathantara Sāma Veda and the sweet nectar-like Gāyatrī, the Giver of Liberation.

Book VI Chapter XXIV

शयाप्रासं महातीर्थं सरस्वल्या: सुपावनम् ।

निवासं मुनिमुख्यानां शर्मदं ज्ञानं तवा ॥ 10 ॥

O King! There was a very sacred place of hermitage, beaming as it were with happiness and self-knowledge, named Śamyāprāsa, on the banks of the river Sarasvatī. There was situated my hermitage.

तमागतमहं प्रेश्य बढ़ापुरं महावृतिम् ।

अध्य्यात्यार्दिकं सर्व कृत्वावन्चैराविकम् ॥ ११ ॥

Seeing the lustrous Nārada Rṣi, the son of the Grand Sire Brahmā, coming, I get up and offered him duly Pāḍya (water to wash his feet) and Argha (offerings of worship), etc., and worshipped him.

अध्य्याएविधि कृत्वा तस्यानन्थितस्य च ।

उपविष्ट: समीपेड़ं मुनेनरमितजेस: ॥ 12 ॥

दूष्वा विश्विमगं शान्ते नारतं ज्ञानपादम् ।

तमपुरुषहं राजन्यरुहोड़ं त्वायेघून ॥ 13 ॥

When that Muni of indomitable lustre took his seat on the Asana, I sat beside him. When I found Nārada, the Giver of Knowledge, at rest and quiet, I duly asked him the very same question that you have asked me just now.

असारेर्स्सितु संसारे प्राणिनां किं सुखं पुने ।

न पश्यामि विनिश्रित्य कदाचित्वकुचित्वस्थिति ॥ १४ ॥

O Best of Munis! What happiness is there on the beings taking their birth in this world. I never found it in any place or in any concern, this I can say positively.

ड्री पे जातो जनन्याहं संत्यक्तस्तक्षणादपि ।

अनाङ्गयो वने वृद्धि प्रापतः कर्मपुसारतः ॥ १५ ॥

Still why do the high minded persons do Karma, fascinated by the enchantments of the world. Look! I was born in an island.

तपस्तपं मया घोरां पवैते बहुवार्षिकम् ।

पुसकामेन देवर्षं शड़रः सपुपासितः ॥ १६ ॥

Just after my birth, my mother forsook me. Helpless, I grew in the forest as my Karma allowed. Next I performed a very severe tapasyā before Mahādeva, the Deva of the Devas, on the mountain with a dosire to have a son.

ततो मया शुक्र: प्राप्त: पुत्रे ज्ञानवतं चर: ।

पाठितसून मया सम्यवेदात्मां सार आदितः ॥ १ ७ ॥

As a fruit of that I got Suka as my son, the foremost of the Gnostics, and taught him completely the essence of the Vedas from the beginning to the end.

स त्यकता में गतः: क्वाथि मृन्ति विरहातुम् ।

लोकाल्लोकानांर साधो वचनातव जोधितः ॥ 18 ॥

O Devarsī! When my son got wisdom from you, he left this world even when I became very distressed on his bereavement and wept aloud and he went away to the next world.

ततोड़े नुवस्नतल्यवबा मेरे महापितिभि ।

मातरं मनसा कृत्वा संग्राम: कृकर्जानम् ॥ 19 ॥

पुत्रस्नेहादिततरां कृशांग: शोकसंयुतः ।

जाननिष्येति संसारं मायावागनियन्त्रितः ॥ 20 ॥

Very much afflicted for the parting of my son, I abandoned the great Mountain Meru. I got very lean due to the absence of my dear son whom I loved very much; and becoming very distressed and knowing this whole world to be an illusion, remembered my mother and went to the Kuru Jāṅgala district, as if bound up and controlled by the snares of Māyā.

तत्तो राजा वृतां जात्वा मातरं वासवीं शुभाम् ।

स्थितोउत्रैवाक्षमं कृत्वा सरस्वल्याप्रातटे शुभे ॥ 21 ॥

When I heard that the King Śāntanu had married my mother, I built my hermitage on the holy banks of the Sarasvati and remained there.

गैतनुः स्वर्गितं प्राणते विपुरा जननी स्थिता ।

पुर्वदययुत साखी धीभेण प्रतिपातिता ॥ 22 ॥

When the King Śāntanu went to the next world, my chaste mother remained with two sons. At that time Bhīṣma looked after their sustenance and maintained them.

चित्रांगदः कृती राजा गंगापुरेण धीमता ।

कालेन सोःपि मे प्राता मृतः कामसम्बृति: ॥ 23 ॥

The intelligent Ganga's son Bhīsma Deva installed Cītrāngāda on the throne. A short while after this, Cītrāngada, too, looking like a second

Cupid and extremely lovely, went to the jaws of death.

ततः सत्यवती माता निममना शोकासागर ।

चित्रानंद मृतं पुत्रं रूतेद भृशमातृना ॥ 24 ॥

The mother Satyavatī was drowned in the sorrows for his son Cittāṅgada and began to weep for him.

संग्राटोड़ महाभाग्र जातव तां दुःखितां सतीम् ।

आश्रासिता मायात्यर्षी भीभेण च महात्मना ॥ 25 ॥

विश्वसीर्यल्पणे वीर्वीवान्यधिवीपितः ।

कृतो भीभेण भाता वे क्षीराज्यविपुढ़न ॥ 26 ॥

O King! Knowing my mother in that sorrowful condition, I went to her. Bhīṣma and I then consoled her with hopeful words. Bhīṣma Deva was averse to marrying and then becoming a King; and, therefore, he installed again the younger brother, the powerful Victiravirya on the throne.

कारिज्ञानसुत रये विनियन पृथिव्योपतीन ।

भीम्पोनापीय स्वबलात्कन्यके इे समर्पिते ॥ २७ ॥

O King! Bhīṣma defeated by his own prowess the kings and brought the two daughters of the Kings Kāśtrāja and handed them over to Satyavati, so that she might give them over to Victiravīrya.

सत्यवर्यै रुषे काते विवाहः परिक्षित्रणः ।

श्रातूर्षित्वत्रीयस्य तताईं सुक्षितो3प्रभम् ॥ 28 ॥

Then, on an auspicious day, and in an auspicious Lagna (moment) when the marriage ceremony of my brother Victiravriya was performed, I became glad.

पुनः सोर्पि मुते प्राता यक्षणा पीक्षितो घृषणम् ।

अनपलो युवा धन्वी माता मे दुःखिताउभवत् ॥ 29 ॥

My brother, a good archer, was shortly afterwards attacked with consumption and thus he died without any issue.

कारीशरावसुरु वे तु मृतं इयूधा परित तदा ।

पतिन्तमाधमपैरे भागीयो संबंचपूवत्: ॥ 30 ॥

ते ऋचतुः सर्ती श्रशूं रुद्तीं भुणुः:खिताम् ।

पतिना सहगामित्यी भविष्यावो हुतागरे ॥ 31 ॥

पूत्रेण सह ते श्रशू स्वर्गे गतालअल नन्दने ।

सुखेन विहीएयावः पतिना सह संयुते ॥ 32 ॥

At this my mother became very sad and dejected. Seeing the husband dead, the two daughters of Kāsirāja became ready to preserve their religion of chastity and said to their mother-in-law, sorrowful and weeping: "We two shall accompany our husband and become Satī (i.e., burnt up with our husbands). O Devi! We will go to the Heaven with your son.

निवारिते तदा माता व्यक्षी तस्मानमहोद्यान् ।

स्नेहभावं समाश्रित्य भीभवस्य वचनात्ता ॥ 33 ॥

गांगेशेन च माता गे संमंत्र्य च परस्पत् ।

कृत्यीधर्वैहिक सर्व सैम्युतेई नगताछ्चे ॥ 34 ॥

We, the two sisters united, will enjoy with him in the Nandana Garden. The mother was very much attached to them and with the permission of Bhisma Deva, very affectionately made them desist from this great attempt.

स्मृतमास्त्रु मात्रा वै नात्वा भार्यं मनोगतम् ।

तत्सेवागतश्रहं नगरं नागसाढूमम् ॥ 35 ॥

When all the funeral obsequies of Victiravīrya were over, my mother consulted with Bhīsma and remembered me in Hastināangara.

प्रणाय्य मातर्ग मुन्नीं संश्वितोदय कृताङ्जसि: ।

तामबूदं सुतलांगी पूत्रगोकेन कर्जितगम् ॥ 36 ॥

मातत्स्वया किमाहुतो मनसाउं तपस्थिति ।

आजापय महत्वायं दासोर्पिम कवाणीय किम ॥ 37 ॥

As soon as she remembered me, immediately I knew her mental feelings and hurriedly came to Hastināṅagara and, with my head bowed, fell prostrate before her feet, and with folded hands addressed my mother who was very much inflamed with the fire of sorrow for the death of her son, thus: O Mother! Why have you called me here mentally? I see you are very much dejected; I am your servant; order me what I can do for you.

तब मे तीर्थे परं मातदेवघ प्रधित: परः ।

आगताश्रितितश्रात्र वृक्ति कृत्यं तव प्रियम् ॥ 38 ॥

O Mother! You are my greatest place of pilgrimage and you are my highest deity; I am very anxious since I have come here; say that what you desire.

Book VI Chapter XXIV

यास उवाच

इत्युक्स्वाहं स्थितस्तत मातुर्ये यदा मुने ।

तदा सा मामुवार्वेदे एप्यन्ती भीष्ममत्के ॥ 39 ॥

पुत्र तेउष घृतो घाता पीडित्ता राज्यक्षणा ।

तेनाहं दु:स्तिता जाता वंशच्छेदभ्यादित् ॥ 40 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O Best of Munis! When I said thus and waited before her, then she looked at Bhisma standing close by and said: “O Child! Your brother died of consumption; therefore I am very sorrowful, lest the family becomes extinct.”

तस्मावचघ मेधावियमातृतः स्माधिन ।

गांगेययय मतेनात्र गाराश्यर्धीसिद्धे ॥ 41 ॥

O Intelligent One! For the continuance of the line, then, with the permission of the Ganga's son, I have called you here today by the Samādhi Yoga. कुर्व स्थापय नहं तं शनतोनीभकारणात् ।

स्म मं दुःखतः कृष्ण यंशछेदोद्ध्वादुद्रतुम् ॥ 42 ॥

O son of Parāśara! you re-establish the name of Śāntanu that is going now to be well nigh extinct.

काशिराजसुते धार्ये धातुस्तव यदीयसः ।

साधोर्विश्वशिर्यसि रूपशैवनभूषिति ॥ 43 ॥

O Vyāsa Deva! Relieve me soon from this sorrow of mine, lest this line be extinct. There are the two daughters of Kāśirāja, honest and good and endowed with youth and beauty.

ताध्यां संगम्य मेधाविन्युजीर्यादनकं कुक ।

तब तंब भारतं वर्णन तारयेघोर्जित कहिंचित् ॥ 44 ॥

O Highly Intelligent One! Better you cohabit with them and save the family of Bharata by begetting sons. You will not be touched with any sin.

व्यास उवाच

इति मातुर्विः: श्रुत्वा जाताश्रितानुते हाहम्।

लज्जाया३कुलचितस्तावम्बूवं चिन्याततः ॥ 4 5 ॥

मात: पापात्रिकं कर्म परादतारमिश्मनम् ।

ज्ञाला धार्मियं सम्यककारीम कक्षमादगत् ॥ 46 ॥

तथा ययीयसो प्राणवर्णु: कन्या प्रक्रक्रितिता ।

व्यधिचारं कंकं कुयामधीत्य निर्मानान्हम् ॥ 47 ॥

Vyāsa said: O Devarṣi! Hearing the mother's

words, I became very anxious and humbly told her with great shame: "O Mother! To touch another's wife is a very sinful act; knowing well the path of Dharma, how can I willingly and intentionally violate that?

अन्यायेन न कतैन्वं सर्वेथा कुलतर्शणम् ।

न तरतिन हि संसारातिवस्तः पापाकारिणः ॥ 48 ॥

So also, the Maharsis say: That the wife of a younger brother is like a daughter. Studying all the Vedas, how can I do this blame-worthy and adulterous act?

लेकानामुपदेष्टा यः पुराणानां प्रवर्तैकः ।

स कर्ष कृत्तिसं कर्म मूला कुर्णम्हाद्धून ॥ 49 ॥

पुनरुक्तो हाहं मात्रा कट्टवा भृग्यान्तिके ।

पुत्रशोकांतिततपाया वैराश्रणकाकायया ॥ 50 ॥

To preserve a line of family by illegal ways is never to be done; for then the fathers of the sinners can never cross this ocean of world. How can he, who is the spiritual preceptor of all, and the writer of all the Purāṇas, do this act knowingly which is awfully strange and very bad and nasty in its nature.

पारास्थरं न ते दोशो वचनान्मय पुत्रक ।

गुरुणां वचनं तथ्यं सदोषमपि मानवः ॥ 51 ॥

My mother was very much plunged into the sea of sorrows for the breavement of her son; so to preserve the family, She came again to me, weeping and said: "O son of Parāśara! If you follow my words, you wo'nnt incur any sin.

कर्तेब्यमविचार्यैंव शिष्टाचारप्रमाणतः ।

वचनं कुक मे पुत्र न ते दोषोजित मानच ॥ 52 ॥

O Child! If the reasonable words of the Gurus be even faulty, one should obey them according to the tradition of the Śiṣṭas. Therefore, O Child! Keep my word and preserve my honour; no sin will touch you.

पुत्रस्य जनने कृत्वा सुखितीं कुक माताम् ।

विशेषेण तु संततां मन्त्रं शोक्षाणघे सुते ॥ 53 ॥

O Child! Think very well. Your mother is very sorry and is immersed in the ocean of afflictions; therefore it is your paramount duty to make her

happy by begetting child for the continuance of the family."

इति तां द्ववर्ती शुचा तदा सुरवर्तीसुतः ।

मामुवाच विशेषेच: सूक्ष्यधर्म्य सिपर्ये ॥ 554 ॥

द्वीपायन विचारोउन न कत्व्यस्त्वयानउन ।

मातुर्वंचनमादाय विहरस्य यथासुखम् ॥ 55 ॥

Hearing my mother speaking to me thus, Bhisma, the Ganga's son, the expert in finding out truth in fine points with regard to Dharma, said to me: "O Dvaipāyana! You are wholly sinless; you ought not therefore to argue on this point; obey your mother as she says and be happy."

व्यास उवाच

इति तत्य वचः शुत्वा मातुश प्रार्थनं तथा ।

नि:शंकोई तत जातः कार्यं तस्मिक्युपियिति ॥ 56 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing his words and my mother's request, I decided to do this very hateful act with a fearless heart without any suspicious.

अधिकवायां प्रवृतो3हयुत्मत्वां मुदा निशि ।

परिव विमनसायां तु तापसे कृत्तिसे घूर्णम् ॥ 57 ॥

ग्राला मया सा सुक्षोणी प्रसंगे प्रथमे तथा ।

संश्रसे भविता पुत्रे यतते नेत्रे निर्मितले ॥ 58 ॥

When Ambikā finished her ablutions after menstruation, I gladly cohabited with her in the

night; but that young lady seeing my ugly ascetic form, was not attachment to me; I then cursed that beautiful woman thus: "As you closed your eyes at the first cohabitation with me, your son will be born blind."

हितीये है मुनिश्रेष्ठ पुर्यो मात्रा रह: पुनः ।

भविष्यति सुतः पुःः काशिरासुस्तोदे ॥ ५९ ॥

मयोक्ता जननी तत श्रीदायमपुंल वह ।

विनेजो भविता पुत्रो मातः शापान्मेव है ॥ 60 ॥

O Muni! On the second day my mother enquired me when I was alone: "O Dvaipāyana! Will there be born a son of the daughter of Kāśrāja? I then bowed my head with shame, and told "Mother! The son will be born blind, through my curse."

तया निर्भीत्सतरन कठोरवचसा मुने ।

कर्थ पुत्र त्वया ग्रपः पुत्रसेउयो भविष्यति ॥ 61 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे श्वरक्ये

वर्गविंशोअष्ण: 11 24 11

O Muni! The mother then rebuked me harshly "O Child! Why did you curse that the son of Ambikā would be born blind?"

Here ends the Twenty-fourth Chapter in the Sixth Book on the description of Vikṣepa Śakti in the

discourse between Vyāsa and Nārada in the

Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000

verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XXV

On the Cause of Moha of Vyāsa

व्यास उवाच

वासवी चकिता जाता श्रुत्वा मे वाक्यमीद्वुभम् ।

दार्गेयी मगुवावेदे पुत्रार्थं भृगमातुगा ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The mother became astonished to hear me. Becoming very anxious for a son, she began to speak to me.

अवालिका वर्ध्यूंट्या काशिराजसुता सुत ।

भार्य विवित्रवैर्य स्थित्य शोकसंयुता ॥ 2 ॥

सर्वतक्षणासम्पत्रा रूपयौवनशालिनी ।

तत्वां जनय सई चं कृत्वा पुत्रं सुसमतम् ॥ 3 ॥

नांशो राजाधिकारी स्थानसमातूर्ध मनोहम ।

उत्पाद्य राजपुत्रां वचनान्मम मानद ॥ 4 ॥

O Child! The wife of your brother, the daughter Ambālika of Kāśtrāja, is a widow; she is very sorrowful; she is endowed with all auspicious signs and endowed with all good qualities; better cohabit with that beautiful young wife and get a child according to the tradition of the Śiṣṭas. Persons born blind are not entitled to kingdoms.

इसुच्छोई तदा मात्रा स्थितस्तत्र गजाछ्द्रे ।

यावदुतुमती जाता काशिराजसुता मुने ॥ ५ ॥

एकान्ते शयनागारे प्राप्ता सा मम सशिधौ ।

लज्जमाना सुकेशाना स्वश्रुवचनात्दा ॥ ६ ॥

Therefore take my word and procreate a

Book VI Chapter XXV

beautiful son and thus keep my honour." O Muni! Hearing the mother's words, I began to wait in Hastināpura till Ambālīkā, the daughter of Kāśirāja, finished her ablutions after menstruation.

दूद्या मां जटितं दान्तं तापसं सर्वर्जिन् ।

सा येवेदवदना जाता पणडुरा विमना भृगम् ॥ 7 ॥

That King's daughter, of curling hair, came to me alone at her mother-in-law's order, and became very much abashed.

कुणितोर्दं तदा दृष्टवा कामिनीं निशि सङ्गताम् ।

वेपमानां स्थितां पार्थं हाइबृहं तामहं रूपा ॥ ८ ॥

Seeing me an ascetic with matted hair on my head and void of every love sentiments, perspiration came on her face; her body turned pale and her mind void of any love towards me.

सूद्यात मां यदि नशेप पाण्डुवर्णी समावृता ।

अतस्ते तनय: पाण्डुर्भक्तिष्यति सुभत्येम ॥ 9 ॥

When I saw that lady trembling and pale beside me, I angrily spoke: "O One of beautiful waist! When you have turned out pale, considering your own beauty, let your son be of a pale colour."

इयुत्क्वा निति नैव विश्वो वातिकया युत: ।

भुक्वा तान्धिणि निवीतः स्थानामुच्छ्य मातम्न ॥ 10 ॥

Thus saying 1 spent there that night with Ambalikā. After enjoying her I took farewell from my mother and went to my place.

तत्सात्मयां सुती काले प्रमुतावस्थापाढुते ।

धृतग्रह्ध पाण्ड़ुश प्रथिती सव्यभूतुः ॥ 11 ॥

In due course, the two daughters of the King gave birth to two sons respectively, one blind and the other pale. The son of Ambikā was named Dhratāṣṭra; and the son of Ambālīkā was named Pāndu, as his colour was Pāndu (pale).

भाता में विमना जाता तदूगी बीक्ष्य ती सुती ।

ततः संवलस्यन्ते मामादूय तद़ोड़बबीत् ॥ १२ ॥

द्वीपाय सुती जाती राज्योयोनी न तादूशी ।

अन्यं मनोहरं पुत्रं समुत्पादय मे प्रियम् ॥ १३ ॥

Mother became absent-minded when she saw the two sons in those states. After one year she again called me and said: "O Dvaipāyana! These

two persons are not so fit to become kings; therefore beget one more son beautiful and according to my liking.

तथैति सा मया प्रोक्ता सुविता जननी तदा ।

अधिकको प्रार्थयामास सुतार्थें काल आगते ॥ 14 ॥

पुत्रि व्यासं समालील्य पुत्रमुचादयादूनम् ।

कुर्वेगस्प्य कतारं राज्यवोर्यं वारानने ॥ 15 ॥

When I consented, she became very glad and, in due course, asked Ambikā to embrace me and give birth to a son, endowed with extraordinary qualities, and fit to preserve the line worthy of the Kuru dynasty.

वधूर्तैज्ञान्यिता किंचिजोवाच वचन्ते तदा ।

गतोर्डं शयनागरे मातुलद्धचनारक्षिति ॥ १ ६ ॥

दासी विधित्रवीर्यस्य रूपयोवनसंयुता ।

प्रेशिताधिकया त्वत्र विधिचामशारावा ॥ १ ७ ॥

चन्दनात्कदेहा सा पुष्पमाताविष्यपिता ।

The bride did not then say anything on account of her bashfulness. But when I went in the night time according to my mother's order, to the sleeping room, Ambikā sent to me a maid-servant of Victiravīrya, full of youth and beauty, and adorned with various ornaments and clothings.

अयाता हावसंयुक्ता सुकेशी हंसगामिनी ॥ 18 ॥

पर्येक मां समावेश्य संस्थिता प्रेमसंयुक्ता ।

That maid-servant of beautiful hairs and of a swan-like gait adorned with garlands and red sandal paste, came to me with many enchanting gestures and making me take my seat on the cot, became herself merged in love sentiments.

प्रसोजईत त्वा तत्या दिलातेनाभवं सुने ॥ १९ ॥

राजी संकीक्षिति प्रेणा तथा सह मया भृशम् ।

O Muni! I became pleased with her gestures and amorous sports and passed the night, full of love towards her and played and cohabited with her.

वरो दस्त: पुनस्तस्ये प्रसजेन तु नारद ॥ 20 ॥

सुभगे भविता पुत्र: सर्वलक्ष्मणसंयुतः ।

सुकूप: सर्वेधमेंत्र: सत्यवादी रामे रतः ॥ 21 ॥

At last I gladly gave her the boon “O Fortunate

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

One! Your child, begotten by me, will be endowed with all good qualities, will be of good form, will be conversant with all the essences of Dharma, calm and quiet and truthful."

स तदा विदुरो जातक्रयः पुत्रा: सहाप्रवन्।

माया बुद्धि गता साधो परक्षेत्रद्वे मा 122 ॥

In due course, a child named Vidura was born to her. Thus I had three sons; and in my mind grew up Māyā and affection that these were my sons."

विस्मूतः शुकसम्बन्धी विगहः शोककारणम्।

दूद्वा तीन्यवसुतान्मार्य वीरान्य वैवीर्यममान्न्। 23 ॥

When I saw again those three sons, heroic and full of manliness, the only cause of my sorrow due to the bereavement of my son Śuka vanished away from my mind.

माया बलवती बह्दान्स्यजा हुकृतात्यभि: ।

अरूषा च निरालबा ज्ञानिनामपि मोहिनी ॥ 24 ॥

O Lord of Dvijas! Māyā is very powerful and extremely hard to be abandoned by those who are not masters of their senses; She enchants even the wise, though She does not possess any form nor any substratum nor any support.

मातिरे स्नेहस्वब्द्रं तथा पुत्रेषु स्यन्तुम् ।

न मे चिन्तं वने शान्तिणामानुविवत्तम ॥ 25 ॥

द्रोतारूढं मनो जातं क्वाधिद्धस्तिपुरे ।

पुनः सरस्वतीरिं न चैकत्र व्यवस्थिति: ॥ 26 ॥

I could not find any peace, even in the forest, as my mind was attached to my mother and children. O Muni! My mind then began to oscillate like a pendulum and I remained sometime in Hastināpura and sometime on the banks of the Sarasvati. I could not stay in a certain fixed place.

कट्यार्थिविर्तरव्यज्ञानं मानसे प्रतिभाषिति वे ।

कै.प्री पुत्र: क्व मोहोर्धेनं न श्राद्राहीं पुत्थये मे ॥ 27 ॥

व्यभियोद्वा: कि वे सुखदा: स्य सुना: किल ।

माया बलवती मोहं चितानोते हि मानसे ॥ 28 ॥

By discrimination, the knowledge sometimes flashed in my mind: "Whose sons are these? The attachment is nothing by merely a delusion. On my death they would not be entitled to perform

my śrāddha ceremony. There sons are begotten by ways and manners not sanctioned by Dharma; what happiness can they bring to me? O Muni! The powerful Māyā has caused this delusion in me.

जान्यमोहीधकूपेप्रियम्यतितोर्नं मृषा मुने ।

इत्यकुर्वं रहस्तार्यं कदार्थिन्युसमाहितः ॥ 29 ॥

What! Knowing this Sarinśara to be unreal. Alas! I have fallen into this well of Darkness of delusion." Thus I, repented when I thought over the matter deeply and when I was alone in a solitary place.

राज्य प्राप ततः पाणुबंलवार्यीध्यमयमतः ।

तदा मम मनो जातं प्रसर्नं सुताकरणात् ॥ 30 ॥

When, subsequently, through the mediation of Bhīsma, the powerful Pāndu got the kingdom, I became pleased to see the prosperity of my son.

कुन्ती माड़ी सुरुले ईपार्यँ तस्य बध्वतुः ।

शूरसेनसुता कुन्ती मूर्ताजसुतापरा ॥ 31 ॥

O Muni! This is also the creation of Māyā. The daughter of the King Śūrasena, named Kuntī, and the daughter to the King of Madra, named Mādrī became the two beautiful wives of Pāndu.

स शर्षे द्विजतः प्राय्य कामिनीहुवर्मसुतः ।

पाण्डुर्निवैद्याप्रास्त्यक्त्वा राज्यं वर्ग नतः ॥ 32 ॥

Pāndu was cursed by a Brāhmana that he would die if he cohabited with any woman; he therefore became dispassionate and quitting his kingdom, went to the forest with his two wives.

तदा मामाविशण्डोकः कुत्वा पुरं वने चैक्षतम् ।

गतोर्ड्र तत्र यजासौ मायांध्यां सह संस्थितः ॥ 33 ॥

तमाश्राय्य वने पाण्डुं पुनः प्राप्तो गजाड़्ये ।

धृतराष्ट्रं समाभाध्य ह्रागयं ब्रह्माजातटे ॥ 34 ॥

Hearing Pandu gone to the forest I felt pain and went to my son who was staying his wives and consoling him, came to Hastināpura, where I held a conversation with Dhrāraśstra and then came back to the banks of the river Sarasvatī.

क्षेत्रजान्यब्रुधुओन्स समुत्पाठ बन्धासे ।

धर्मती वायुतः शक्तादिष्यां पड़ पोडवान् ॥ 35 ॥

युधिष्ठिरो भीमसेन्नताधिवाजून इत्यपि ।

कुन्हीपुत्रा: समाख्याता धर्मानिलसुरीश्वा: ॥ 36 ॥

Book VI Chapter XXV

नकुतः सहदेवक्ष मद्राराजस्तुासुती । कदाचितुः रोहो मार्जी समालिंगय महीपाति: । 37 ।। मृतः शपालु मुनिधिः संरकृतो हतनुष्टयुपे । मार्जी तत्र सती भूत्वा प्रयिष्ठा पतिना सह ।। 38 ।।

Pāndu in his forest life, got five sons of his wives by the Devas Dharma, Vāyu, Indra, and the twin Aśvins. Dharma, Vāyu, and Indra begat respectively of Kunti the three sons Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhimasena and Arjuna; and the two Aśvins begat of Mādri the two sons Nakula and Saha deva. Once Mādri, full of youth and beauty, was staying alone in a solitary place and Pāndu seeing her embraced her and due to the curse, died. When the funeral pyre was ablaze, the chaste Mādri entered into the fire and died a Satī. Kunti was prevented from doing so, as she was to nurse and look after her young children.

स्थिता पुरयुता कुन्ती ज्वलिते जातवेदिस ।

मृनयः सुतसंयुक्तां गुरूसेनसुत्तां तदा ॥ 39 ॥

दुःखितां परिकहींना तामानिन्युर्गजसाछ्ये ।

समर्पिताशं श्रीभाय विद्युराय महात्मने ॥ 40 ॥

The Munis then took the sorrowful Kunti, the daughter of Śūrasena, bereft of her husband to Hastināpur and handed her over to the high-souled Bhismā and Vidura.

शूलाड़ं सुखदुःखाथ्यां पीडोतस्तु परातमिथः ।

भीभेण पालिताः पुत्रा: भण्डोर्शिति विविध्त ते ॥ 4 1 ॥

When I came to hear this, my mind was greatly agitated to see the pain and pleasure that other people suffered. Bhisma, Vidura, and Dhrtarāstra began to nourish and support Yudhiṣthira and others as they considered them the sons of their dearest Pāndu.

विद्युने तथा प्रीत्या धृतताष्ट्रण धीमता ।

दुर्घैधनादयलत्थय पुत्रा चे कृमानसाः ॥ 42 ॥

एकत्र स्थितिमाप्ता विरोधो चकुन्धादूनम् ।

ग्रेणाचार्यैस्तु सम्प्राप्तस्तर चीभेण मानितः ॥ 43 ॥

The cruel and wicked sons of Dhrtaārāstra, Duryodhana and others united with each other and began to quarrel horribly with the sons of Pāndu

Dronācārya came there accidentally and Bhīṣma treated him with great respect and requested him to stay in Hastināpura and educate the sons of Kuru.

अध्यापनाय पुत्राणां पुरे तत्मिश्रवासितः ।

कर्ण: कृत्वा परित्यन्तो जातमानः शिश्युद्वी ॥ 44 ॥

Karna was the son of Kunti, when she was young and unmarried; and he was quitted by her no sooner he was born.

सूतेन पालितो नदां प्राप्तशाधिर्येन ह ।

दुर्योधनप्रियक्षापूर्कर्णः शूरतमस्तथा ॥ 45 ॥

परस्यं विरोधेप्रधूदीमदुर्वीधनादिषु ।

धृतराष्ट्र सर्जित्य क्लेशं पुरेषु तेषु च ॥ 46 ॥

The charioteer Sūta (or carpenter) Adhiratha found him in a river and nourished him. Kārpa was the foremost of the heroes and therefore the great favourite of Duryodhana. The enmity between Bhima and Duryodhana, etc., began to grow greater day by day.

निवासं कल्यमावास भणद्धानां महात्मानम् ।

वितोधशमानयैव नगरे वारणावते ॥ 47 ॥

Dhrtarāstra, thinking the difficult situation of his children, fixed the residence of the sons of Pāndu at the Vāranāvata city so that the quarrels might die away.

दुर्योधिनेन तत्रैव ग्रैहाज्जतुगृहाणि वै ।

कारितानि च दिव्यानि प्रेष्म मित्रं प्रोचनम् ॥ 48 ॥

Out of enmity, Duryodhana ordered his dear friend Purocana to build there a house of lac for the Pānḍavas.

श्रुत्वा जतुगृहे दर्शन्याण्डवान्युथया सुतान् ।

पौत्रभावान्युनिश्रेष्ठ मनोर्धं व्यसनार्वे ॥ ४१ ॥

शोक्तातुरे भृगं शून्ये चने पश्यत्रहिंशम् ।

दृहा मदैकचक्रायां पाश्वदा दुःखकर्शितः ॥ ५० ॥

O Muni! When I heard that Kunti and her five sons were burnt in the lac-house, I became merged in the ocean of sorrows and thought that they were my grandsons. I was overwhelmed with sorrow and began to search after them in deep forests day and night till at last I found them in Ekacakrā city, lean and thin and very much distressed with sorrow.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

ततस्तुष्टमनाश्रां जातः पार्थपिल्लोक्य च ।

प्रेरितात्मे मया तृर्णं द्वाद्यस्य तृरं प्रति ॥ 51 ॥

ते नातास्तत्र दुःखातां विग्रवेषधारः कृशाः ।

मृगचर्मपरीधानाः सभायां संस्थित्यात्त्व ॥ 52 ॥

I became very glad to see them and sent them soon to the city of the King Drupada. Wearing the deer's skin, they went there dejected with sorrow in the Brāhmīn's dress and stayed in the royal court.

कृत्वा पराक्रमे विज्युः स जिल्या दुर्बदामजाम् ।

चकूर्विवहां मानिया पड्वेय मातृबालयच: ॥ 53 ॥

The victorious Arjuna shewed prowess and pierced the mark (the eye of the fish) and obtained Kṛṣṇā, the daughter of the King Drupada. By the order of the mother Kunti, the five brothers married her. O Muni! I became very glad to see that they were all married.

दृष्टवा विवाहें तेषां तु मुदितोड़ं पूर्णं तव ।

ततो नागाद्धे प्रापता: याझात्यीसहिता मुने ॥ ५४ ॥

निवासं खाणवप्रस्थं धृतारोषुण कल्पयतम् ।

याणबजानां द्विजश्रेय वासुदेवसुतेन वै ॥ ५५ ॥

The Pāndavas, then, accompanied by Pāncālī, soon went to Hastināpura. Dhṛtarāṣṭra then fixed Khāndavaprastha as the residence of the Pāndavas. Viṣṇu, the son of Vasudeva, then performed the Yajña with the victorious Arjuna and satisfied the Great Fire.

ताँपित: पावकसत विध्युना सह जिध्युना ।

राजसूय: कृती यजस्तदारंडं मुहितो5पवन् ॥ 56 ॥

टूद्वात्रध विषयवे तेणा तथा मवकृतां सभाम् ।

दुयोंधनोर्जितसंतणे दुरोदरमथाकरोह् ॥ 57 ॥

The Pānḍavas next performed the Rājasūya sacrifice and that made me very glad. Seeing the affluence and prosperity of the Pānḍavas and the great assembly hall beautiful and exquisitely artistic, Duryodhana was burnt up, as it were, with malice and made arrangements for play in dice, very injurious in its consequences. Šakuni was expert in playing deceitfully and Yudhiṣṭhira the son of Dharma, was not expert in this play.

दुर्बूध्वेती शकुनिनरक्षण्डश धर्मंकः ।

हर्त राज्य धर्म सर्व याजसेनी च कलेर्षिता ॥ 58 ॥

So Duryodhana made, Śakuni play for him and stole away all that Yudhīsthira had and insulted, at last, in the royal assembly, the daughter of Drupada, Yājñaseñ and gave her much trouble.

वने झटारा वर्षिण याण्डवासले विवासिताः ।

पाङ्गालीसहितासने दुःखं मे जनितं भृणम् ॥ 59 ॥

एवं नारद संसारे सुखदुःखात्मके भृणम् ।

निम्नभोर्हं प्रभेषीव जानन्यमं सनातनम् ॥ 60 ॥

The Pāndavas then went with Pāncālī in an exile in the forest for twelve years. And I was very much grieved to hear this. O Muni! Though I know all about the Sanātana Dharma, yet I was deluded and merged in these worlds of pains and pleasures.

कोड़ं कस्य सुलासेधी का माता किं सुखं पुः।

येन मे इदयं मोहाद्धमतीति दिवानिगम. ॥ 6 1 ॥

Who am I? To whom do these sons belong? My mind roams day and night on the thought of all these. O Muni! What shall I do?

कि क्रोगि कव गच्छामि सन्तोषो नाथिच्छिति ।

दोलात्छं मनो मेउर चझतं न स्थिरं भवेत् ॥ 62 ॥

And whether shall I go? I don't find happiness anywhere; my mind is, as it were, floating in a rocking machine and it is never being fixed.

सर्वैतोरिस मुनिश्रेष्ठ सन्देहं मे निवर्तय ।

तथा कुरु सधारई स्वां सुखितो विनतर्जच: ॥ 63 ॥

इति शीमदैवीभागवते महापुराणे फधरकन्चे

पद्धिशोधयाव: 11 25 11

O Best of Munis! You are all-knowing; solve my doubts so that my mental fever may be quietened and I may be happy.

Here ends the Twenty-fifth Chapter on the

cause of Moha of Vyāsa Deva asked

before Nārada in Śrimaddevibhāgavatam

of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi

Veda Vyāsa.

Book VI Chapter XXVI

CHAPTER XXVI

On Nārada's Moha

व्यास उवाच

इति वे वचनं श्रुत्वा नारदः परमार्थिति ।

मागह च सितं कृत्वा पृष्ठन्तं मोहकारणम् ॥ १ ॥

नारद उवाच

पाराशयं प्राणात्र किं पृष्ठसि मुनिश्रयम् ।

संसारोप्रस्मित्विना मोहं कॉपि नास्ति शरीतच्न ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When I asked him why this delusion overtook me, Maharṣi Nārada smiled and said: “O son of Parāśara! You are thoroughly acquainted with all the Purāṇas. Why then are you making this question about the cause of my Moha (delusion).

बह्ना विष्णुस्तथा रुद्र: सनकः कपिलस्तथा ।

म.यया वेहिता: सर्वे भ्रमित भववर्तमैति ॥ 3 ॥

No embodied soul an exist in this Sarīśāra without this Moha. Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Rudra, and the other Devas, Śaṅkara, Kapila and the other Rṣis, all these are surrounded by Māyā and are thus travelling in this path of Sarīśāra.

ज्ञानिनं मां जनो वेति प्रोतोर्भई सर्वीलोकवत् ।

श्रुणु मे पूर्ववृत्तानं प्रवतीम पुनीश्रितान् ॥ 4 ॥

दुःखं मया यथा पूर्वन्नुप्तां महत्तम् ।

स्वकृतेन च मोहेन भायर्वीं वासवीसुत् ॥ 5 ॥

The people know me as a Jñāni; but I, too, am deluded like an ordinary man. I am now speaking to you as certain as anything my of previous history now. I was deluded by Māyā; hear it attentively. O Son of Vāsavī! Great troubles and pains felt by me before, due to this Moha, for my wife.

एकादा पर्वतशहं देवलोकामहीतालम् ।

प्राणो विलोकनायीय भारतं खण्डुगतम् ॥ 6 ॥
