One day Parvata and I, the two Devarşis, went out together from the Devaloka to see the excellent portion of the earth named Bhārata and came to the Mrtyuloka or the land of the mortals.

भ्रमती सहितल्यों पर्यन्ती तीर्थमण्डलम् ।

पावानिन च स्थानानि मुनीनामाप्रियाम्ब्छुम्। ७ ७ ॥

गर्पथं देवलोकानु कृत्वा पूर्व परस्यम् ।

चलिती समयं चेयं समपन्य निश्छयेन वे ॥ ८ ॥

We then began to travel over various places and saw the places of pilgrimages and the holy places and the beautiful hermitages of the Munis.

चितचूतिस्तुं वनक्या याकुरी यस्य जायते ।

गृभा वारप्रत्युभा वार्थि न गोलद्या कवाचन ॥ 9 ॥

Before we went out from the Devaloka, we consulted with each other and entered into this agreement that we would not hide our feelings from each other, whether they be good or bad, while we would travel over the face of the earth.

भोजनेच्छा धेपेच्छाउपि रतीच्छा वा भवेदेपि ।

यादृषी यस्य चित्ते तु ककवीरा परस्पतम् ॥ 10 ॥

Whether it be our desire to get food, or wealth or women for enjoyment, whatever arisen in the mind of any of us, we would express that freely amongst ourselves.

इच्चावां समयं कृत्वा स्वार्वभूलेकामगताः ।

एकवित्ती मुनीभूर्ती विचारन्ती यवेच्छ्वा ॥ 1 1 ॥

Thus making an agreement, we went out in right earnest as Munis to travel over the face of this earth.

एवं भ्रमनी लोकेंस्मिनीभाने सुभगगते ।

संजस्यय पुरं रत्यं समप्राणो नुपतेः पुनः ॥ 12 ॥

Thus roaming all over the face of the earth, at the end of the summer season, when the rainy season commenced we came to the beautiful city of the King named Sañjaya.

तेन सभ्यक्ती नक्षता गजा सभ्यातीय भृणम् ।

स्थिती तत्र गृहे तस्य चातुर्मार्थं महातपनः ॥ 13 ॥

The King showed us great respect and worshipped us with devotion. Since then we remained for four months at his house.

वार्षिकाशुतो मासा तुर्गमाः पथि सर्वेता ।

तस्पादेकत्र विवृष्टिः स्थानत्यमिति निश्रयः ॥ 14 ॥

अष्टी मासास्तु प्रवसेत्स्वा कार्यविश्रादुज्ञः ।

वर्षाकाले न गतन्व प्रवासे सुरुखमिछ्छता ॥ 15 ॥

During the four months of the rainy season, the roads are always almost impassable; it is, therefore, wise to stay at one place. For eight months, the Dvijas should always remain abroad on some work or other. Thinking all these, we two began to stay in the house of the King Sañjaya.

इति सखिल्य मनसा संजयस्य गृहे तथा ।

संस्थिती प्रातिरा त्राता कृतातिथ्यो महान्मान ॥ १ ६ ॥

दययन्तीत विश्व्याता तस्य पुत्री महीपतेः ।

आज्ञाता परिचार्यां सुत्तरी सुन्दरी सुन्दरी ॥ ७ ॥

That liberal-minded King gladly and with respect kept us as his guests and tendered to us all our requirements. The King had a very beautiful daughter named Damayanti, with good teeth. The King ordered her take care of us.

विवेकज्ञ शिवगताक्षी राजपुत्री कृतोघाम ।

सेवनं सर्वकाले च व्यद्धाप्रभयोरि ॥ १ १ ॥

That large-eyed princess, of great discrimination, was very energetic, day and night. She began to serve both of us.

स्नानार्थमूदंक काले भोजनं मृष्टमायतम् ।

मुखबासंलथा चान्यं यदिहे तद्वराति सा ॥ १९ ॥

In due time she gave us water for our bath, excellent meat, food, towels for cleaning and rubbing our faces, in fact, everything what we desired.

मनोप्रभिलिताकामानुभयोरिप कन्यका ।

व्यज्ञासरण्याद्वीराष्टिक्तानप्यक्क्यन्त ॥ 20 ॥

एवं संसेव्यमानौ तु स्थितौ राज्ञो गृहे किल ।

वेदात्ययवनसंगीलावायां वेदवते रती ॥ 21 ॥

She kept ready for us whatever we desired, fans, seats, beds, whatever were necessary for us. Thus she began to serve. We were also engaged in the study of our Vedas and in those practises that were approved by the Vedas.

अई वीर्णा को कृत्वा साधयित्या स्वोत्तमम् ।

गायनं साम सुस्वादमगां कर्णरासायनम् ॥ 22 ॥

O Dvaipāyana! I used to sing, then, with lute in my hands, the sweet lovely Sāma Gāyatri songs in tunes and good Svaras.

राजपुरी तु तच्छूला सामगानं मनोहरम् ।

बधूव मयि रागाखा प्रीतियुक्ता शिवगरद ॥ 23 ॥

The princess herself appreciated the songs and when she heard these Sāma songs ravishing to one's mind, she became attached to me and showed signs of affection.

दिने दिनेउनुगानोङ्सा मयि बुद्धिं गत:  परः ।

ममापि प्रीतितुक्तायां मनो जातं सृष्टापरम् ॥ 24 ॥

Day by day the attachment towards me grew stronger. Seeing her attached to me, my mind also became attached to her.

मम तस्य च सा कन्या भोजनाविषु कर्हिंघत् ।

अकरीदंतरं किंचित्सोभमेदं रसाचिता ॥ 25 ॥

Thus that princess indulged in amorous sentiments towards me and began to make slight distinctions between the food and other things offered to me and Parvata.

सनानायोष्जलं महां पर्वताय च शीतलम् ।

दधि महां तथा तके पर्वतायावकल्प्यत् ॥ 26 ॥

I got water for my bath and Parvata used to get cold water; I got nice curds when food was served to me whereas Parvata got only whey.

शयानस्तरणं शुभं मदर्थे पर्वकल्प्यत् ।

प्रीत्या परमया घट्वल्विताय न ताडूगम् ॥ 27 ॥

विलोक्यिति मां प्रेणा सुन्दरी न च पर्वतम् ॥

ततोउस्यासतादृषुं दृष्ट्या पर्वतः प्रेमकारणम् ॥ 28 ॥

I got nice white bedding for myself to sleep on whereas Parvata had merely a dirty sheet to lie down. Thus the princess began to serve me with great love and devotion but not so she served Parvata. The fair lady began to look at me with eyes of love; not so towards Parvata.

मनसा चिंतयामास किमेत्तिविति वियमतः ।

पप्रच्छ मां रहः सम्यवृष्टि नारद सर्वथा ॥ 29 ॥

Parvata was very much surprised to see all this and thought within herself “What is this?” Parvata, then, asked me in private: “O Nārada! Speak out to me truly in detail.

राजपुत्री त्वयि प्रेम क्रांतीि मुदिता भृणम् ।

द्वाति भृध्यभोज्यानि रेनोहयुक्ता समंततः ॥ 30 ॥

Book VI Chapter XXVI

The princess shews with much gladness and affection her deep love towards you; she serves you with dainty dishes but she behaves not so with me.

न तथा मायि भेघोउस नस्टेहं जनयत्समी ।

मन्ते त्वां पत्ति कर्तुं सर्वथा संजयात्मजा ॥ 3 1 ॥

I therefore suspect when I see all these distinctions made between you and me, that the daughter of the King Sañjaya wants with her heart and soul to make you her husband. And you also want to make her your wife.

तवापि तादूर्ग भावं जानामि लक्षणैरदूम् ।

नेत्रवक्स्विकार्तैश्ञा ज्ञावे त्रिजितकारणम् ॥ 32 ॥

I have come to know this by signs and symptoms; for affection and love reigning inside can be made out by outward expressions of eyes and face.

सत्यं बद न ते मिथ्यां वत्क्यं च्चनं मुने ।

स्वर्गतः समयं कृत्वा चक्तिलों संस्मारामुना ॥ 33 ॥

Whatever this be, O Muni! Now speak truly to me; do never tell a lie. When we went out from the Heavens, we made out that agreement; now remember that."

नारद उवाच

पृड़ोर्नं पर्वितेनैं कारणं तु हठाधवा ।

तदारई हौस्माकान्तः संजानेश्रावूब पुनः ॥ ३४ ॥

पर्वतैँना विगालासी पति मां कांपुशुधात ।

मभापि मानसो भावो वर्तितेस्वा विशेषतः ॥ ३५ ॥

Nārada said: Thus questioned suddenly by Parvata, I became very much abashed and said: "O Parvata! This large-eyed princess ready to marry me and I am also very much attracted towards her."

तच्छुला वचनं सत्यं पर्वतः कोपसंयुतः ।

मामुवाच मुविवर्ध विधिपिति पुनः पुनः ॥ 36 ॥

प्रथमं शपथान्कृत्वा वश्छितोड़े त्वया वतः ।

भव वान्तवक्रसन्तं शापाच्छ मम मिवमुक् ॥ 37 ॥

When Parvata heard all these, he became very much angry and uttered repeatedly “Fie! O Nārada! Fie O Nārada! First you swore on oath and then

you deceived me afterwards. Therefore, O Deceiver of friends! I curse you and let your face become that of a monkey."

इति शलस्तु तेनाई कुपितेन महात्मना ।

सहसा हृषये कृरः शाखामुभ्युखस्तदा ॥ 38 ॥

When the high souled Parvata cursed thus. the face turned immediately into that of a monkey, elongated and distorted.

मयासपि न कृता तस्मिन्मा तु भपीनीसुते ।

सोसि शलोजिकोबघौं मा स्यतं ते नाहि: किनत ॥ 39 ॥

I did not excuse him, though he was my sister's son. I also got angry and cursed him "Certainly, your journey to the Heavens will be stopped. You will not be able to go to Heaven).

स्वल्पेउपरोघे यम्यानां शतावानसि पर्वत ।

तस्मातवीय मन्दाम्मानुतोके स्थिति: किन ।। 40 ॥

O Parvata! When you cursed me so heavily for so trivial a fault of mine, I see you are very mean. Whatever it be, you will have to remain on earth so long."

पर्वतैनतु गततस्माङ्गरगाईमना धृगम् ।

अहं वान्तवक्सन्तु संज्ञाततक्षणादपि ॥ 4 1 ॥

At this Parvata became very sad and went out of the city. My face became immediately like that of a monkey.

दूर्धा में वानरं कूरं राजपुत्री विलक्षणा ।

विम्नान्तीव संज्ञाता वीणात्रशवणालासा ॥ 4 2 ॥

The daughter of the King became very sorry to see my face thus distorted into that of a monkey. I did not see her glad as she was before; but her desire to hear my playing with my lute remained the same as before.

व्यास उवाच

ततः किमभवद्वह्म-कर्थ शपो निवर्तितः ।

मानुगाय्य: पुजान्जोत भवान् हिं विधाविधि ॥ 4 3 ॥

पर्वतः क्व गतो भूयः सुझगो युवयोग्यत् ।

कदा पुत्र कर्थं सर्वे विस्तरेण बदस्य ह ॥ 44 ॥

Vyāsa said: O Muni! What happened next? How did you get yourself rid of your curse and how did you get your man like face? Whither did Parvata

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

Rṣi go! When and how did you again re-unite with each other? Kindly describe all these to me in detail."

नात्व उवाच

क्रिं क्रवीम महापाग मायायाश्ररितं महत् ।

दू:खितोई भूर्ग तत पवैते क्षिते गते ॥ 45 ॥

Nārada said: “O Highly Intelligent One! What shall I say about the nature of Māyā?

पुनः सेवापरास्वर्ष राज्ञुरी मामभवत् ।

गतेउथ पवैते कामं श्वित्तसत्रैव सचानि ॥ 46 ॥

When Parvata went away angrily, the daughter of the King began to serve me with greater care than before.

उर्दे दुःखात्रितो दीनतसथा वानरवमुख: ।

विशेषेण तु चिन्तातैः किं मे स्यादिति चिन्तयन ॥ 47 ॥

I remained there, though Parvata went away, and seeing my face monkey-like, I became very dejected and sorry and was specially troubled with the care and anxiety would happen to me hereafter?

संव्योद्ध सुत्तां ईद्या किंचित्यकद्योवनान् ।

विद्याहर्षे राजसुतामपूच्छत्थित्वे तदा ॥ 48 ॥

The King Sañjaya saw that his daughter Damayanti was slipping into her youth and asked the prime minister about her marriage.

विद्याहाकलः संग्रावः सुताया मम संग्रान् ।

वोरयं वरं मम बृहि राजपूत्रं सुसंतमन् ॥ 49 ॥

He said: “The time of marriage of my dear daughter has now come; I will now marry her in accordance with due rites and ceremonies.

रूपौदार्युर्णीयुक्ते शूरं सुकुलसंभवम् ।

विद्याई विधित्यानुतः कंरामे किल सामानम् ॥ 50 ॥

Now tell me particularly about a prince worthy of her, as we like, in beauty, qualifications, large-heartedness, calmness, patience and heroism and who is of a good family.

प्रधानस्वबजीराजन राजपुत्रा हुनेकशः ।

वर्तैते पृवि पुत्रासोे योग्यः सर्वगृणात्थिता ॥ 5 1 ॥

The minister said: "O King! There are many princes on the face of this earth, worthy in all respects, of your daughter.

थस्मिनुचिते राजेन्द्र तमाद्धय नृपात्मजम् ।

देहि कन्यां धनं भूरि हलत्यश्यत्संतुलम् ॥ 52 ॥

Whomever you like, you can call on him and give him your daughter with elephants, horses, chariots wealth, gems and jewels."

नारद जवाब

वितृछिकर्षितं ज्ञाना दयवंती तदा नृपम् ।

धात्या मुखेन वाक्यजा तुमुलाच रहः स्थितम् ॥ 53 ॥

Damayanti, knowing the intention of his father informed the King of her own desire by her nurse and attendant.

धात्युबाच

दमवनी महाराज पुत्री ते मामधाब्बीत् ।

पित्रं बृहि धारेयि वचनाने मुखाल्यित् ॥ 54 ॥

मया वृतोशं मेघावी नारदो महतीयुतः ।

नादधोहितया कामा नान्यः कोर्पि प्रियो मम ॥ 55 ॥

कुरु मे वार्षिक्तं तत्त विद्याईं मुनिना सह ।

नान्यं वरिष्ये धर्मत्र नरतं तु पतिं जिता ॥ 56 ॥

The nurse went to the King and said: "When my father will sit at his ease and comfort you would go and speak to him in private that I am enchanted with the enchanting Nāda sound of the great lute played by Maharṣi Nārada and selected him as my bridegroom. No other person will be dear to me. O Father! Marry me with Nārada and thus fulfil my desire; O Knower of Dharma! I wo'n't marry anybody but Nārada.

मरानाई नादिसंधी वै नकहीने रसात्मके ।

अक्षरे सुख्यमपूर्णे तिमिंगतिवर्जिति ॥ 57 ॥

इति श्रीमदैवीभगवाने महापुराणे भरत्कन्धे वर्धिजीशोअधावः 126 ।

O Father! I am now merged in the Nāda-occan (sound ocean) of bliss, sweet and joyful, void of anything destructive of happiness, void of Nakra, alligators, and fishes, Timingala, etc. (injurious animals) and without any salty taste; my mind won't be satisfied with any other thing.

Here ends the Twenty sixth Chapter of the Sixth Book on the description by Nārada of his own Moha in the Mahāpurānam Śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book VI Chapter XXVII

CHAPTER XXVII

On Nārada's Marriage

नारद उवाच

तत्पूर्यां वचनं श्रुच्या राजा धार्मीमुखाततः ।

भार्यां प्रीवाच कैकैकीं समीपरथ्यां सुलोचनाम् ॥ १ ॥

राजोबाच

यदुक्ते वचनं कानते धाय्या तत्पुं त्वया श्रुतम् ।

दृतोर्ज नारदः कामं मुनिवर्नारवक्रवभाक् ॥ २ ॥

Nārada said: On hearing these words of her daughter from her nurse, the King addressed the queen Kaikeyī, of lovely eyes, standing close by, thus: “Have you heard what the nurse has said? Damayantī has mentally chosen the monkey-faced Nārada as her husband.

किसिदं दितितं पुर्या बुद्धिद्वीन विवेष्टितम् ।

कथमस्मै मया देरा कन्या हरिमुखाय सा ॥ ३ ॥

व्यासौ चिक्षुः कुरूपः कव दायवनी मामताया ।

विपरीतिमंद कार्य न विधेयं कदाचन ॥ ४ ॥

What has she thought? Whatever it be, it is no doubt, an act of great foolishness. His face is monkey-like; how can I betroth my daughter to him? Where is an ugly beggar Nārada? And where is my daughter Damayanti? The marriage between them is quite unjust; never it should take place.

तामेकाने सुकेशाने निवारय हदासुतान् ।

युक्ना युनिरतां मुभ्यां शास्त्रवृद्धानुसारया ॥ 5 ॥

O Beautiful One of good hairs! Better call her before you in private and shew her reasons approved of the Śāstras and of the aged persons and make her desist from such a rash course."

इति भनुरंचः शुत्वा जननी तामधावयित् ।

वन ते रूपं मुनि: वक्तावी नावतस्यो5धन: पुनः १६ ॥

कर्मे गोहमवालाओंसि चिशुक्रे चतुग पुनः ।

On hearing her husband's words, the mother of Damayanti called her in private and said: "O Child! Where is your this beautiful face? And where is the monkey-like face of Nārada? You are smart

and quick; how have you been, then, deluded by such a Moha?

लताकोमलदोहा त्वं भयस्कक्षतनुस्थयम् ॥ 7 ॥

वातां वानरवक्रेण कर्थं युक्ता तत्वानघे ।

का प्रीति: कुलिते पुंस भविष्यति शृचिभिते ॥ 8 ॥

O Child! You are the daughter of a king! Your body is gentle like a creeper. And Nārada always besmears his body with ashes; so his body is very rough.

वरस्ते राजपुओंस्तु मा कुरू त्वं घावा हुवम् ।

चिता ते दुःख्यामोति श्रुता धार्मीयुखाद्धचः ॥ 9 ॥

O Spotless One! How will you change your words with him? Why do you shew your attachment to an ugly person? What pleasure do you feel thereby?

लनां बबूलचुळेण कोमालां मालतीलाताम ।

दूर्वा कथम मनः खेदं चतुरस्य न पच्छति ॥ 10 ॥

You would be married to a beautiful prince; never follow this rash course; your father is very sorry to hear these from your nurse.

दारसेंकाय ताम्बुतीदालानि कोमालानि कः ।

ददाति भ्रभणाधय मूर्खोंपि धरणीतले ॥ 11 ॥

O One of soft body! Judge this yourself, what intelligent man is there that is not sorry at the soft Málatí creeper entwining a thorny tree? Even a stupid silly man would never feed a camel, that likes thorns, with soft betel-leaves.

बीक्ष्य त्वां कारसंलनां नारदस्य समीपतः ।

विवाहे वर्तमाने तु क्य चेते न द्वहिति ॥ १२ ॥

कुमुळेउ समं वातां न रचिं जनयन्ततः ।

आमरणातु कंब कालाः शपित्वल्यलाजमुना ॥ 13 ॥

When your marriage time arrives, say yourself, who will not be sorry to see you going to Nārada and embracing him by his arms! Nobody likes to speak with an ugly-faced one; how will you be able to spend your time with him till you death!"

<table><tr><td>नारद उवाच</td></tr><tr><td>इति मातुर्विः: शुत्वा दयवन्ती भुशातुगा ।</td></tr><tr><td>मार्गे प्राह तन्त्र्वीं माय सा कृतनिद्धया ॥ 14 ॥</td></tr></table>

Nārada said: On hearing the mother's words, the gentle Damayanti, with her mind intently fixed on me, spoke to her mother, very much depressed in her spirits.

किं मुखेन च रूपेण मूर्दीय च धनेन किम ।

किं राज्येनाविदध्यस्य रसमागचिदोउस्य च ॥ 15 ॥

“O Mother! What good face and beautiful form will avail, who is not in the path of love and who is quite ignorant of amorous feelings and sentiments! हरिण्योउपि वने ध्या या नोदेन विमोहिताः ।

मात: प्राणान्यच्छिन विध्यूर्षानुपाशुवि ॥ 16 ॥

And what will the wealth and kingdoms of that unskilled illiterate person avail! The deer, that roam in the forest, getting enchanted by the Nāda (sound) Rasa, give up their lives even to the singers. So they are fortunate.

नात्यो वैनि यां विधां मातः सत्यवशतिकाम् ।

तृतीयः कोर्जीय नो वेह ऐशिवान्यः पुण्याक्लि ॥ 17 ॥

But fie to the persons who are illiterate and void of feelings of love! O Mother! Nārada Rsi is well conversant with the science of music with seven Svaras. No other man save Mahā Deva knows this.

भूद्रीय सह संबासो मरणं तल्बणे क्षणे ।

रूपवास्यनवांसत्याचे गुणतीने नर: सदा ॥ 18 ॥

Living with an illiterate person is courting death at every moment. One devoid of qualifications should be always avoided, by all means, though he be wealthy and of a beautiful form.

धिकृदीकी मूर्ख भृपाले वृथा गर्वसम्पन्नित ।

गुणजे भिशुके श्रेष्ठा बचनात्सुखदायिनी ॥ १९ ॥

Fie on the friendship with kings that are illiterate and puffed up with vain arrogance! A well-qualified man, be he even a beggar, is fare better to be cultivated friendship with. Leaving other circumstances out of account, even to change words with such a well qualified man, makes one highly delighted.

स्वत्जो प्राभवित्कामं भूर्भान्जानभेदभाव् ।

दुर्लंभः पुरुषश्राद्धासजो दुर्बतोर्जीय चै ॥ २० ॥

The man is very rare in this world, though he be weak, if he be well versed in the science of music and if he knows Svara, Grāma, Mūrchanā and be skilled in eight sentiments of love.

Note: Svara-Šadaja, Rṣabha, Gāndhāra, Madhyama, Pañcama, Dhaviata and Nīṣada. Grāma□the gradual increase and decrease in Svaras. Mūrchanā—the rising of sounds, an intonation; a duly regulated rise and fall of sounds conducting the air and the harmony through the keys in a pleasing manner; changing the key or passing from one key to another; modulation; melody.

यथा न्याति कैतलासं गड़ा चैव सरस्वती ।

तथा न्याति कैतलासं स्वराजानविशारादः ॥ 21 ॥

The man versed in the knowledge of Svara leads one to the Heaven of Kailāśa as the rivers Ganges and Sarasvatī by their own merits lead one to Kailāśa.

स्वरामाने तु यो बेद से द्वेो मानुषोर्षि सन् ।

सतधेदे न यो बेद से यजु सुराखधि ॥ 22 ॥

There is not the least doubt in this. He is a Deva in his human body who knows the Svara measure; and he who does not know the Svara and its seven grades is a beast though he has a human form—he who finds no delight when he hears the tune regulated by Mūrchanā and the seven Svaras.

मूर्भानातामागतं तु शुवा मोदं न याति यः ।

स पशु: सर्वेधा जेयो हरिणा: पशुचो न हि ॥ 23 ॥

Do not consider the deer as beasts for they get enchanted when they hear the musical notes. The venomous snakes, though they have no ears, get delighted to hear the enchanting Svara Nāda by their eyes.

वरं विषधरः सर्वः शुचा नादं मनोहर्म् ।

अश्रोजोरपि मूदे याति चिक्सकाण्ड्धि मानवान् ॥ 24 ॥

They even are to be praised; but fie on those human beings who have ears but who do not find any delight when they hear the Nāda!

बालोर्धि सुस्वयं गेयं श्रुत्वा मुदितमानसः ।

Book VI Chapter XXVII

जायते किन्तु ते वृद्धा न जानन्ति धिनस्तु तान् ॥ 2 5 ॥

The little children feel intense pleasure to hear the music, but fie, on those elders who are void of this musical sentiments!

विता में किं न जानाति नारदस्य गुणान्बृद्न ।

डितीय: सामगो नासि तिथु लोकेषु ततस्म: ॥ 26 ॥

Does not my father know that Nārada Rsi is ornamented with many qualifications? Who is there in the three worlds like him in singing the Sāma songs!

तस्मादसौ मया नूनं वृत: पूर्वं समागमात् ।

पशुच्छापवशाज्जातो वानारस्यो युणाकर: ॥ 27 ॥

For this very reason, indeed! I have already selected him as my husband; afterwards, due to a curse, the Muni, the ocean of qualifications, got his face changed into that of a monkey. The Kinnaras, skilled in the science of music, have their faces horse-like; but are they not dear to all?

किंसरा न प्रिया: कस्य भर्वित तुरागना: ।

गानविद्धासमायुक्ता: किं मुखेने वरेण है ॥ 28 ॥

चितरं बृहि ये मातर्कृतीयं मुनिसनमः ।

तस्माचसप्राङ्ट लयक्वा देवैत तस्मै च मां मुता ॥ 29 ॥

What business have they to get good faces? They enchant the Devas even by their sweet ravishing songs. O Mother! Kindly tell my father I have already chosen Nārada as my husband. Therefore let him deliver me to his hands, without making any further requests in this matter."

नारद जवाच

इति पुत्रा: वच श्रुत्वा राजी रोजे न्यवेदयत् ।

आग्रहं सुदरी ज्ञाला सुताया नारदे मुने । ३० ॥

विवाहं कुरु राजेन्द्र दयवन्त्या: शुभे दिवे ।

मुनिना स च सर्वजी वृत्तोउसी मनसाउनया ॥ ३१ ॥

Nārada said: On hearing the words of her daughter Damayanti, that unblamable pure queen knowing her attachment deep towards me, spoke to the King thus: "O King! Now celebrate in an auspicious day and on an auspicious moment the auspicious marriage of Damayanti; the daughter

has said that she has already selected Nārada as her bridegroom and it cannot be otherwise."

नारद जवाच

इति संचोदिति राज्या संजय पुरिवधीपति: ।

चकार विधित्यवस्त्वं विधिं वेबाहिकं ततः ॥ 32 ॥

Thus prompted by the queen, the King Sañjaya performed the marriage ceremony of her daughter in accordance with due rites and customs and in an exceedingly becoming manner.

एवं दारगई कृत्वा वानरास्यः परंतप ।

स्वितस्तत्रैव मनसा दहामानेन चान्यम् ॥ 33 ॥

O Rsj! Thus I entered into the married life and remained there though my heart constantly burned with the thought of my monkey-face.

यद्यान्छद्धज्ञानुसं सेवार्यं मम सत्रिषी ।

अभयं दुखसन्तादस्ताद३हं वानानानः ॥ 34 ॥

दयमन्ती तु मां बीक्ष्य प्रकृललवदानीबुजा ।

शाकं वानरवकल्याज चकार कवाचन ॥ 35 ॥

Whenever the princess used to come to me for my service, I used to get tormented with the remembrance of my monkey-face; but her face beamed with gladness whenever she saw me; never she became sorry nor dejected, even for a moment, to see my face monkey-like.' Thus time passed on.

एवं गच्छति काले तु सहसा पर्बती सुविः ।

कुर्वेस्तीधान्येकानि उर्दुं मा समुपगातः ॥ 36 ॥

मयातिमानिनः प्रेणा पूर्तक्रष्ण यवाबिधि ।

आसीन आसने दिव्ये वीक्ष्य मां कु:क्षितो छभूत् ॥ 37 ॥

One day the Muni Parvata suddenly came there, after making his sojourn to many places of pilgrimages. I showed him a great respect and gladly loved him and greeted him duly; he got himself seated in an excellent Asana and became very sorry to see me. I am his uncle and have entered into a married life; my face has become monkey like.

कृतदारं वानरास्यं दीनं द्वितानुं घृभग् ।

दयावाम्पायुचेदं पर्वती मातुलं कृपग् ॥ 38 ॥

Therefore I am very much depressed in spirits and worried with the sad thought and has become lean and thin.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

मया नारद कोपान्चें शप्तोउसि मुनिसत्तम ।

निष्कृति तस्य शापस्य क्रोपेमद्ध निशममा ॥ 39 ॥

Seeing this he was over whelmed with pity. He then said: "O Muni! The curse that I cast on you before out of my anger, I now withdraw.

भव तं चाफछदनो मम पुष्पेन नारत ।

दूद्वा रावसुतां चिते कृमा जाता माभुना ॥ 40 ॥

“Hear. O Maharṣi! Let your face be by my merits, again as good as it was before; I now feel pity for the daughter of the King.”

नारद उवाच

मथाउपि प्रवर्ण चित्रं कृत्वा शुवाउस्य भाषितम् ।

अनुप्रहः कृतः सघतस्तस्य शापस्य तत्बणात् ॥ 41 ॥

भाषीमेव तवायस्तु गमनं सुरस्वयिन ।

शापस्यानुग्रहः कामं कृतोउयं पर्वताभुना ॥ 42 ॥

Hearing thus, my heart also became gentle and instantly with a view to free him of my curse, I said: "Let your journey to the Heavens be re-established. I now make this special favour on you as regards my curse on you before."

नारद उवाच

जातोड़ं चाक्वद्वी वचनात्तस्य यप्यतः ।

राजपूर्णी तुं संतुहा मातरं प्राह सत्वम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

मातले सुयुखी जातो जामाता च महावृत्ति: ।

वचनावलवल्याह युक्तशापो युनेरपृत् ॥ 4 4 ॥

O Dvaipāyana! At his word, before our sight, my face became exceedingly handsome as it was before. The princess Damayanti became very glad and instantly she went to the mother and said: "O Mother! At the words of Parvata, the great Muni, the curse of your son-in-law has been removed and his face his become handsome as before and the lustre of his body has also increased.

तच्छुला वचनं राश्या कथितं ततृ राजनि ।

ययौ उहुं मुनि तन संवयः प्रीतिमांस्तदा ॥ 45 ॥

धनं समार्थितं राजा सन्तुँरेन तदा महत ।

महं च पागिजीयाय पारिहर्द महात्मना ॥ 46 ॥

The queen was very much filled with ecstasy and joy at Damayanti's words and went hurriedly and informed the King. The King Sañjaya gladly

went at once to see the Muni. The great King became very glad and gave lots of wealth, gems and jewels to me and my nephew Parvata as a dowry.

एत्ते सर्वमार्थात यत्नं यत्पुरातनम् ।

मायाया बलमाहान्यं ह्यनुभृतं यथा मया ॥ 47 ॥

O Dvaipāyana! Thus I have described to you my old story how I felt the strong influence of Māyā.

संसारोप्रिम्नमहाभाग मायागुणकृतेंउन्ते ।

तनुष्येसुखी नासिन न भूतो न घधिष्यति ॥ 48 ॥

O Fortunate One! Owing to the illusory nature of the Gunas, like a magic, no embodied being in this world could have been happy before, or he is happy now or he will be happy hereafter.

कामकोधी तथा लोभो मतसरो ममता तथा ।

अहंकारो मदः केन जिताः सर्वे महावलाः ॥ 49 ॥

Lust, anger, greed, jealousy, attachment, egoism, and vanity, each one of these is very powerful; nobody is able to conquer these.

सत्वं जलतमहीशे गुणाल्य शुभे किल ।

कारणं प्राणिज्ञां देहसंभवे सर्वेधा मुने ॥ 50 ॥

O Muni! The three Gunas Sattva, Rajas and Tamas are the entire causes of the coming into this bodily existence of every being.

कर्मिश्रित्यमये यास वनेई विष्णाना सह ।

गछ्छान्यविनोदेन सभीभां गमितः क्षणात् ॥ 51 ॥

O Dvaipāyana! Once I was passing with Bhagavān Viṣṇu, laughing and joking, making merriments through a forest, when suddenly I was transformed into a woman.

राजवनील्यापत्रो मायावलियोगेहितः ।

पुत्र: प्रभुता बढ़ोो गेहे तस्य पुरस्य ह १ ५2 १।

Next I became the wife of a king enchanted by Māyā, I remained in his house and gave birth to many children.

यास उवाच

संशयोदयं महान्याधो श्रुत्वा ते वचनं किल ।

कर्थ नारील्यमापत्रलं सुने ज्ञानवान्यूगम् ॥ 53 ॥

Vyāsa said: "O Devarsi! A great doubt has now

Book VI Chapter XXVIII

arisen in my mind at your word. O Muni! You are very wise; how then did you get womanhood; how again did you regain your manhood?

कथं च पुरुषो जातो बृहि सर्वमेणैषतः ।

कर्थ पुत्रात्पदाय जाताः कस्य राजो गृहेउज्ञान । ५४ ।।

Who was the king at whose house you stayed and how did you give birth to children; describe fully and satisfy my curiosity.

एतदाखहि चरितं मायावहमहद्धुतम् ।

मोहितं च यथा सर्विमिदं स्थावरर्गेनम् ॥ ५५ ॥

Describe to me, now the nature of Māyā, extremely wonderful, by which this entire universe, moving and non-moving, all are enchanted.

न तृषिमधिमछामि शुर्बंस्तव कथामतम् ।

सर्वीम्थान्योत्चं च सर्वसैर्यवनारानम् ॥ ५६ ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवतो महापुराणे घरेरकन्चे

सत्त्विंशोभ्ययः ॥ 20 ॥

O Muni! Though I have heard your nectar-like words, capable to remove all the doubts, embodying the essence of all the Śāstras, yet I am not fully satiated.

Here ends the Twenty-seventh Chapter of the Sixth Book on the marriage of Nārada and his face getting transformed into that of a monkey in Śrimaddevi-bhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

CHADTED VVUIII

Book VI Chapter XXVIII

CHAPTER XXVIII

On Nārada's Getting Feminine Form

नारद खाया

निशायय मुनिश्छि गढ़तो मम सत्कथाम् ।

मायावलं सुदुर्जये मुनिश्चिर्यानिवतनोः ॥ १ ॥

Nārada said: “O Thou whose only wealth consists in asceticism! I am now describing to you all those good stories; hear attentively. O Muni! This Māyā and Her Power are incomprehensible even by those who are the foremost amongst the Yogins.

मायया मोहितं सर्वे जगत्थावरजंगमम् ।

बह्नादिस्त्वायपत्नसया दूर्विभाव्यया ॥ 2 ॥

कदाविसत्यलोकाढ़े धैतड़ीपे मनोहरे ।

गतोउई दूर्णनाकांशी हरेरुद्रतकर्णः ॥ 3 ॥

वादयम्हर्ती वीणां स्वतानविष्यितान् ।

गायनं गायमानस्तु साम सप्तवरात्वितम् ॥ 4 ॥

This whole Universe, moving and non-moving, from Brahmā to the blade of grass, is enchanted by that Unborn and Incomprehensible Māyā; therefore no one can escape from the hands of that Māyā. One day I wanted to see Hari, of wonderful deeds, and went out with lute in my hand from Satyaloka, to the lovely Śvetta Dvipa (the residence of Viṣṇu) singing the beautiful Sāma hymns in tune with the seven Svaras.

दृष्टो मया देववेदशुक्रमाणिगंदाधर: 1

कोस्तुभोद्वासितेरको मेयश्यामश्रतुर्षुज: ।। ५ ॥

I saw there Gādādhara, the Deva of the Devas, with four arms holding disc in one of his hands. He resembled a newly-formed rain-cloud of Śyāma colour. He was illumined with the lustre of the Kaustubha jewel in his breast. He was wearing an yellow apparel.

पीताम्बरपीधानी पुकुटाजुदराजितः ।

लक्ष्म्या सह विलासिन्या क्रीड्यामनी मुदा युत् ।। ६ ॥

बीक्ष्य मां कमला देदी गतातर्पांमन्सितात् ।

सर्वेलक्षणमम्पा सर्वेभूषणभूषिता ॥ 7 ॥

His head was beaurified with a lustrous crown. Thus the Bhagavān Nārāyaṇa was playing in amorous movements with the daughter of the ocean, fully capable to give one delight and enjoyment. Seeing me, the lovely Devi Kamalā, dear to Vāsudeva, full of youth and beauty, decorated with ornaments, endowed with all auspicious signs, superior to all the woman, went away at once (to another room) from the presence of Janārdana.

नारीर्ण प्रवरा कान्ता रूपयैवनगविता ।

सुप्रिया वासुदेवस्य वरचामीकरप्रभा ॥ 8 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

अन्तर्गूहं गतां दृष्ट्वा सिस्भुजां व्यझ्ञान्विताम् ।

मया पृष्ठो देवदेवो वनमाली जगतमृषुः ॥ 9 ॥

The breast of Laksmī Devi was becoming visible even through the cloth thrown over it; therefore she went hurriedly to the inner compartment. Seeing this I asked Janārdana, the Deva of the Devas, the Lord of the worlds, and holding a garland of forest grown flowers—thus: "O Bhagavān! O Slayer of Mura! O Padmanābha! Why has Kamalā Devi, the Mother of all the Lokas, on seeing me coming here, gone out of Your presence.

भगन्देवेवेग पवनाभ सुरारिह्न ।

कंध च मा गता शृह्या माभागच्छतननितकान् ॥ 10 ॥

नाहं विटो न वा धृतैः तापसोड़ं जगदगुरुते ।

नितेनिद्वो सिताकोधो जितमायो जनदिन ॥ 11 ॥

O Lord of the worlds! I am not a rogue nor a cheat; I have conquered my passions and am become an ascetic; I have conquered even Māyā. Therefore O Deva! What is the cause of the departure of the Kamalā Devi from here? Kindly explain this to me.

नारद उवाच

निशम्य वचनं किश्रिद्वर्युक्ते जनादनः ।

उवाच मां सित्त कृत्वा वीणावम्भुर्गो सिगम ॥ 12 ॥

Nārada said: "O Dvaipāyana! Hearing my words, expressive of my pride, Janārdana smiled and spoke to me in words sweet like the sound of a lute:

विष्यूकवाच

नार्देश्विधा नीतिनं स्थात्ययं कदाचन ।

पर्ति विज्ञान्यसाधिये कस्यचिधोषया कवचित् ॥ 13 ॥

“O Nārada! The rule in such cases is this: The wife of any man whatsoever ought not to stay before any other male outsider than her husband. माया सुरुजंया विद्यत् योगिरिभिजितमन्तः ।

सांख्यविर्द्वर्नैगहरेत्पापसेक्षा विलेत्रियः ॥ 14 ॥

O Nārada! It is very hard to conquer Māyā; even those, who by Prānāyāma have conquered their Prāna Vāyu, their organs of senses and their

food, even those Sānkhya Yogins and the Devas are not able to conquer Māyā.

देवेश्व मुनिगार्तूल यच्चयोक्तं चवो5पुंना ।

वित्तमादोस्म यतित्व नैवं वाच्यं कदाचन ॥ 15 ॥

The words that you have just now uttered that you have conquered Māyā are not fit to come out of your mouth; for by your knowledge of music, it seems that you are enchanted with the sounds of the music.

नाहे शिलो न वा बह्या जेतुं तां प्रभवोर्याजम् ।

मुभ्य: सनकाधायं कस्वं कें-ग्रे क्षमा जरे ॥ 16 ॥

Brahmā, I, Śiva, and the other Munis, none of us has been able as yet to conquer that Unborn Māyā; how, then, can it be possible that you or any other man can conquer that Māyā!

देवदेहं नृद्रें वा तिर्येन्द्रधापि वा ।

विषय्यातः शगीरं च स क्रकं तां जदेवदाम ॥ 17 ॥

वियुतन्तों कर्म भायां जेतुं शकः: पुभाम्यन्ते ।

वेदविद्योगविद्यानि सर्वजैं विज्ञानेत्रिज्ञ: ॥ 18 ॥

Any embodied being' be he a Deva, a human being, or a bird, no one is able to conquer that Māyā Unborn. Whoever is endowed with the three Gunas, be he a knower of the Vedas, or a Yogin, or conqueror of his passions, or all knowing, is not able to conquer Māyā.

कालोपीय तस्य कर्ण हि रूपधीन: स्वरूपकृत ।

तड़गे वर्तते देही विद्धान्यूर्षोंदा मध्यमः ॥ १९ ॥

The Great Time (Kāla) though formless, is one form of Māyā and fashions this universe. All the Jīvas are subservient to this Kāla, be he a good literary person, or of a mediocre nature, or an illiterate brute.

कालः क्रोतीत धर्में कदाचिद्धिकलं पुनः ।

स्वभावात्कर्मीत वारप्र दुर्जं सत्य चेहितम् ॥ 20 ॥

This Kāla sometimes makes even a religious man that kiwos Dharma confounded and eluded; so you know the nature of Māyā is very incomprehensible and Her ways mysterious.

Note: This Kāla is of the fourth dimension, time and space.

Book VI Chapter XXVIII

नारद उवाच

इत्युक्चा विराते विद्यगृहं विस्मयमानस: ।

तमवुवं जगत्रार्थ वासुदेवं सनातनम् ॥ 21 ॥

रमापते कंधेक्णा माया सा कौनूशी पुनः ।

किंयद्रुला कवसंस्थानां कस्याधाता बदल्य मे ॥ 22 ॥

O Dvaippāyana! Thus saying, Viṣṇu stopped. I was greatly astonished and asked that Eternal Vāsudeva, the Deva of the Devas, the Lord of the World, “O Lord of Rama! What is the form of Māyā? How is She? What is measure of Her strength? Where She resides? Whose substratum is She? Kindly tell these to me.

प्रक्रामोःप्रिय तो मारां द्रशायुग मृतीधर ।

जातुपिछ्णति तां सम्यव्यसादं कुक मापते ॥ २३ ॥

O Preserver of the Universe! I am greatly desirous to see Māyā; Shew Her to me quickly. O Lord of Rama! I am very eager to know Māyā. Be graciously pleased to describe to me the glory of Māyā."

विष्यु उवाच

त्रिगुणा साउष्टितधारा सर्वत्रा सर्वसम्मता ।

अवेबान्दिकरणा च सर्वे व्याय्य स्थिता जगत् ॥ 24 ॥

Viṣṇu said: Māyā resides everywhere throughout this whole Universe; Her nature consists of the three Guṇas; She is the substratum of all; She is omniscient, and acknowledged by all; invisible, and of diverse forms.

दिद्धा यदि ते चितने नरतारोहणं कृक ।

गरड़े मनमोतेष्ठा गच्छावोरन्य सामप्रम्न ॥ 2 5 ॥

दर्शचिधामि ते मावां दुर्जयामिततामधि: ।

दूद्वा तां ब्रहापुरं तव मा विश्वादे मन: कृधा: ॥ 2 6 ॥

O Nārada! If you want to see Māyā, then come quickly and mount with me on Garuda; we both will go elsewhere and I will shew you that Māyā, invincible by those who have not conquered themselves.

इत्युक्त्वा देवेदोवो मां सम्मार विनतास्तुमः ।

सूतमात्रस्तु गृहस्तदाग्याद्विरसिधी ।। 27 ।।

आगतं गरई वीक्ष्य आरुरोह जनदानं: ।

समातोष्य च मां पुहुं गमनाय कृतादर: ॥ 28 ॥

O Son of Brahmā! Do'n't be depressed when you see Māyā. Thus saying, Janārdana Hari remembered Garuda and instantly he came to Hari. Janārdana mounted on him and gladly made me also get up on his back and took me with Him.

चलिता विनतापुरो वैकुणवाद्राव्युवेगान् ।

प्रेरितो यत्र कृष्णेन गनृकामेन काननम् ॥ 29 ॥

महावानिन विद्यानि सारांसि सरितस्तथा ।

पुरुषामाकराईश खेटखर्षोगेत्रजान् ॥ 30 ॥

मुनीनामाश्रमात्यमायावार्थीड सुमनोहराः ।

पल्बलानि विशालानि हृद्यान्यूबन्धपूर्वितान् ॥ 31 ॥

भुगाणों च वराहाणां वृन्दान्यथलोक्य च ।

गतावारां कान्यकुळसरीयं गरडासनी ॥ 32 ॥

तत्र न्ययं सरो दिव्यं दृष्टि युब्जमपिडतम् ।

ईसकारणडवाकीर्णं चक्रवाकोपशीघितम् ॥ 33 ॥

नानावर्षीं. प्रफुल्लेशं पूज्ञैभरात्रितम् ।

गृचि मिछाजलं भूक्रयूधनाद्विराजितम् ॥ 34 ॥

मामाह भगवान् वीक्ष्य तडागं परमाबुदूनम् ।

स्पर्धकं चोद्योः क्रीं मिछे वार्ति विशेषतः ॥ 35 ॥

श्रीभगवानुवाच

पश्य नातर गर्भीरं सर: सारसनाहितम् ।

सर्वकं पशूकैश्छत्रं स्वच्छनीरामूर्तिम्न ॥ 36 ॥

In a moment Garuda, went, at his command, with the speed of wind to the forest where the Bhagavān desired to go. Mounting on Garuda we passed and saw on our way beautiful forests, nice lakes, rivers, towns, villages, huts of cultivators, towns close to the mountain, huts for cow-keepers in cowsheds, the beautiful hermitages of the Munis, lovely Jhils, tanks and lakes beautified with big lotuses, flocks of ewes, packs of wild boars, etc., till, at last, we came to a place close to Kanauj. I saw there a beautiful divine tank; nice lotuses blossomed there, spreading their sweet fragrance all around; the bees were making lovely humming noise and ravishing away the minds of men; various flowers, lilies, etc., were beautifying the place; Geese, Kāranḍavas, and Cakravākas and other acquatic fowls were playing with their cackling

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

noise, the water was very sweet like milk; the tank was defying, as it were the ocean. Seeing such a wonderful tank, the Bhagavān told me: "O Nārada! See, how beautiful is this deep tank with its clear waters, and adorned all over with lotuses! The sweet voiced flamingoes are roaming on the lake making lovely sounds!

अत्र स्नाला गमिष्याव: कान्यकुळं पुतेताम् ।

इत्युक्नवा गकडादिषु मापुतनार्थ व्यतारथत् ॥ 37 ॥

We will bathe in this tank and then go to the city Kanauj. Thus saying, He made me descend quickly from Garuda and He himself also got down. शिवस्य भगवांस्त जग्राह मम तर्जीम् ।

त्वुवनसोरं वृद्यस्तीरं मामवनतघुः ॥ 38 ॥

Then the Bhagavān smilingly caught hold of my fore finger and repeatedly praising the glory of the tank took me to its bank.

विश्रवा तद्यागे तु नित्याक्ष्यये मनोहरे ।

मातुवाच मुने स्नाने कुल त्वं विमले जले ॥ 39 ॥

We rested a while on the cool umbrageous beautiful bank when Śrī Bhagavān said: "O Muni! Better bathe you first in this tanks; next I will bathe in this very holy pool of water.

पशुवाहं क्रौथ्यामि तडागेत्रिसन्त्युपावने ।

साधुपामिव चेतोसि जलाति निर्मालिति च ॥ 40 ॥

O Nārada! Look! Look! How clear crystallike is the water of this pool like the heart of a saint; see how it smells also fragrantly in contact with the lotuses on it."

सुरपंपीति परारैसैनु पड्ज्ञानां विशेषतः ।

इत्युङोड़ं भगवता मुक्लवा शीणं भूगाबिनम् ॥ 41 ॥

When the Bhagavān spoke thus to me; I kept my lute and deer skin aside and gladly went to the edge of the tank.

स्नानाय कृतधीस्तीर गतः प्रेमस्मिततः ।

पाटी प्रशल्य हतौं च शिखां बद्धया कुग्याहम् ॥ 42 ॥

Washing then my hands and feet I tied my hair lock and, taking Kuśa grass, I performed my Acamana and, purifying myself, began to bathe myself in that tank.

कृताचय शुश्चितोये स्नातवान्सि तजले ।

यदा तस्मिजले ग्ये स्नातोर्दं पश्यतो हरेः ॥ 43 ॥

विद्यार यौरकं रूपं प्राप्त क्षीतवमनुतमम् ।

हरिपूरीत्वा वीणां मे तथा कृष्णात्रिनं शुभम् ॥ 44 ॥

While I was bathing, Hari was looking at me; by the time I took a dip, I saw that I quitted my male form and got a beautiful female form. Hari took away, then, my deer skin and mounting on Garuda went away in a moment to His own residence.

आक्रह्य गरुकं तुर्णं जगाम स्वगृहं क्षणात् ।

ततोर्कं क्षीत्मवपत्रश्राकृष्णपृष्णपितः ॥ 4 5 3 ॥

ततक्षणान्मनसो जाता पूर्वदैष्टस्य विस्मृतिः ।

विस्मृतोर्जी जगजाधो महती विस्मृता पुनः ॥ 4 6 ॥

Getting the female form and decorated with excellent ornaments, my memory of my previous male form vanished at once; I forgot all about my famous lute and forgot also Jagannātha, the Deva of the Devas.

संग्राय मोहितीकर्ता तडागाशिरांत बहिः ।

अपर्थ नलिनीजुई सरसतिभुतचलादम् ॥ 47 ॥

किमेत्तदिति मनसाकरं विसमयं गुहुः ।

एवं चित्तयानस्य नारीकरणस्य मे ॥ 48 ॥

I then came out of the tank in that enchanting woman form, saw the pool of water filled with clear limpid water and adorned with lotuses. Seeing that, I began to think: "What is this?" and I became very much astonished.

सहसा द्ववर्ष प्राप्तस्तर तालधजवो नृपः ।

गजाश्रवदू-दैक्षं संयुतो रथसंस्थितः ॥ ४१ ॥

युवा भूषणसंवीता देह्वानिव मन्थः ।

बीक्ष्य मां भूपतितस्त रिद्विष्यूषणभूषितम् ॥ 50 ॥

While I was thus meditating in my woman form, a king, named Tāladhvaja, came there, all on a sudden, on a chariot, accompanied by numerous elephants and horses. The King looked like a second Cupid; he was decorated with various ornaments on his various limbs; he was just entering into his youth and he looked very enchanting.

Book VI Chapter XXIX

राकाचन्द्रमुखीं योषां विस्मयं परमं गतः ।

पण्ड्छ कार्मिक कल्याणि कस्य पुत्री सुरस्य वा ५ ५ १ ॥

The King saw me at once and looking at me decked with divine ornaments and my moon-like face, was greatly astonished and asked me: "O Kalyānī! Who are you?

मानुष्य च वा काने नायर्वस्योरास्य च ।

एकानिकी कवं बाला रूपयीवनभूषिता ॥ ५२ ॥

Are you the daughter of a man or a Nāya (serpent) or of a Gandharva or a Deva? I see you are now in your youth; why are you alone here?

विवाहिता5थ कन्या वा सत्यं वद सुलोचने ।

किं पश्यसि सुकेशाने तडागेसिमनुभ्यधेम ॥ ५३ ॥

O Lovely-eyed! Has any fortune person married you? Or are you still unmarried? Speak all these

truly to me. O Fair-haired One! What are you looking at in this tank?

विकार्थिक्त पिकालापे बूहि मन्यभोगेहिनी ।

भुंश्व भोगान्मारलाभ्रि मया सह कृणोदरि ।

वार्जिहानात्मसा नूतं कृत्वा मां परितुसामम् ॥ 54 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे भशुककन्धेडणाविंशो3धायक:128।

O One enchanting, as it were, like the Cupid! What is your desire? Say, O Slanting-eyed! My mind is ravished to hear your cuckoo-like voice. O One of thin waist! Choose me as your husband and enjoy various excellent things as you like."

Here ends the Twenty-eighth Chapter of the Sixth Book on Nārada's getting the feminine form in the Māhapurānam Śrīmaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Veda Vyāsa.

[1] 2014年1月1日

Book VI Chapter XXIX

CHAPTER XXIX

On Nārada Again a Man

नारद उवाच

इत्युक्तोड़ं तदा तेन राजा ताताधउनेच च ।

विश्यग्य मनसाठल्यर्थ तमुवाच विशांपते ॥ १ ॥

राजत्राहं विज्ञानापि पूत्री कल्येति निश्रयम् ।

पित्तौ क्व च मे केन स्थापिता च सरोदरे ॥ 2 ॥

किं करोमि क्व नच्छामि कवं मे सुकृतं भवेत् ।

निरूपास्त्रसि राजेन्द्र चिंतनामि चिकीर्षितम् ॥ 3 ॥

Nārada said: “O Dvaipāyana! When the King Tāladhvaja asked me thus, I thought over earnestly and said thus: “I do not know whose daughter I am; nor do I know quite certainly where are my father and mother; one man placed me here on this tank and has gone away, whither I do not know.

दैवमेव वरं राजनास्त्रव प्रौर्भं मम ।

धर्मज्ञोंसि महीपाल यथेच्छसि तथा कुरु ॥ 4 ॥

तद्धीनाउमवहं भूप न मे कोयसित पालकः ।

न पिता न च माता च न स्थानं च बान्यवः ॥ 5 ॥

O King! I am now an helpless orphan; what shall I do now? Where to go? What to do by which I can have my welfare? I am all the while thinking on these. O King! The Destiny is powerful; I have not the least control over it; you know Dharma and you are a King. Do now as you like.

इत्युक्तेपरी मया राजा बधूव मदनातुरः ।

मां निरीक्ष्य विगालाहर्षी सेवकांनिव्युबारा हूं ॥ 6 ॥

नरायानमानयखे चतुबाँहुं मनोहर्म ।

आरोहणाधेमस्यन्तु कौशेगंबारवेहितम् ॥ 7 ॥

O King! Do nourish me; I have no father, no mother, nor any acquaintances and friends; there is no place for me also to stand on; therefore I am now your dependant."

मूढास्तरणावंयुक्ते मुक्ताजलविभूषितम् ।

चतुरयं विशालं च मुवर्णपित्ति शृभम् ॥ 8 ॥

तस्य तद्वानं श्रुत्वा भूत्या: सत्वरागयिनः ।

आनिन्यु: शिविकां दिव्यां मद्यं वस्त्ववेहितम् ॥ 9 ॥

आरूक्षोर्धृ तदा तस्यां तस्य प्रियचिक्तीयां ।

When I spoke thus, the King looked at my face and became love-stricken for me; he then tole his attendants to bring an excellent rectangular and spacious palanquin to be carried on four men's shoulders, gilt and adorned with jewels and pearls, where soft sheets were spread inside and covered all over with silken cloths. Instantly the servants went away and brought for me a beautiful palanquin.

मृदितोउसी गृहे नील्या मां तदा पृथिवीपति: ॥ 10 ॥

विद्वाहिविधाना राजा शुभे लगने शुभे दिते ।

उपयेमे च मां तत्र हुतभूवसाधिते ततः ॥ 11 ॥

I got on it to serve the best wishes of the King. The King also gladly took me home. In an auspicious day and in an auspicious moment he married me in accordance with due rites and ceremonies on the presence of the Holy Fire.

तस्याहं वल्लभा जाता प्राणेधोउपि गरीयती ।

सोभायम्युद्रतीर्वेवं नाम त्रकृतं मम ॥ 12 ॥

I became dearer to him than even his own life and the King, with great fondness, kept my name as Saubhāgya Sundarī.

त्यभागो मया सार्थं सुख्याप महीपति: ।

नानाभोगिवलासीः कामगलाश्ठोदिततंथा ॥ 13 ॥

The King then began to sport with me amorously according to the rules of the Kāma Śāstra in various ways and with great enjoyments and pleasures.

राजकायाणि संलब्ज क्रीक्षास्तो द्विविणम् ।

नासौ विवेद गछ्ठंत कारं कामकलारतः ॥ 14 ॥

उद्यानेषु च रधेषु वाणीषु च गृहेषु च ।

ह्यर्थेषु वर्गैलेषु दीर्घिकासु वासुर च ॥ 15 ॥

He then left all his kingly duties and state affairs and he began to remain day and night with me deeply immersed in amorous sports; so much his mind was merged in me in these plays that he could not notice the long time that passed away in the interval.

वारणीयदार्तोर्सी विह्र-कानने शुधे ।

विसुक्य सर्वकायिणि मदधीने बषूव ह ॥ १६ ॥

He used to drink the Vārunī wine and, forsaking all the state affairs, began to enjoy me in nice gardens, beautiful lakes, lovely palaces, beautiful houses, excellent mountains and enviable forests and became completely subservient to me.

व्यासाई तेने संसक्ता क्रीडास्वर्गीकृता ।

स्मृतावाम्बूर्वेदेह न पुंभावं मुनिनवम च ॥ १ ७ ॥

O Dvaipāyana! Being incessantly engaged with the King in amorous sports and remaining obedient to him, my previous body, male ideas, or the birth of Muni nothing whatsoever came in my memory.

मदीवार्य पतिर्वाँषाउं पत्नीषु चित्रा सती ।

पडुताङ्गी विलाससा सफलं जीवितं मम ॥ १ ८ ॥

इति चिंतयती तत्मन्येमवन्द्धा दिवानिगम् ।

क्रीडासत्ता सुखे लुक्यां त चैथता वरणवर्तिनि ॥ १ ९ ॥

I remained always attached to him, being obedient to him with a view to be happy and I constantly thought over “that this King is very much attached to me, I am his dearest wife to all others; always he thinks of me, I am his chief consort, capable to give him enjoyment.”

विस्मृतं बढ्धारिजानं बढ़ानानं च ग्राशन्तम् ।

धर्मग्रापरिज्ञानं तदासत्कामना: स्थिता ॥ 20 ॥

My mind became entirely his and I completely forgot the eternal Brahmanjāāna and the knowledge of the Dharma Śāstras.

एवं विहृतस्तत्र वर्षिण इतद्रौये तु ।

गतानि क्षणवक्तामकीडास्तस्मय मे मुने ॥ 21 ॥

O Muni! Thus engaged in various amorous sports, twelve years passed away as if a moment and I could not perceive that.

जाता भर्षेति चाहे मुद्रं प्राप नृपस्तदा ।

कारयामास विधितदर्भसंराकारमं च ॥ 22 ॥

Then I became pregnant; and the King became very glad and performed all the ceremonies pertaining to my impregnation and holding of the child in my womb.

अपूष्ठहोईर जाग प्रीणयमां पुनः पुनः ।

नाङुवं लज्जमानाउं नृपं प्रीतमना भृगण ॥ 23 ॥

In order to satisfy me, the King used to ask me always what things I liked; I used to be very much abashed; seeing this, the King used to be still more glad.

संपूर्णे द्वगमे मासि पुत्रो जातस्तती मम ।

गुधेउढ़ हान्द्रभलनन्तरावलान्विते ॥ 24 ॥

बभूव नृपतौहि पुत्रजनमहोत्सव: ।

राजा परमसन्तुषो बभूव सुतजनमतः ॥ 25 ॥

Ten months thus passed away and in an auspicious Lagna and when the asterism was favourably strong, I gave birth to a son; the King became very glad and great festivities were held on the birth ceremony of the child.

Book VI Chapter XXIX

सुतकाने सुतं वीक्ष्य राजा सुदमवाप्त है ।

अई भूमिपतेश्रासं प्रिया भार्यों परंतप ॥ 26 ॥

ततो वर्षद्धाते वै पुनर्भार्म मया धृत: ।

द्वितीयस्तु सुतो जातः सर्वलक्षणसंयुतः ॥ 27 ॥

O Dvaipāyana! When the period of the birth-impurity was over, the King saw the face of the child and was greatly delighted; I then became the dearest wife of the King. Two years after again I became impregnated; the second auspicious son was born.

सुध्येति सुतस्याय नाम चके नृपस्तदा ।

वीरवर्मिति ज्येषुस्य बाहागीः प्रेरितस्तवयम् ॥ 28 ॥

एवं द्वादण पुत्राध प्रमृत्ता भृतमंसता: ।

मोहितोई तदा तेषां प्रीत्या पालनलालने ॥ 29 ॥

The King gave the name Sudhanvā to the second son and on the authority of the Brāhmins, kept the name of the eldest son as Viravarmā. Thus I gave birth to twelve sons, in due course of time, to the King's great liking; and I was engaged in rearing up those children and thus I remained enchanted.

पुनरष्ट सुतः काले काले जाताः सुरूपिणः ।

गाईस्वयं मे ततः पूर्ण समवर्ष सुखसाधानम् ॥ 30 ॥

तेषां दारिक्रिया: कालं कृता राजा यथोतिथिता ।

सुर्पाधिश्र तथा पुरैः परिवारो महानभूत् ॥ 31 ॥

Again in due course, I gave birth to eight sons; thus my household was filled with happiness. The King performed the marriage ceremonies of all those children duly and befittingly; and our family became very large with sons and their wives.

ततः पौत्रादिस्म्प्रतालेश पिं कीतास्वातिवतः ।

आसबानासोपोरात मोहबुद्धिकरा भूर्णम ॥ 32 ॥

कदाचित्सुखमैष्यवं कदाचिदचुदुःखमद्धुतम् ।

पुत्रेषु रोगजित्ने देहसंतनायककामू ॥ 33 ॥

Then I had some grandsons and they increased my attachment and the consequent delusion with their all sorts of playful sports. Some times I felt happy and prosperous and sometimes I felt pain and sorrow when my sons fell ill.

परस्यें कदाचित् विरोधोःभृन्तुसहणः ।

पुत्राणां वा वधूनां च तेन सन्तापसम्भवः ॥ 34 ॥

Then my body and mind became very much troubled with sorrows. Again the quarrels amongst my sons and my daughters-in-law, brought terrible pain and remorse in my mind.

सुखदुःखात्मके घोरे मिथ्याचारकरे भृणग ।

संकल्पजिति क्रुंट मनोरड़ं मुनिसत्तम ॥ 35 ॥

विस्मूतं पूर्वविज्ञानं शास्त्रज्ञानं तथा गतम् ।

योषाभावे विलोनोरई गृहकार्येषु सर्वथा ॥ 36 ॥

O Best of Munis! Thus I was greatly immersed in the terrible ocean of these imaginary thoughts, sometimes happy and sometime painful, and I forgot my previous knowledge and the knowledge of the Śāstras. I was merged in the thought of myself being a woman and lost myself entirely in doing the household affairs.

अहंकारसु संजातो भूर्ण मोहविद्यकैः ।

एते के बलिनः पुत्रा: सूपायः सुकुलसभ्याः ॥ 37 ॥

एते पुत्रा: सुसन्धज्ञा: मौडौति मम वैश्मसु ।

ध्यार्यई खलु नारीणां संसोरोस्मिजों घृणम् ॥ 38 ॥

I began to think that “I have so many daughters-in-law; so many powerful sons of mine are playing together in my house; Oh! I am fortunate and full of merits, amongst women” and thus my egoistic pride increased.

नारदोर्भं भगवता वशितो मायया किल ।

न कदाचिन्यायोर्वे चिन्तितं मनसा किल ॥ 39 ॥

राजप्नौ शुभावारा बहुपुरा परितता ।

धन्यादर्भ किल संसारे कृषीर्वे मोहितस्तद्वर्भ ॥ 40 ॥

अथ काक्षिवृपः कामं तूरदेशाधिपो महान् ।

अरातिभावामपत्र: पतिता सह मान्द Ⅱ 41 ॥

Not for a moment even occurred the thought that I had been Nārada; the Bhagavān had deceived me by His Māyā. O Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana! I was deluded by Māyā and passed away my time in the thought “that I am the king’s wife, chaste and of good conduct, following good Ācāra; I have so many sons and grandsons; I am blessed in this Sarhsāra and that I am so happy and prosperous.”

कृत्वा सैन्यसमाधोगे रथेशु धागधौतुम् ।

आजगाम कान्यकुको पुरे युद्धमित्यत् ॥ 42 ॥

Šrimadievibhāgavatam

वेहितं नगर तेन राजा सैन्ययुतेन च ।

मम पुत्रश्र धौत्रश्र निर्मात नगरातच ॥ 4 3 ॥

One powerful king of a distant country turned out an inveterate enemy of my husband and came to the city of Kanauj to fight with my husband, accompanied by chariots, and elephants and the fourfold army.

संधामासुभुतस्तत्र कृततरीतेन पुत्रकैः ।

हता रणे सुता सर्वे वैरिणा कालवोगतः ॥ 44 ॥

राजा भगस्तु संग्रामावर्ताः स्वगृहं पुनः ।

शुतं मया मृततः पुत्रा: संग्रामे भृगदाकणे ॥ 45 ॥

That enemy besieged the city with his army; my sons and grandsons went out and fought valiantly with him but owing to the great Destiny, the enemies killed all my sons. The King retreated and returned to his palace.

स हत्वा मे सुतान्यौजान्तो राजा बलान्वितः ।

कंद्रमाना हईं तत्र नाता समरमण्डले ॥ 46 ॥

Next I heard that powerful King killed all my sons and grandsons and had gone back to his country with his army. I then hurriedly went to the battle-field, crying loudly.

दूर्धा तान्यतितान्यूग्राम्यौत्रांशु दुर्बपीडिता ।

विललापाहामायुष्मछोकसागरसंवेदो ॥ 47 ॥

हा पुका: क्व सात मेउदा हा हतामि दुरात्मा ।

दैवेनातिवलिछेन दुविरणातिपाधिन ॥ 48 ॥

O Long-lived One! Seeing my sons and grandsons lying on the ground, in that horrible and distressed state, I became merged in the ocean of sorrows and lamented and wept loudly and wildly "O my Sons! Where have you gone leaving me thus? Alas! The pernicious Fate is very dominant, and very panisgiving and indomitable.

एतस्मिन्ते तत्र भगवान्यभ्युद्नः ।

कृत्वा रूपं द्विजस्यागद्वुद्धः परमशोभनः ॥ 49 ॥

सुसासा वेविलकामं मतस्मीये समागतः ।

मामुचाचातिदीनों संकन्द्यानां रणाधिरे ॥ 50 ॥

It has killed me to-day." By this time, the Bhagavān Madhusūdana came to me there in the garb of a beautiful aged Brāhmīn. His dress was

sacred and lovely; it seemed he was versed in the Vedas. Seeing me weeping distressedly in the battle-field he said:

ब्राहण उवाच

किं विवीदासि तन्यांग घमोउंप्र फकटीकृतः ।

भोहेन कोकिलालाये परित्यज्ञहात्मके ॥ 51 ॥

“O Devi! O cuckoo-voiced One! It seems you are the mistress of a prosperous house and you have got husband and sons! O thin-bodied One! Why are you thus lamenting and feeling yourself distressed!

का लं कक्याः सुता: केज्यी चिंतयामालिं परम् ।

उत्तिष रोन्द नं कल्पा क्वस्पा भव सुलोचने ॥ 52 ॥

All this is simply illusion caused by Moha; think; who are you? whose sons are these? Now think of your best hereafter; Do'nt weep, get up and be comfortable, O Good-eyed one?

स्नानं च तिलदानं च युजाणं कुक कामिनि ।

पतलिकानातां च मयादारक्षणाय वै ॥ ५३ ॥

कर्तव्य सर्वेश तीर्थे स्नाते तु न गृहे कवचित् ।

मृतानां किल कब्नूतां धर्मशाखविनिणयः ॥ ५४ ॥

O Devi! To shew respect to your sons, etc., gone to the other worlds, offer them water and Til. The friends of the deceased ought to take their bath in a place of pilgrimage; never they should bathe in their houses. Know this as ordained by Dharma."

नात्व उवाच

इत्युकला तेन विशेषय कुद्रेन प्रतिवोधिता ।

उचिताउई नृपेणाय युक्ता बन्युभिपरावृतां ॥ 55 ॥

अग्रतो तिज़करणेण भगवान्भूतभावनः ।

चलिताऊँ तत्सनुर्ण तीर्थ परमपावनम् ॥ 56 ॥

Nārada said: O Dvaipāyana! When the old Brāhmin thus addressed me, I and the King and other friends got up. The Bhagavān Madhusūdana causing this creation, in the form of a Brāhmana, led the way and I followed him quickly to that sacred place of pilgrimage.

हरिमाँ कृपया तत्र पुंतीर्थ सरसि प्रपुः ।

नीलाउङ्ग भगवाविष्णाईजरूपी जनदांदः ॥ 57 ॥

Book VI Chapter XXX

स्नानं कुरु तडागोस्मिन्यावने नगगामिनि ।

त्वज शोके क्रियाकालः पुजागणं च निर्यकंम् ॥ 58 ॥

The Viṣṇu Bhagavān, the Lord Janārdana Hari, in the form of a Brāhmin, kindly took me to the tank named Puritīrtha (male tīrtha) and said: “O One going like an elephant! Better take your bath in this tank; forego your sorrows that are of no use; now the time has arrived to offer water to you sons.

कोटिगस्ते भूता: पुत्रा जन्यजनसमुद्धावः ।

पितर: पतयक्षेध भात्तो जामयस्तथा ॥ 59 ॥

Better think that you had millions of sons born to you in your previous births and for that your millions of sons and daughters lost their lives; you had millions of fathers, husbands, and brothers and you lost them again; O Devi! Now tell me for whom you will now grieve?

केवां दुःखं त्वया कार्यं म्रप्रेस्मिनानसोद्धवे ।

विवधे स्वप्नदूरुते तापदे देहिनामि ॥ 6 0 ॥

All these, then, are merely phenomena; this world is full of delusion, false like a mirage and dream-like; the embodied souls, simply get pains and sorrows and nothing else."

नारद उवाच

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा तीर्थे पुरुषसंज्ञेक ।

प्रविष्टा सन्तुकामारई प्रेरिता तत्र विध्गुना ॥ 6 1 ॥

मज्जनादेव तीर्थेषु पुमाझातः क्षणाद्वि ।

हरीर्णीणां कें कृत्वा विश्वतसीरे स्वदेश्वान् ॥ 6 2 ॥

Nārada said: On hearing his words, I went to

bathe in that Punitritha, as ordered by him. Taking a dip, I found that, in an instant, I became a man; the Bhagavān Hari, in his own proper form, was standing on the edge with a lute in his hand."

उन्मज्ज च मया तीरे शूधः कमललोचनः ।

प्रत्यधिज्ञा तदा जाता मम चिते द्विजोतम ॥ 63 ॥

O Brāhmīn! When getting out of the water, I came to the bank and saw the lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa, pure consciousness then flashed in my heart.

संचिंत्तितं मया तत्र नारदोःहमिहानतः ।

हरिणा सह क्षीभांवं प्राणो मायाविमोहितः ॥ 64 ॥

Then I thought that “I am Nārada; I have came to this place and being deluded by the Maÿā of Hari, I got the female form.”

इति चिन्तापश्रहं चवा जानसदा हरि: ।

मामाह नारतगघ्छ किं कारीष जले सिचत: ॥ 65 ॥

विस्मतेहं तदा स्यत्वा स्त्रीभावं द्वारभुं भुगम् ।

पुन: पुरुषायश्व समयः: केन हेतुना ॥ 66 ॥

ड्रौते श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे घटस्कचे

एकौनविशो3ध्याय: 11 29 11

When I was thinking thus, Hari exclaimed “O Nārada! Get up; what are you doing, standing in the water?” I was astonished; and, recollecting my feminine nature, very severe indeed, began to think why I was again transformed into a male form.

Here ends the Twenty-ninth Chapter of the Sixth Book on the Nārada's getting again his male form in the Mahāpurāṇaṃ Śrimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣī Veda Vyāsa.

Book VI Chapter XXX

CHAPTER XXX

On the Glory of Mahā Māyā

नारद उवाच

मां बृह्वा नारदं विश्व विश्वमितोउसी महीधपति: ।

क्व गता मम भार्यों सा कुतोउवं मुनिसस्तम: ॥ १ ॥

वित्तलाप नृप्सतत्र हा प्रियेति मुकुँदुः ।

क्व गता मां परित्यज्य विलयन्ते विलयोगियम् ॥ २ ॥

Nārada said: “O Best of Munis! The King was greatly astonished to see me dip in the tank in a female figure and get up from the tank in a male

figure and thought. Where is my dearest wife? And how is this Nārada Muni suddenly come here! The King, not seeing his wife, lamented very much and cried frequently O my dear Wife! Where have you gone, leaving me here thus."

विता तां विपुलओणीच बृथा मे जीवितं गृहम् ।

राज्य कमलपत्राशिक्र किं करोमि शुचिभित्मे ॥ ३ ॥

न प्राणा मे बहिर्यान्ति विरहेण तवाधुना ।

गतो वै प्रीतिध्यमैस्तु तवामृते प्राणधारणात् ॥ ४ ॥

Without you, O One of spacious hips! My life, palace and kingdom, all, are quite useless. O Lotus-eyed one! What shall I do? O Smilling One! Why is not my life getting out of my body, suffering thus from thy separation? Without you, my sentiment of love has left me for ever.

विल्यामि विशालासिंह वेहि प्रवृत्तरं प्रियम् ।

क्व गता सा मायि प्रीतिवार्षभूद्यमयंगे ॥ 5 ॥

O Large-eyed One! Now I am lamenting for you, O Dear! Better give me your sweet reply; the love that you expressed at our first union, where has it gone now?

निमाना कि जले सुचृष्ठीक्षिता मत्स्यकच्छधेः ।

गृहीता वकृणेनाशु मम दोषाण्ययोगतः ॥ 6 ॥

धन्या सुचासर्वर्शि या त्वं पुत्रैः समागता ।

अकृतिमस्तु पुरेशुं नेत्रतेरेप्रतभाषिशि ॥ 7 ॥

O One with good eyebrows! Are you sunk in the water and have you given up your life? Or are you devoured by fishes or crocodiles? Or are you carried away by Varuna, the Deva of the waters, to my great misfortune? O One of beautiful limbs!

न युकमधुना व्यमां विहाय तिथिदंय गता ।

विलपन्ते पति दीनं पुत्रसनेहन यन्धिता ॥ 8 ॥

उभयं मे गतं काने पुत्रस्तं प्राणवल्लभे ।

तथापि मरणं नातित झुःवितत्य मृष्णं प्रिये ॥ 9 ॥

You are blessed, as you have gone away with your sons; O sweet-speaking One! Your affection for them was not artificial. Is it right for you to go up to the Heavens, attached by affection for yours sons, leaving me your distressed husband alone, thus weeping for your separation?

कि कारीम वक्ता मच्छामि रोगो नारित महीत्ते ।

रामाविरहर्जे दुः:खुखानाति सूचनन्दनः ॥ 10 ॥

O Dear! I have lost both, you and my sons; yet death is not carrying me away; O! How hard is my lot! What to do? Where to go? Rāma is not now in this world. He knew what was the pain caused by the separation from one's dearest wife.

विधिना निहुर्णान्र विवरीतं कृतम्भ्युवि ।

द्यपत्योर्मरणं भिन्नं सर्वथा समाचितयो: ॥ १ १ १ ॥

Oh! The cruel Fate has ordained very unwisely with great inconsistency the periods of parting from one another at different periods; when their minds and all other things are exactly the same in all circumstances of pleasure and pain.

उपकारस्तु नारीणां भूतिभर्वित्र: किल ।

यदुर्फे धर्मशक्षेषु ज्जलनं पतिनां सह ॥ १ २ ॥

एवं विलयमानं तं राजानं भगवाहर्य: ।

निवारयामास तदा चवनैयुक्तिक्योजितः ॥ 13 ॥

The practise of Satī (burning with one's deceased husband), as ordained by the Munis, is certainly for the good of the chaste women; but it would have been good no doubt, were there such practises allowed for the men to burn themselves with their deceased wives." Bhagavān Hari then spoke to the lamenting King in reasonable words and consoled him thus:

श्रीभगवानुवाच

क्रिक विवादिति राजेन्द्र स्व यत ते प्रियाङ्गन ।

न श्रुत किं स्व्य शास्त्र न कृतोतयी झुयाययः ॥ १ ४ ॥

"O King! Why are you thus troubling yourself with pain and sorrow? Where has gone your dearest wife? Have you not heard anything of Śāstras? or Have you not taken any shelter of any wise man!

का सा कतस्तंच स्व योगोय विवेयाः कौहुगस्तल ।

प्रवाहरूप संसारे नृणां नौतततापिव ॥ १ ५ ॥

Who was your wife? Who are you? Of what nature was your union and disunion and where did it take place? The union of wives and sons in this Saṃsāra is momentary like the meetings of persons on boats, while crossing a river.

गृहे गच्छ नृपश्रेष्ठ बुधा ते कहितेन किम् ।

संशोधाग्र विशोधाग्रुं देवधानि: सदा गुणान् ॥ 16 ॥

O King! Now go home, there is no use in your weeping thus in vain; the union and disunion of men are always under the control of Fate, the Daiva; therefore the wise should not lament for them.

अनया सह ते राजनसंयोगसिद्ध संवृतः ।

भुक्ता तथा विशालाक्षी सुंदरी तनुष्यमा ॥ 17 ॥

Book VI Chapter XXX

O King! Your union with the woman took place here; and now you have lost that beautiful, thin-bodied, large-eyed woman here also.

न दृष्टी पित्तावस्यास्त्वया प्राप्ता सरोवरे ।

काककालीप्रेसोन यद्भूतं ततथा गतम् ॥ 18 ॥

Her father and mother you have not seen; you have got her like what is heard in the story of the crow and the Tāla fruit; as you got her wonderfully, so you have lost her wonderfully.

मा शोकें कुरं राजेन्द्र कातोस्तिवृतिकमः ।

कालयोगी समासादा घूब्ध्व भोगानूहे यथा ॥ 19 ॥

यथाउउगता तगा सा तु तथैव वरविण्नी ।

यथा पूर्व तथा तत्र कष्ट कार्य कुरु प्रभो ॥ 20 ॥

O King! Do not grieve; Time cannot be ruled over; go home and enjoy yourself subservient to Time. That beautiful woman has gone away in the manner she came to you; you ought to do your stately affairs in the way as you used to do before as the ruler of all.

रूदितेन त्वाठीय नागमिष्यति कामिनी ।

दृथा शोचसि पृथ्वीश योगयुक्तो भवाछ्ञान ॥ 21 ॥

भोगः कालवशादेति तथैव प्रतिवाति च ।

नात्र शोकस्तु कत्होये निष्क्ले भवकल्मिन ॥ 22 ॥

O King! Consider that if you weep day and night, that woman will never return; why then are you giving vent to your sorrows in vain? Go now and have recourse to the path of the Yoga and thus while away your time.

नैकत्र सुखसंयोगो दुःखयोगस्तु नैकतः ।

यदिकायन्वकानां भ्रमणं सुखदुःखयोः ॥ २३ ॥

The enjoyable things come in course of time and they go away again in due course; therefore in this world of no gain whatsoever, the wise should never lament. Continuous pleasure or continuous pain does not always take place; pleasure and pain are never steady; they rotate always like a rotary instrument.

मन: कृत्वा सिंधां भृपु केतार्चं घ्यासुखम् ।

अथवा नस्य दायादे वर्न सेवस्य समीप्रणि ॥ 24 ॥

Therefore, O King! Make your mind calm and

a rotary instrument. Therefore, O King! Make your mind calm and quiet and rule happily your kingdom; or make over the charge of the kingdom to your sons and retire to the forest.

दुर्लभो मानुषो देहः प्राणिता क्षणधर्मग्रः ।

तस्मिन्नाते तु कतिथ्यं सर्वेश्वात्मसाधानम् ॥ 25 ॥

This human body is seldom obtained; it is frail; therefore getting that body it is advisable to practise the realisation of the Supreme.

विद्धोपस्थसरो राजन्यगुधोपितु वर्तते ।

ज्ञानं मानुपदेहे वै नान्यासु च कुयोगीम् ॥ 26 ॥

O King! This organ of generation and this tongue reside also with the beasts, the peculiarity of human body is that knowledge can be realised in it; not in any other inferior births.

तस्मादुपच्छ गृह्टवक्त्वाशोके कान्तामुकुद्वम् ।

मायेयं भगवत्यास्तु यया सम्मोहितं जगत् ॥ 27 ॥

Therefore leave your home, leave your sorrows for your wife; all this is the Māyā of Bhagavān; by Her the world is deluded."

नात्य उवाच

इत्युक्तो हरिणा राजा प्रणयम कमलापतिम् ।

कृत्वा सनाविषिधं सम्यजगाम तिजमतिर्यम् ॥ 28 ॥

Nārada said: Bhagavān Hari speaking thus, the King bowed down to Him, the Deva of the Devas and finishing the bathing duties returned to his home.

दत्ता राज्यं स्वपौत्राय प्राय पिवैदंमढुतम् ।

वर्नं जगाम भूपालस्तच्ज्ञानयाव च ॥ 29 ॥

He then became possessed of dispassion and discrimination and making over the charge of his kingdom to his grandsons retired to the forest and realised the Supreme Knowledge.

गते राजन्यई वीक्ष्य भगवनमाधोक्षजम् ।

तमकुबं जगताथं हस्तां मां पुन: पुन: ॥ 30 ॥

When the King went away, the Bhagavān began to laugh and laugh, seeing me again and again. I then told him “O Deva! You have deceived me.

विश्रितोई त्वया देह ज्ञातं मायावलं महत् ।

स्माराम चरितं सर्वे क्रीदेहे चक्तृत मया ॥ 31 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

I now come to know how great is the power of Māyā. O Janārḍana! Now I remember all that I did in my feminine form.

यूहि मे देवेदेयेन प्रविष्ठोई सरोये ।

वित्तं पूर्वविज्ञानं स्नानादेव कर्थे है ॥ 32 ॥

Tell me, O Hari! O Deva of the Devas! How I lost my previous consciousness, when I got down into the tank and bathed in it.

शोभिर्हें समासाद्ध मोहितोर्जे जगद्युगे ।

पर्ति ग्रण्य नृपश्रेष्ठ पुरुलोभी वासवं यथा ॥ 33 ॥

O Lord of the world! Why was I enchanted, when I got the female form and when I got the King as my husband like Śaci's getting Indra.

मनस्तदेव तिच्चतं देहः स च पुरातनः ।

लिंगं तदेव देहेश स्युतेनीश: कर्थ है ॥ 34 ॥

The same mind I had; the old Jīvātmā was there and the previous subtle body was there; how, then, I lost their memories?

विस्मयोर्ध महान्योज ज्ञानगांश प्रति प्रभो ।

कयवधा रमाकान कारणं परमं च यत् ॥ 35 ॥

O Lord! Give out the cause of it and clear my doubts; a great doubts has arisen in my mind.

नारीदेहं भवा प्राथ्य मुक्ता भोगा हुचनेकरः ।

सुपारपांत कृति निव्य विधिहीनं च कोवाजम् ॥ 36 ॥

Many enjoyments I had in my female form, drinking liquor and other prohibited things I tasted; O Slayer of Madhu! What is the cause of all these?

मया तदेव न ज्ञातं नारदोड़भिति सुकटम् ।

जानाभ्यद्य यथा सर्वे विकिति न तथा त्वा ॥ 37 ॥

I could not know then that I was Nārada, as I now recognise clearly what I was in and what I did in my female form. Say the Why of all these things.

विष्णुर्वाच

पर्य नारद् यायावी विलासतोर्प महामते ।

देहेषु सर्वेन्तूनां द्राणेवा हुधलेकशः ॥ 38 ॥

Visnu said: "Know, O Intelligent Nārada! That all this is merely the Pastime of Māyā. There are many states going on in the bodies of all the living beings.

जाग्रत्वपनमुचुलिहा तुरीया देहिनां द्वणा ।

तथा देहान्तरे प्रापते सन्देहः कौणुगः पुनः ॥ ३ १ ॥

The embodied beings have got their waking, dream, deep sleep and Turiya (beyond all the three above mentioned) states; then why you doubt that when there is another body, there would be also the change in the states?

सुगतो नरो न जानाति न शृणोति वदत्यपि ।

पुनः प्रवृद्धो जानाति सर्व ज्ञातमशेषतः ॥ 40 ॥

When a man sleeps, he knows not anything, he does not hear anything; but when he gets awake, he again comes to know everything completely.

निद्रा चाल्यते चिंतं भवन्ति स्वतमस्पषाः ।

नानाविधा मनोभेदो मनोभावा हुनेकशः ॥ 41 ॥

The Citta gets itself moved by sleep; then mind gets different states by dreams and there arises a variety of feelings.

गजों में हनुभावाति न शकतोरमि सलायने ।

कि कंकीम न ये सीनींच यज भख्यास सकर: ॥ 42 ॥

A mad elephant is coming to kill me, and I am not able to fly away; what to do? Where to go? There is no place where I can quickly go: thus, in dreams, there arise different mental states.

वितामहं स्वाने पश्यति स्वगृहात्मान् ।

संयोगसन्तेन वातां च भोजनं सह मयने ॥ 4३ ॥

Sometimes we see in dreams that our departed grandfathers are come in our houses. I am seeing them, talking with them and I am dining with them.

प्रकुबः खलु जानाति स्वपे दुर्भे सुखासुखम् ।

भ्युला सर्वे जनेभ्यस्तु विस्ताराव्रदत्यपि ॥ 44 ॥

Whatever pain and pleasure are felt in dreams, when they awake, they know of what happened in their dreams and can also describe in details, recollecting what had been happened.

स्वपे कोप्रि न जानाति भ्रमोर्धिति निश्रयः ।

यथा तर्वेय विभयो मायाया दुर्गम: किल ॥ 4 5 ॥

O Nārada! Know the power of Māyā incomprehensible as the things seen in dreams cannot be certainly known that all those are false.

नाहं नारद ज्ञानापि पारम्परमुद्यंम ।

गुणानां किल मायाया नैव शाम्यानं घचज: ॥ 46 ॥

Book VI Chapter XXX

O Muni! Neither I, nor Šambhu, nor Brahmā can measure the power wielded by Māyā and Her three Guṇas, very hard to fathom.

கொலு சாள்து சுழல்பேழமானவே மறடே: ஒந: 1

మామాగురాగినాన న కంజువి పలేద్ద 147 11

How, then, can any ordinary mortal know them! Therefore, O Nārada! None is able to fathom the Māyā.

गुणारयकृतं सर्वे जगत्थावाज्जूमम् ।।

विना गुणैन संसारो वर्तते किंचिद्वाद्वः ॥ 48 ॥

उहं सत्वप्रधानोउसि रजतसमसप्तितः ।

न कदाचित्रिभर्षिणीं भावामि घुवनेश्र: ॥ 49 ॥

This world, moving and non-moving, is fashioned out of the triple Gunas of the Māyā; nothing whatsoever can exist without them. The predominant Guna in Me is Sattva; but Rajas and Tamas exist in me; being the Lord of this world, I cannot override the three Gunas.

तथा बहा पिता तेउत्र रजोमुख्यः प्रकीर्तितः ।

तम:सत्त्वसाम्युक्ति न ताध्यामुक्ति: किलि ॥ 50 ॥

So your father, Brahmā, is predominant in Rajo Guna; but Sattva and Tamas never leave Him.

शिवस्तथा तमोमुख्यो रजःसत्त्वसाम्यात् ।

गुणत्रयिवहीनस्तु नैव कोउपि मया शुतः ॥ 51 ॥

Our Mahā Deva is predominant in Tamo Guna, but Sattva and Raja are always with him. Therefore, no being can exist as separate from the three Gunas; this point I have settled in Śruti.

तस्मानोहो न कतिथ्व: संसारोत्रिस्मनुनीश ।

माया विवितिपेलुरासेपरे परमदुष्टे ॥ ५२ ॥

Therefore, O Lord of the Munis! Quit this endless Moha for the world, caused by Māyā, and very hard to get over and worship Bhagavatī, Who is of the nature of Brahman.

दूषा माया त्वयास्त्रीयं भुक्ता भोगा हुंचनेकशः।

किं पृष्छासि महाभाग तत्यश्रहीरात्मद्धुतम् ॥ 53 ॥

श्रित श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे भश्छकन्ये

प्रिंशोर्यावाः : 11 30 ॥

O Intelligent One! Now you have seen the power of Māyā; and you have enjoyed many things produced by Māyā and you have realised the extremely wonderful nature of Her. Then why do you ask me further on this point?

Here ends the Thirtieth Chapter of the Sixth Book on

the glory of Mahā Māyā in the Mahāpurānam

Śrimaddevībhāgavatam of 18,000 verses

by Maharṣī Veda Vyāsa.

Book VI Chapter XXX

CHAPTER XXXI

On Mahā Māyā

व्यास उवाच

निर्णाय महाराज ब्वीपि विश्वादक्षगम् ।

माहात्म्यं खतु मायाया नारतानुं मया श्रुन्तु ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! I am now recounting the detailed glory of Māyā, heard by me from Nārada.

मया पुनर्मुंपि: पृष्टो नारद: सर्ववितिमः ।

श्रुत्वा कर्णं मुनेतस्य नारोदेहसमुद्धवम् ॥ 2 ॥

Again I asked sage Nārada who is the most omniscient of all, after hearing the story of that sage in the female body.

ब्रह् नारद पश्रात्रिक कथितं हरिणा तदा ।

क्व तात्ध जगत्राधो भवता सह माधयः ॥ 3 ॥

O Nārada! Tell me! What did Hari say at that time? Where did Mādhava the lord of the universe go along with you?

नारद उवाच

इत्युक्चा भगवांस्तस्मितडागेगतिनोहरे ।

आहक्रा गरड़ नन्तु वैकुण्ठे च मनो दधे ॥ 4 ॥

Nārada said: After saying thus, in that beautiful lake, the lord made up his mind to go to Vaikuntha, mounting on Garuda.

मामुवाच समाकानो यथेह गच्छ नारद ।

एहि वा मन लोकं त्वं यथा कहित तथा कुभ ॥ 5 ॥

Mahāvisnu, the consort of Lakṣmi, told me. O Nārada! go as you wish. Or come to my world. Do as you wish.

ब्रह्मलोककृतश्रह्मापृष्छच मधुसूदनम् । भगवानपि देवेशरासत्कणारूक्षासनः ॥ 6 ॥ वैकृष्टमयमातृष्णं भगाभिर्य यथासुखम् । ततोड़ं चितुसदन्तं गतो याते जनादंने ॥ 7 ॥

After taking leave of Madhusūdana, I went to Brahmaloka. Viṣṇu the lord of Devas, seated on Garuda, went to Vaikuntha quickly, in a happy manner after directing me. When Janārdana was gone, I went to the region of my father (i.e., Brahmā).

चिंतयन्मकलं दुःखं सुखं च परमाद्धुतम् ।

गत्वा प्रणाय्य चितरं स्थितो यावत्पूर: चितृः ॥ 8 ॥

तावत्पृधो मुने चित्रा बीक्ष्य चिन्तानुर्ते मुम् ।

Thin king about all the wonderful miseries and the joys undergone, I went to my father and after prostrating to him, stood before him.

ब्रहोयाच

क्व गतो5स महाभाग कस्मार्थितानुः सुत ॥ 9 ॥

स्वयं नैवादा पश्यामि मानते मुवितस्तम ।

Then seeing me tormented by thoughts, my father Brahmā asked: O great one! Where had you gone? Son! Why are you troubled by thoughts?

केनापि वंचितोसि तवं चूदं या किंचिद्धुद्धुम् ॥ 10 ॥

विषणं गतवित्रानं पशयामि त्वां कंबे सुत ।

O great sages! I do not see your mind happy today. You have been deceived by some body. Or you have seen some wonder.

नारद उवाच

इति पृषस्तदा पित्रा बूस्यां समुपवेश्य च ॥ ११ ॥

तमबुंचं स्ववृत्तानं मायावलसमुद्वम् ।

वंचितोर्जमितः कामं विल्याना प्रभविष्यान् ॥ १२ ॥

O Son! How can I see you sad and devoid of discrimination?

Nārada said: Thus I was asked by my father. I sat on the seat.

I told him my story arising out of the power of Māyā. O father! I have been cheated by the powerful Mahāvisnu.

हीभावझूमितः कार्य वर्षाणि सुबह्न्यति ।

अनुभूत महदुःखं पुत्रशोकसुभुंद्वम् ॥ 13 ॥

I was made to attain the state of a woman for many many years. Extreme misery arising out of death of son was experienced by me.

प्रवोधितोड़ः तैनैव मुटुवावयामुनेन च ।

पुनः सरोबेर सन्ताव जातोड़ं गरादः पुणम् ॥ 14 ॥

I was instructed by him by nectarine sweet words about my real nature. Again after taking bath in that tank. I became a man (the old) Nārada.

किमेतत्कारणं बहान् यन्मोहमगां तदा ।

विस्युतं पूर्वविज्ञानं तन्यवसतसा कृत: ॥ 15 ॥

O Brahman! What is the reason that I attained delusion at that time? Earlier knowledge was forgotten. I became identified with that female form, so quickly.

एतन्यायावलं बह्रात जानेउई दुत्पत्यम् ।

ज्ञानात्विकर्त जातं मृतं मोहस्य विस्मुटम् ॥ 16 ॥

O Brahman! This is the strength of Māyā, which is difficult to cross. It is beyond my understanding. It destroyed all my knowledge. It was a clear cause of delusion.

अनुभूतं मया सम्यज्ञातं सर्वे शुभाशुभम् ।

कर्थं त्वं तिजातंस्तत तनुपायं वद्यव मे ॥ 17 ॥

I experienced it (power of Māyā) well. All good and bad were known. Of father! How did you attain victory over it? Tell me that clue.

नात्व उवाच

विज्ञतोउरी माया धाता प्रीतिपुर्वितः पनम् ।

मामुवाच स्मितं कृत्वा विगत मे वासवीर्तु ॥ 18 ॥

O Muni! When my Father learnt the cause of my cares, he smiled and spoke to me in sweet words:

ब्रहोवाच

दुर्जंथ्या सुरैः सर्वैर्जनिधिश्र महात्यमिथः ।

तापसैज्ञान्यैक्श्य योगिधि: पवनागरैः ॥ १९ ॥

न हांता सर्वथा जातुं गुक्तो मायां महावलम् ।

विष्णुजांतु न रणक्रहा तथा रंगभुभमापतिः ॥ 20 ॥

"O Child! The Devas, the high-souled Munis, the wise ascetics and the Yogis subsisting on air only are not able to conquer this Māyā. O Nārada!

Book VI Chapter XXX

The power of Māyā is so very great that I, Viṣṇu and Sāmbhu, the Lord of Umā, none are able to know Her power.

दूरंगा सा महामाया सृधिश्वत्यंतकारिणी ।

कालकर्मव्यापावाहीद्विपित्तकारणैवृत्ता १ 2 1 १

That Mahāmāyā is creating, preserving and dissolving this world by Time, Karma, and Nature and other efficient causes.

गोकं मा कुक भेधार्थित्त प्रायामाहावले ।

न चैव विसयः कावीं वयं सर्वे वियोहिताः ॥ 2 2 ॥

O Child! Know Her to be inconceivable and unapproachable. O Intelligent One! Do not be sorry nor should you be surprised about Māyā's great strength, for we all are deluded by Her."

नारद उवाच

चित्युक्तस्तदा व्यास तमापृष्छ्छा नात्मयः ।

आगतोउम्यय पश्य-वै तीर्थीनि च वारीण चे ॥ २३ ॥

O Dvaipāyana! Thus advised by my Father, my wonder disappeared. I then asked permission of my Father Padma Yoni (Lotus-born) and went out on tour round the sacred places of pilgrimages and on my way, seeing by and by the chief Tirthas, I have now come here.

तस्मात्रविमीय सत्यज्य मोहं कोरवानाजम् ।

काल्बयं सुखासीय: स्थानेउस्मिकुरु सत्तम ॥ 24 ॥

अवश्यमेव पोक्तयं कृतं मुणगुपम् ।

निश्छय हृदये कृत्वा विवारस्य यथासुखम् ॥ 25 ॥

Therefore, O Muni! Dost thou relinquish your sorrows for the extinction of the Kuru's family and remain here and pass your time in great joy and happiness. One must bear the fruits of one's Karma, good or bad; knowing this fully roam at your will wherever you like.

व्यास उवाच

इल्युक्ला नारदो राजनातो मां प्रतिवोध्य च ।

'अर्ह तत्चित्यन्याक्यं चतुर्भ मुनिना तत् ॥ 26 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Maharṣi Nārada thus kindling knowledge in me, went away; I also thought over his words.

स्थितः सरस्वतीरो करेले सारखते वरे ।

कालातिकाहन्योरत्कृतं भागवतं मया ॥ 27 ॥

On the banks of the river Sarasvati, I composed this Devi Bhāgavat to pass away my time during the excellent period of Sārasvata Kalpa.

पुराणमुक्तां भृप सर्वसंशयनाशनम् ।

नानाख्यानसमयमुक्ते वेद्यामाणयसंश्रिन् ॥ 28 ॥

This Purānam is excellent; it is composed on the authority of the Vedas; all doubts are removed by it; many nice events are narrated here.

सन्दोड़ोत्र न कतिथ्वः सर्वांश युपसत्तम् ।

यधेत्रजातिकः क्रौश्यांचाती उदार्यों के ॥ 29 ॥

कृत्वा नर्तेने कामं चेख्छाया यशवर्तीनम् ।

तथा नर्तेने माया जान्स्थावरणंजमम् ॥ 30 ॥

Therefore, O King! Not the least doubt should be entertained. As a magician makes the wooden dolls dance in his hands at his will, so this world-enchanting Māyā is making this world, moving and non-moving, dance from Brahmā down to the blades of grass and all human beings.

ब्रह्मातिवर्षपर्वतं सदेवासुरमानुपम् ।

पञ्चैद्रियसमापुर्न मन्त्रिभान्तुरवर्तीन् ॥ 31 ॥

O King! Know Māyā's triple Guñas to be the cause of the this mind consisting of five organs of senses, that follows the Citta (mind, Buddhi and Ahankāra).

गुणान्तु कारणं राजनर्वेंां सर्वेथा प्रयः ।

कार्यकारणसंयुक्ते भवातीति विविधास्यः ॥ 32 ॥

भिन्रभित्रव्यभावास्ते गुणा मायासमूद्वतः ।

शान्तो घोरस्तथा मृद्धक्यन्तु विविधा चत: ॥ 33 ॥

तत्समेत: पुगाजित्यं ताईहितः कर्णं भवेत् ।

न भवत्येव संसार रहितस्तुषि: पटः ॥ 34 ॥

Actions arise from the causes thereof; there is no doubt in this; what doubt, then, there can arisen that all these creatures of different temperaments will come out of the different Gunas of Mayā. Peaceful, terrible and stupid become the persons in contact with the Maÿic Gunas.

तथा गुर्गीक्षिभिंडीनी न देहौति विविश्रयः ।

देवदेहो मनुष्यो वा तिरशो वा नाराधि १३ ५ ॥

Šrimuddevibhāgavatam

गुर्णीविंहितो न स्यान्पृद्धितीना घटो यथा ।

बहा विषूमत्या करूस्यधामी गुणागधाः। ३ ३ ६ ॥

कदाचिद्धीतीयुक्तास्ते त्याउप्रीतयुतः पुनः ।

तथा विषादयुक्तासे भवति न गुणयोगतः ॥ ३ ७ ॥

How, then, can they exist, bereft of them? As the cloth cannot exist without threads, so the embodied beings cannot exist in the world without the triple Gunas of Māyā. There is no doubt in this. As a pot cannot be made without clay, so these bodies, Devas, human or blinds cannot be created without the Gunas. Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, too, are possessed of those three Gunas and therefore they become sometimes happy and satisfied, sometimes unhappy and dissatisfied and sometimes they become sad and remorseful as they are then under the influence of one Guna or the other.

इदा कदावित्तस्यस्वरा शान्त: सामाधिमान्।

प्रीतियुक्ति भवेत्सर्वभेतु ज्ञानसंयुतः ॥ 38 ॥

पुनः सच्चिवीरान्सु रजोगुणसाम्यातु: ।

तदा भवेद्योरोकरूप: सर्वेत्रग्रीतिसंपुतः ॥ 39 ॥

यदा तमोगुणाविष्टे बाहुल्येन भवेढ़िधिः: ।

तदा विषादसम्पत्रो गृहो भवित नान्यथा ॥ 40 ॥

Brahmā happens at times to be full of wisdom and knowledge, his temper peaceful, sweet and pleasant; and his soul rapt in Samādhi, when he becomes possessed of Sattva Guṇa; again when he is void of Sattva and filled with Rajo Guṇa, His temper becomes unpleasant and his appearance gets dark and awful everywhere; and when he becomes grossly Tāmasic, He becomes sorrowful and bereft entirely of intelligence.

माधवोप्रि सदा सत्त्वसंश्रितः सर्वथा भवेत् ।

यदा शगत: प्रीतिवृष्ठने भवेक्रानसर्विन्त: ॥ 41 ॥

स एव रज्ञाप्रियव्याप्रीसिनंसुन्तो भवेत् ।

घोरछ सर्वभूतेषु गुणाधीनो रामपति ॥ 42 ॥

Vísnu, when resting in Sattva, becomes peaceful, sweet-tempered, and full of knowledge; when Rajo Gunā preponderates in Him, He becomes void of sweetness and becomes awful to all the beings.

रुद्रापि सचस्युक्तः प्रीलिमाङ्गनिलामाभ्यते ।

राजीनिधितिः सोअप्रि योरः प्रीतिविचिति: ॥ 43 ॥

तमोगुणयुतः सोअपि मुहो विषायदुभयते ।

एते यदि गुणाधीना बह्याविष्युहारदय: ॥ 44 ॥

सुर्विंगीज्ञद्वासतद्वासमावेशपवा अप्रि ।

मन्वादध३ ये प्रोक्ताश्रतुदैशगुणे युगे ॥ 45 ॥

Rudra becomes, too, peaceful and pleasant under the Sattva Guna, awful and void of sweetness under the Rajo Guna, and becomes sad and stupid under the Tamo Guna. O King! When Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Maheśvara and the solar and lunar Kings, the fourteen lords of Manvantaras, Manu and other are under the control of the magic Gunas, what to speak of other ordinary mortals, men and the other Jivas. अन्येघं चैव का वातां संसारोस्मिन्योपेत्तम ।

मायाधीनं जानतस्यं सदेवासुरामानुम् ॥ 46 ॥

तस्मात्राज कतन्य: संदेहोत्र कदाचन ।

देही मायापराधीनश्रेते तदनुगानुगः ॥ 47 ॥

The whole world is under the control of Māyā; the Devas, men and all other beings. None should doubt on this point. All the embodied beings labour under the directions of Māyā; never can they work independently.

सा च माया परे तत्वे संविदूरोप्रि सर्वदा ।

तद्यपीन प्रेरिता च तेन जीवेषु सर्वदा ॥ 48 ॥

This Māyā is again always residing in the Highest Essence, the Samvit or the Universal Pure Consciousness. Thus Māyā is dependent on the Highest Goddess, Who is of the nature of Samvit, and, stimulated by Her, resides in the hearts of all the Jivas.

ततो मायाविशिष्ठां तां संविद्धे परमेश्वीम् ।

मायेश्वी भगवती सचिव्यान्द्रक्षणिणी ॥ 49 ॥

Therefore one ought to meditate, worship and bow down before the Bhagavati, the Creatrix of Māyā and Who is of the nature of Sarivīt, Pure Existence, Intelligence and Bliss.

ध्यायेतशास्त्रप्रपेच्च प्रणमेच्च जपेद्वि ।

तेन सा सदया भृत्वा मोचयत्येव देहैन्नि ॥ 50 ॥

Thus She becomes gracious and merciful and

Book VI Chapter XXX

liberates the Jivas, giving them Her realisation and drawing together Her own Māyā away from them.

स्वायायां संहत्येव स्वानुभूतिप्रदानतः ।

भुवनं खतु माया सत्यदीक्षरी तस्य नायिका ॥ 51 ॥

भुवनेगी ततः प्रोक्ता देवो शैलोक्यसून्दरी ।

This whole cosmos is nothing but Māyā and the Consciousness (Sarivīt) of the nature of Brahman is the Lord of Māyā. For this reason that Beautiful One in the triple worlds, the Devi Bhagavatī is known by the name Bhuvaneśvarī, the Great Lady of the worlds.

तदूये यदि सर्के स्याचित्चतं भूयिपते सदा ॥ 5 52 ॥

मायया किं भवेत्तनत सदसद्धुतया नृप ।

O King! If the Jivas can fix their hearts on that Samvit, then Māyā, born of the real and unreal, is quite unable to do any harm to them.

तस्मान्यायानिरासार्थ नान्यदे देवतान्तरण ॥ 53 ॥

सभर्थे तु वित्त देवों सचिवदानंरक्षिपशिण ।

तभोराशि नागरियुं रुक्त नैव तमो भवेत् ॥ 54 ॥

किन्तु भानुपभाचन्द्रविद्युविद्विपभाद्यः ।

No other Deva than the Bhuvaneśvari, of the nature of pure existence, intelligence and bliss is able to remove this Māyā. O King! Darkness cannot destroy darkness; the Sun, Moon, Lightning or Fire can destroy it.

तस्मामावेश्वीमवां स्वप्रकाशं तु संविदम् ॥ 55 ॥

आताधयेद्वितिज्ञा मायागुणनिवृत्तये ।

इति सम्वहूसया उज्यातं चूवासुर्वधादिकम् ॥ 56 ॥

Therefore it is highly incumbent on us to worship the Lady of Māyā, the Sarīvatī, the Mother with a cheerful heart to remove the Māyā and Her Guṇas. O King! Now I have narrated to you all the events concerning the killing of Vṛtrāsura that you asked.

यत्पूरं राजशान्तैन किमन्यच्छौतुमिच्छिस ।

पूर्वार्थोंदंय पुराणस्य कविषतस्तव सूत्रत ॥ 57 ॥

यत्र देयास्तु मडिमा विस्तरेणोपादितः ।

एतद्रहस्यं श्रीमान्तै देवं यस्य कस्यचित् ॥ 58 ॥

What more do you want to hear now. O One devoted to vows! I have now described the first half of that Purāṇa, which describes in detail the glory of Śrī Devi Bhagavatī. This Purāṇa, the secret of this Mother of the whole Universe, is not to be disclosed indiscriminately to anybody.

देशं भक्ताय शान्ताय देवीभक्तिताय च ।

शिषाया ज्येषुरुाया युभभक्त्युता च ॥ 59 ॥

Those that are peaceful, self-restrained, devoted, and possessed of Bhakti to the Devi, the disciples that are devoted to their Gurus and the eldest son, those are the fit recipients thereof.

इदमविलकबाना सारभृतं पुराणं

निश्वलनिमानतुल्यं सरमणानुनिविद्धम् ।

पदति परभावाहा: शुणोतीह भक्ल्या स

भवति धरावान्दे ज्ञानवामान्वोउरा १ ६० ॥

इति श्रीदैर्शिभागवते महापुराणो घरकन्ये

भगवतीमाहान्ये एकत्रिंशोउध्या ।। ३१ ।।।

वेदाठवसुभूमखेदी: (1884) यद्योर्मकृतै: शुभै: ।

देवीभागवतत्याय चरकक्यः समापनावन् ॥

।। सामानोयं बष्टः कक्नः ॥

Whoever reads, or hears with greatest devotion this Mahāpurānam, equivalent to the Vedas, fraught with sound proofs and the essence of all talks, becomes, in this world, possessed of great wealth, becomes wise and passes his time in the greatest happiness. There is no doubt in this.

Here ends the Thirty-first Chapter on the Sixth Book

on the glory of Māyā in the Mahāpurānam

Śrimaddevibhāgāvatam of 18,000 verses by

Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

***
